## Bug found: Vacancy.requirements/preferredSkills silently capped at 191 chars

While testing the Create Vacancy flow, hit `PrismaClientKnownRequestError P2000: "The provided value for the column is too long for the column's type. Column: requirements"`. Root cause: `Vacancy.requirements` and `Vacancy.preferredSkills` were declared as plain `String?` in schema.prisma instead of `String? @db.Text` like `description` right above them -- Prisma maps a plain String to MySQL `VARCHAR(191)`, so any Requirements/Preferred Skills text over 191 characters crashed the create/update instead of saving, even though both are rendered as multi-line textareas clearly meant for long-form text. Not caused by anything built this session -- pre-existing schema bug, just never hit because nobody had typed a long enough paragraph into either field before.

Fix: added `@db.Text` to both fields, matching `description`. Requires the user to run `npx prisma migrate dev --name widen_vacancy_text_fields` (safe, additive column-type widen, no data loss) and restart the backend.

## Schema — Interview split into InterviewSlot + Interview (multi-candidate support)

Working through "Corrections that should be made to the frontend.docx" top to bottom (per the user's locked sequencing decision), the HR Interviews section ("The HR's Interview pages are not created so create these") describes a calendar-based module where HR schedules one time/panel/round slot and then separately adds one OR SEVERAL candidates to it ("add candidate or candidates to an interview", a multi-select "select all... add to interview" flow). It also explicitly calls out that HR's own "round" label ("just after the lunch break, it's the next round of it") is a free-text convenience label HR types, NOT the same thing as `VacancyStage.order`'s strict no-skipping sequence.

Both of those broke the old assumption baked into `Interview`: one row = one time/panel/round *and* exactly one candidate (`Interview.applicationId` was a direct required FK).

**Decision (autonomous, per the user's standing "change the database schema" call on this conflict):** split into two models --

- `InterviewSlot`: the calendar entry -- `vacancyStageId`, `scheduledAt`, `roundLabel` (optional, HR's free-text label), panelists (moved here from `Interview`, since a panel is assigned to the *session*, not per candidate).
- `Interview`: now just `slotId` + `applicationId` -- one row per candidate's participation in a slot. `Feedback` still attaches to `Interview` unchanged, so panelist scoring/feedback semantics didn't need to change at all, only how `vacancyStage`/`scheduledAt`/`panelists` are reached (via `.slot` now instead of directly).

**Blast radius, deliberately minimized:** every existing read endpoint (feedback, follow-ups, HM Pending Decisions readiness gate, HM Candidate Comparison scoring) now queries through `.slot` but immediately flattens the result back onto the old field names (`vacancyStage`, `scheduledAt`, `panelists` on the returned object) before any business logic runs or before the JSON is sent to the frontend -- so the already-locked readiness-gate and scoring logic in `hiringManager.controller.ts`, and every frontend screen that consumes these endpoints (Interviewer dashboard, Feedback screen, HM Comparison, Follow-Ups), needed **zero changes**. `POST /applications/:id/interviews` (the existing single-candidate `scheduleInterview` CandidatesPage already calls) also keeps its exact old request/response shape -- internally it now creates an `InterviewSlot` + one `Interview` row in a transaction instead of one `Interview` row directly.

**New, additive:** `POST /interviews` (create a slot, no candidates yet) and `POST /interviews/:id/candidates` (add one or more candidates to an existing slot, per-candidate success/failure reporting like the CV upload endpoint) -- these are the primitives the new HR Interviews calendar module (task #30) will build on.

**Files touched:** `schema.prisma` (new `InterviewSlot` model, `InterviewPanelist.interviewId` renamed to `slotId`, `Interview` stripped down to `slotId`+`applicationId`), `interview.controller.ts` (full rewrite), `feedback.controller.ts`, `hiringManager.controller.ts`, `followUp.controller.ts` (query/flatten changes only, business logic untouched), `interview.routes.ts` (2 new routes).

**Verification ceiling:** this sandbox cannot run `npx prisma generate`, so `tsc --noEmit` was checked against the *old* generated client and (as expected) only flagged the exact lines that reference the new `InterviewSlot`/`slotId`/`.slot` shape -- no unrelated/unexplained errors, which is the expected signature of "correct code, stale types." Cannot be fully verified until the user runs the commands below and retests Pending Decisions, Candidate Comparison, Follow-Ups, and Feedback submission end-to-end.

**Required from the user:**
```
cd backend
npx prisma generate
npx prisma migrate dev --name interview_slot_split
```
Then restart the backend and retest: scheduling an interview from the Candidates page (should behave identically to before), submitting feedback, HM Pending Decisions and Candidate Comparison, and the Follow-Ups pending-feedback list.

**Update:** user confirmed `prisma generate` + `prisma migrate dev --name interview_slot_split` ran successfully (migration file dated 2026-08-30 13:36, confirmed by reading the generated SQL directly -- correctly moves `scheduledAt`/`vacancyStageId` off `Interview` onto `InterviewSlot`, adds the new FKs). `tsc --noEmit` on the backend is now clean against the real regenerated client, which also caught two real bugs the stale-client pass couldn't have: (1) a generic helper function in `hiringManager.controller.ts` that didn't narrow types correctly when used as a `.map()` callback -- replaced with a plain inline map; (2) `roundLabel: undefined` not assignable where the Prisma field type is `string | null` (this project's tsconfig has `exactOptionalPropertyTypes: true`) -- fixed to `roundLabel ?? null`. Also updated the two vacancy/candidate deletion scripts and the wipe-all script, which still referenced the old `InterviewPanelist.interviewId` field and didn't know about the new `InterviewSlot` table at all.

## HR Interviews module (task #30) built on the new schema

Built the actual calendar module the corrections doc asked for, on top of the InterviewSlot/Interview split above. `frontend/src/pages/hr/InterviewsPage.tsx` (+ `.css`), wired at `/hr/interviews` (replacing the `ComingSoon` placeholder).

- Month calendar, days with any scheduled slot highlighted (red, per the wireframe's explicit color choice) and clickable; other days inert.
- Clicking a highlighted day shrinks the calendar and opens a side panel listing that day's slots, auto-labelled "Vacancy - Round - HR's label" (e.g. "Software Engineer - Technical Interview - Round 02"); clicking a slot drills into its detail (panel + candidates + per-candidate feedback-submitted count).
- Esc backs out one level at a time (slot detail -> day panel -> calendar-only), matching the wireframe's explicit instruction.
- "+" button opens a 3-option menu matching the wireframe exactly: Assign Interview Panel (adds staff to a vacancy's standing interviewer pool -- thin UI over the existing `assignInterviewerToVacancy` endpoint, nothing new on the backend), Schedule Interview (creates a slot via the new `POST /interviews` primitive -- no candidate required), Add Candidate(s) to Interview (two-step: multi-select shortlisted candidates with a Select All action, then pick which existing slot to add them to from an auto-labelled dropdown, via `POST /interviews/:id/candidates`).

New backend endpoints added alongside this: `GET /interviews` (list slots, optional `from`/`to` range, for the calendar) and `GET /interviews/slots/:slotId` (slot detail for the drill-in panel).

Deliberately did not chase pixel-perfect wireframe fidelity (square-centered popups, exact spacing) per the earlier locked "keep current theme, fix structure" decision -- functionality and information architecture match the wireframe, visual polish matches the rest of the app's existing black/gold theme instead.

**Not yet done / explicitly out of scope for this pass:** email confirmation to candidates when added via the new Add Candidate(s) flow reuses the same template as the single-candidate path; no interview-cancellation/reschedule UI yet; the interviewer-side "My Interviews" screen wasn't touched (task #32) even though it now technically reflects multi-candidate slots correctly (each candidate still shows as its own row, which was already the right shape for that screen).

## HR Interviews page width bug — root cause found (class-name collision, not CSS logic)

Multiple rounds of CSS edits to `InterviewsPage.css` (`width: 900px` → `width: 100%` → `flex: 1 1 auto`) never visibly changed the reported "calendar doesn't reach the right edge" gap, which was the tell that the bug wasn't in the file being edited at all. Root cause, found via `getBoundingClientRect()` numbers pulled directly from the user's DevTools console rather than more screenshot-guessing: `frontend/src/pages/interviewer/MyInterviewsPage.css` independently defined `.ivw-page { max-width: 1000px; ... }` and several other identical class names (`.ivw-title`, `.ivw-divider`, `.ivw-muted`, `.ivw-error`). Vite bundles all page CSS globally in dev (no CSS Modules/scoping in this project), so same-named rules collide sitewide regardless of which route is active — the interviewer page's `max-width: 1000px` was silently capping the HR Interviews page's width, and no amount of editing the HR file's own rule could ever fix a bug caused by a different file. **Fix:** renamed every class in `MyInterviewsPage.css`/`.tsx` from `ivw-` to a unique `miv-` prefix. **Done, verified** — confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` that `.ivw-calendar-wrap`'s right edge now matches `.hr-main`'s content-box right edge exactly, and via `grep` that `ivw-` no longer appears in any file but `InterviewsPage.css`/`.tsx`.

**Process note worth keeping:** two of the DevTools screenshots used during this investigation were red herrings — one had DevTools docked to the side (squeezing the actual page into a ~250–400px viewport, making every measurement meaningless), another was in Chrome's Device Toolbar/responsive-emulation mode (a simulated viewport, not the real window). Getting real `window.innerWidth` + `getBoundingClientRect()` numbers via the Console (not eyeballing screenshot pixels, and not trusting a screenshot without first confirming DevTools' dock state/emulation mode) is what actually resolved this after several rounds of guessing.

## HR Candidates — detail view converted from a modal to a routed page

Per user feedback: the candidate detail view (CV preview, review notes, applicant history, hiring manager assignment, interviews, schedule interview, reject/shortlist) moved from an in-page modal to its own route, `/hr/candidates/:applicationId` (new file `frontend/src/pages/hr/CandidateDetailPage.tsx`, reusing `CandidatesPage.css`'s `cnd-` classes). Section order changed to match the requested reading order: CV Preview first, then Review Notes, Applicant History, Hiring Manager, Interviews, Schedule Interview, and finally Reject/Shortlist at the very bottom (previously top-of-modal). All modal-only state/handlers (`openDetail`, `closeDetail`, `handleAssignHm`, `handleScheduleInterview`, etc.) were removed from `CandidatesPage.tsx`; the row click now does `navigate(`/hr/candidates/${r.id}`)`.

There's no backend endpoint that returns a single application already shaped like the candidates-list row (`GET /applications/:id` returns a differently-shaped object), so rather than reconciling two shapes or adding a new endpoint, the detail page re-fetches the full candidates list on mount and finds the matching row by id client-side. Fine at this app's data scale; would need a dedicated endpoint if the candidate list ever grew large enough for this to matter.

**Also fixed in the same pass, all confirmed via `tsc --noEmit` on both frontend and backend:**
- Removed the "Remove" button (and its now-dead handler) from the CV Review & Confirm step, per user feedback.
- Upload CV's "Upload Complete" summary step removed entirely — full success now closes the modal and shows a bottom toast ("Successfully uploaded N CVs"); any failure keeps the modal open and shows the same inline red notice style used for the existing "PDF only" rejection, naming the actual reason (e.g. "already applied to this vacancy") rather than a generic message.
- Candidate ID display zero-padded (`C-0055`) — cosmetic only, does not renumber existing database ids.
- Added a shared `roleLabel()` helper (`api/staff.ts`) so every staff picker (Assign Interview Panel, Schedule Interview, Edit Vacancy panel list) shows "Hiring Manager" instead of the raw `HIRING_MANAGER` enum value.
- Schedule Interview's Panel field reworked from a per-person checkbox list to a single click-to-expand "Panel (N members)" summary — the vacancy's whole assigned panel is now always included on the slot, no manual per-person selection (this only applies to the standalone Schedule Interview modal on the Interviews calendar page; the per-application Schedule Interview section on the new Candidate Detail page still uses its own panel checklist, unchanged).
- A repo-wide sweep replaced every user-facing " -- " (double dash) with a single " - " across both frontend and backend (26 occurrences); left untouched everywhere it only appeared inside code comments.
- Two new one-off backend scripts (`backend/scripts/add-staff.ts`, `delete-staff.ts`, `rename-staff.ts`) for creating/removing/renaming `User` rows from the command line, following the same pattern as the existing `delete-candidate.ts`/`delete-vacancy.ts`. `delete-staff.ts` accepts either an exact email (single person) or a name prefix (bulk, e.g. clearing out "Regression Interviewer 2 ..." test accounts) and cleans up every FK-dependent row first, nulling out optional "who did this" pointers on records that should survive rather than deleting those records.
- `prisma/seed.ts`'s `Ian Interviewer`/`Harry Hiring Manager` renamed to real-looking names (`Ian Foster`/`Harry Dawson`) so a fresh seed doesn't bake the role into the display name — does not retroactively rename already-seeded rows (`rename-staff.ts` handles that).

## HR Follow Ups page — Shortlist/Reject removed, "Calls" section added, "Round" renamed to "Stage"

Per user feedback, three changes to `frontend/src/pages/hr/FollowUpsPage.tsx`:

- Removed the Shortlist/Reject buttons and the "Action" column entirely from the "Pending CV Review" table, along with the now-dead `handleShortlist`/`handleReject` handlers, the `actionBusyId`/`actionError` state, and the `updateApplicationStatus` import. This table is now read-only — HR reviews/decides candidates from the Candidate Detail page instead. Also removed the description line "New applications waiting on a Shortlist/Reject decision. Resolves as soon as you act - no reminder email applies here." (no longer accurate once the buttons were gone).
- Renamed "Round" to "Stage" everywhere it appeared as a user-facing label in the two Schedule Interview forms (`InterviewsPage.tsx`'s standalone modal and `CandidateDetailPage.tsx`'s per-application section) — label text, placeholder text, and validation error messages. Left the underlying `VacancyStage`/`round`/`roundLabel` identifiers in code as-is; this was a copy-only rename, not a schema/type rename, to avoid an unnecessarily large diff for a label change.
- Investigated the "can't type the AM/PM segment" report on the native `<input type="time">` control: confirmed this is a browser/OS locale behavior (12-hour format only accepts A/P or arrow-key input for that segment, not digit-typing) rather than an app bug. User confirmed no fix needed.

New "Calls" section added to the same page, backed by a new `upcomingCalls` array on `GET /follow-ups` (`backend/src/controllers/followUp.controller.ts`): every `Interview` whose slot is in the future, excluding applications already `HIRED`/`REJECTED`, sorted soonest-first — same "computed fresh on every read, no stored pending state" pattern as the existing two sections. Each row shows candidate/vacancy/stage/scheduled time and a "Call" button that reveals the candidate's phone number (`Candidate.phoneNumber`, already existed in schema but was unused) in a small popover for HR to dial manually from their own phone; shows "No phone number on file" when null.

This followed a scoping conversation that also covered an "Emails" half of the original ask (candidate invitation/rejection emails, interviewer reminders) — turned out those already exist and fire automatically today: a HIRED/REJECTED decision emails the candidate (`application.controller.ts`), and scheduling an interview emails both the candidate and every panelist (`interview.controller.ts`), on top of the pre-existing manual "remind a panelist for feedback" flow already on this page. All of it goes through a dev-only mailer (`backend/src/utils/mailer.ts`) that logs to the console — no real SMTP/Outlook is connected, so none of this is actually delivered yet. Once this was surfaced, the user confirmed no email UI changes were needed and to build the Calls section only.

**Follow-up round (same page):** removed the two section-description lines under "Pending Feedback" and "Calls" (just section headings now, matching "Pending CV Review" which never had one). Widened `.fu-page`'s `max-width` from a fixed `1100px` to `90vw` to match the spacing convention already used on Vacancies/Candidates/Interviews. Wiring up real SMTP/Outlook delivery for `mailer.ts` (see above) is explicitly deferred until tasks #31-36 (remaining screens) are done — tracked as task #43. Added `backend/scripts/seed-followup-demo.ts`, a self-contained, re-run-safe demo-data script (own vacancy/stages/candidates) that creates one past interview with no feedback (Priya Demo -> Pending Feedback) and one future interview with a phone number on file (Sam Demo -> Calls), so the user can see both sections populated without needing real production data.

**Second follow-up round (same page), from user feedback on the screenshots:**
- Pending CV Review table: dropped the candidate's email (name only in that column); Vacancy column collapsed from title+department stacked on two lines to a single "Title - Department" line.
- Pending Feedback cards: header line simplified to just "{vacancy} - {stage}{ - roundLabel if the slot has one} - {candidate name}" — dropped the scheduled-date/time and the "(closed vacancy)" tag, since the user's spec named exactly vacancy/stage/round/candidate/interviewer/button and nothing else. Added `roundLabel` (the slot's own optional free-text label, e.g. "After lunch") to the `GET /follow-ups` `pendingFeedback.round` payload so it's available to show when present.
- Pending Feedback "Send Email" behavior changed: previously, sending just disabled that one button and relabeled it "Sent" while the entry stayed visible; now, once a reminder is successfully sent, that interviewer's row is removed from the list entirely (tracked client-side via a `dismissedPendingFeedback` set of `interviewId-userId` keys), and the whole card disappears once every pending interviewer on it has been reminded. This is a deliberate UI-only dismissal, not a real "feedback resolved" state — the underlying Pending Feedback data is still computed fresh from actual submitted `Feedback` rows server-side, so a page refresh will bring a dismissed entry back if that interviewer still hasn't actually submitted feedback. Flagged to the user as an intentional trade-off, not silently changed.
- `.fu-action-btn` (used by both "Send Email" and "Call") given a shared `min-width: 92px` and centered text so the two buttons render as same-sized pills; the Calls table's action cell right-aligned (`text-align: right`) to match the Pending Feedback card rows, which already right-align their button via flexbox.

**Third follow-up round (same page):**
- Sending a feedback reminder now shows a bottom toast ("Email sent to {name}.") in addition to removing the row, using the same toast pattern as CandidatesPage/VacanciesPage/InterviewsPage (4s auto-dismiss).
- Calls: replaced the small inline popover with a centered modal (same `fu-modal-backdrop` overlay as the Send Reminder modal) showing the candidate's name and phone number in a moderately large font (26px, not full-screen-huge), with two actions: "Cancel" (closes, no side effect) and "Done" (dismisses that candidate from the Calls list, same client-side-only dismissal pattern as Pending Feedback, and shows a toast: "Call with {name} marked as done."). Wording chosen over the user's suggested "done calling" for brevity, consistent with the existing short button-label style elsewhere on this page.

## HR Follow Ups — round-1-auto / round-2+-manual candidate invite emails ("Interview Invites")

User proposed: auto-send the candidate invite email for the first interview round (as already happens today), but from round 2 onward make it a manual action surfaced in Follow Ups. Two real ambiguities were raised and resolved before building, both taking the recommended option:

1. **When does the round-1 email fire?** At Schedule Interview, same as today (not at the Shortlist click) — round 1 keeps its exact existing behavior, nothing changed there.
2. **Does the new section replace Calls for round 2+, or sit alongside it?** Alongside — a round 2+ candidate can appear in both "Interview Invites" (needs the email sent) and "Calls" (upcoming interview to ring ahead of) at the same time; they're two independent to-dos.

**Implementation:**
- `interview.controller.ts`: both places that auto-send the candidate-facing "your interview has been scheduled" email (`scheduleInterview()`'s single-candidate path and `addCandidatesToSlot()`'s multi-add path) now gate that specific send on `vacancyStage.order === 1`. The panelist-facing "you've been assigned to interview X" email is unaffected — that's an internal staff notification, sent every round regardless.
- No new schema field for "was the invite sent" — reused the existing `AuditLog` (`NOTIFICATION_SENT`, `reason: "interview_scheduled_candidate"`) as the source of truth, since both branches were already writing it. `GET /follow-ups` now also returns `pendingInvites`: every round 2+ Interview whose application isn't HIRED/REJECTED and that has no such audit log entry yet, sorted soonest-first (no past/future filter, mirroring Pending Feedback's "stays visible until resolved" approach).
- New endpoint `POST /interviews/:id/candidate/remind` (`sendCandidateInvite` in `followUp.controller.ts`) — same "fixed template, editable before send" pattern as the existing panelist feedback reminder, and writes the identical audit reason string, so a manually-sent invite correctly clears the `pendingInvites` entry the same way an automatic one would.
- Frontend: new "Interview Invites" section on the Follow Ups page, positioned between Pending Feedback and Calls per the user's explicit ordering request. Same table layout/action-button styling as Calls, "Send Email" opens the same reminder-modal pattern already used for feedback reminders, pre-filled via a new `buildInviteTemplate()` (reuses the exact wording of the automatic round-1 email so round 2+ candidates get consistent messaging). Successful send dismisses the row client-side and shows the same toast pattern as the other two actions on this page.
- The existing `seed-followup-demo.ts` demo data already exercises this without changes: "Sam Demo" is on stage 2 ("Final Interview", order 2) with no audit log entry, so re-running the Follow Ups fetch now shows Sam Demo under both Calls and Interview Invites simultaneously — a live confirmation of the "alongside, not replacing" decision above.

**Follow-up: split into "Interview Invites - Interviewers" and "Interview Invites - Candidates".** The round-1-auto/round-2+-manual rule above only covered the candidate-facing email; the panelist-facing "you've been assigned to interview X" email was still auto-sending every round regardless. Extended the same rule to panelists for consistency, and gave each audience its own Follow Ups section rather than merging them:

- `interview.controller.ts`'s `scheduleInterview()`: the panelist notification loop and the candidate invite are now both inside the same `if (vacancyStage.order === 1)` block (previously only the candidate send was gated). `addCandidatesToSlot()` was already candidate-only and needed no change here (it never sent panelist emails to begin with — panelists are notified once, at slot creation, not per candidate added).
- `GET /follow-ups` now also returns `pendingPanelistInvites`: same derivation as `pendingInvites` but grouped by interview with a `pendingFrom` list of not-yet-notified panelists (mirroring `pendingFeedback`'s shape), checked against the audit log by matching both `reason: "interview_scheduled_panelist"` and the specific panelist's email as `recipient` (so if a slot has 3 panelists and only 1 was manually notified, the other 2 still show up correctly).
- New endpoint `POST /interviews/:id/panelists/:userId/invite` (`sendPanelistInterviewInvite`) — same pattern as the existing feedback-reminder/candidate-invite manual-send endpoints.
- Frontend: "Interview Invites - Interviewers" section added above "Interview Invites - Candidates" (renamed from the plain "Interview Invites"), per explicit ordering request. Interviewers section uses the same per-panelist card layout as Pending Feedback (one card per interview, one row + Send Email button per still-unnotified panelist), pre-filled via a new `buildPanelistInviteTemplate()` that reuses the existing panelist-notification email wording.

**Table column widths fixed.** All `.fu-table` tables were using the browser default auto layout, which sizes each column to its content and leaves the remainder as dead space after the last column instead of spreading it across the row -- visible as "Applied"/the action column sitting well short of the table's right edge. Added `table-layout: fixed` plus explicit percentage-width classes applied to each table's `<th>` row.

First attempt at this got it wrong: one shared set of column classes (`fu-col-candidate`, `fu-col-vacancy`, `fu-col-applied`, `fu-col-stage`, `fu-col-scheduled`, `fu-col-action`) was applied to both the 3-column Pending CV Review table and the 5-column Interview Invites/Calls tables, but a valid `table-layout: fixed` column set has to sum to 100% *per table* -- reusing `fu-col-candidate`/`fu-col-vacancy` across both shapes meant the 3-column table's widths only summed to 76% (still leaving a gap) and the 5-column table's summed to 114%. Fixed by splitting into two independent sets: `fu-col3-candidate`/`fu-col3-vacancy`/`fu-col3-applied` (30/45/25, Pending CV Review only) and `fu-col-candidate`/`fu-col-vacancy`/`fu-col-stage`/`fu-col-scheduled`/`fu-col-action` (20/26/22/20/12, the two 5-column tables) -- each set now correctly sums to 100%.

Also created task #44 (not built): add a small read-only "Email History" list to the Candidate Detail page (per-candidate, sourced from existing `NOTIFICATION_SENT` audit log entries) as the lighter-weight alternative to a dedicated sidebar email tab -- deferred until explicitly requested. Flagged that the audit log currently only stores recipient/channel/reason, not the actual subject/body sent, so this can only show "what kind of email, when" without further changes to every send path.

**Still not actually fixed after the two width-percentage corrections above:** the column widths were correct (each set does sum to 100%, verified), but the visible gap the user kept pointing at wasn't a column-width problem at all -- it was that the last column's *content* (the "Applied" text, or the action button) is left-aligned by default within its own cell, so even a full-width column still shows its text sitting well short of the actual right edge, with the true corner just being empty cell padding. Added `.fu-table th:last-child, .fu-table td:last-child { text-align: right; }` so the last column's content itself hugs the corner, which is what "reach the corner" actually meant.

**One more pass:** flush-right touching the table border read as too far once seen live, so added `padding-right: 24px` to that same last-column rule to pull it in slightly. Also centered the second column (`nth-child(2)` -- Vacancy, on every table on this page) per request, so each row now reads left-aligned/centered/right-aligned across its columns instead of everything left-aligned.
