## Cloud deployment

User relayed a course requirement to "run the system in the cloud" after building it, but didn't know which platform the lecturer expects (confirmed via clarifying question — genuinely unspecified, not just unstated). Before recommending a platform, checked whether the app could even be split across two hosts (a typical free-tier pattern: static frontend on Vercel/Netlify, backend on Render/Railway) and found a real blocker: **every frontend API call uses a relative path** (`fetch("/api/...")` throughout `frontend/src/api/*.ts`), which only resolves correctly today because `vite.config.ts`'s dev-server proxy forwards `/api` to `localhost:4000`. Deployed as two separate production hosts, those calls would 404 against the frontend's own domain instead of the backend — this would have silently broken the entire deployed app regardless of which platform got picked, so it needed fixing before any deployment guide would be honest.

**Fix (Done, `tsc --noEmit` clean):** `backend/src/app.ts` now serves the built `frontend/dist` directly from the same Express server, gated behind `fs.existsSync(frontendDist)` so it's a no-op in local dev (where `frontend/dist` doesn't exist — `npm run dev` still uses the Vite proxy as before, untouched). Added a catch-all route (`app.get(/^(?!\/api).*/, ...)` — a raw RegExp, not the v5 string-wildcard syntax, since Express 5's `path-to-regexp` upgrade only changed string patterns) so client-side React Router routes survive a hard refresh. Also added a `"start": "tsx src/server.ts"` script to `backend/package.json` — there was only `"dev"` (watch mode) before, no non-watch entry point suitable for a host to run.

**Recommendation given (not a platform decision made unilaterally — presented as a default, not the only option):** Aiven's free MySQL plan (genuinely free, no time limit, unlike PlanetScale which dropped its free tier in 2024, or Railway's one-time $5/30-day trial credit) for the database, deployed as one combined service on Render's free web-service tier (no card required; free tier cold-starts after 15 min idle, acceptable for a demo, not for something meant to stay live). Full step-by-step (Aiven setup, `prisma migrate deploy` against the cloud DB, Render build/start commands, required env vars) written to `docs/cloud-deployment-guide.md`, with Railway noted as the alternative if the team would rather deploy right before the demo than keep something running for weeks. Searched current (Aug 2026) pricing/free-tier terms rather than relying on training-data knowledge, since platform free-tier terms change often — sources cited in the guide itself.

## Sprint 2 backlog reconciliation + built 4 remaining stories (SCRUM2-29/30/42/31)

User pasted screenshots of the team's actual Sprint 2 Jira board (SCRUM2-XX tickets) and asked for a corrected read against what's really built, after independently cross-checking my Selenium sprint-scope answer against `story-implementation-table.md`. Two separate problems surfaced, not one:

1. **The Jira board itself has duplicate tickets** — SCRUM2-36/43 are both "complete audit logs," SCRUM2-37/44 are both "automatic system backups." Flagged as a board-hygiene issue for the user to fix, not something I can resolve from here.
2. **`story-implementation-table.md` was stale in both directions.** Four rows (US-31 Candidate count per stage, US-35 Department Performance, US-36/US-49 Hiring Trends) said "Not Built" despite shipping earlier in this project (Management's Candidate Progress page, Leadership's Department Performance/Hiring Trends tabs) — pure doc drift, corrected in the table with no new code. Separately, five real stories were still genuinely unbuilt: US-27 (interview reminder emails), US-30/SCRUM2-30 (duplicate candidate detection — see below, this one turned out more nuanced), US-42 (configurable notification templates), US-44 (automatic backups), and the in-app half of US-50 (multi-channel notifications).

**Scope decisions confirmed via `AskUserQuestion` before writing any code** (all four were genuine forks, not something to guess at):
- **US-44 (automatic backups): left out of scope.** User deferred to the lecturer rather than picking an option — correctly not built this pass. Systems page continues honestly reporting backups as not yet configured.
- **US-39/SCRUM2-52 (Gmail vs SMTP): kept SMTP, documented the gap.** The story literally says "Gmail integration" (implying the Gmail API, OAuth2, refresh tokens); what's built is generic SMTP. User chose to keep SMTP (already works with a Gmail account via SMTP AUTH + app password) rather than build real Gmail OAuth, which would need a Google Cloud OAuth client this environment can't provision anyway. Documented explicitly in `story-implementation-table.md`'s US-39 row and flagged here so nobody claims literal Gmail-API integration to a grader who checks closely.
- **US-27 (interview reminder emails): real scheduled job, not HR-triggered.** User explicitly chose "real automated" over the Follow-Ups-style manual-send pattern used everywhere else in this app for reminders — this is the one send path that genuinely runs with no human action.
- **US-46/SCRUM2-30 (duplicate candidate detection): warn, don't block; matching scope confirmed as CV+vacancy (the original decided design from the story-implementation-table note), not the broader fuzzy-name/exact-email-only options offered.**

**Real finding while building the duplicate-detection fix**: matching logic already existed and was already running silently — `confirmCvUpload` failed with a P2002 unique-constraint error on a repeat email, and the frontend caught that specific error string and looked the candidate up itself to re-apply them to the new vacancy. It worked, but it was two things at once: fragile (a future wording change to that one error string would have silently broken the re-apply path) and invisible to HR (a "new" CV upload could silently turn out to be someone already in the system, with zero indication). Rebuilt as a deliberate, up-front `Candidate.findUnique({where:{email}})` check in `candidate.controller.ts`'s `confirmCvUpload`, returning a distinct `matched` array in the response (separate from `created`/`failed`) with the existing candidate's name and every other vacancy they've already applied to. `CandidatesPage.tsx`'s upload flow now shows an explicit amber toast naming who matched and why, instead of folding the merge silently into the generic success count. `findCandidateByEmail` (frontend) is no longer called from this flow — its regex-based failure detection is gone — but the export itself was left in `api/candidates.ts` in case something else wants it later.

**Schema (backend/prisma/schema.prisma) — three additions, migration NOT run from this sandbox (no DB access, per standing constraint):**
- `InterviewSlot.reminderSentAt DateTime?` — set once the reminder job has processed a slot, so it never double-sends on its next 15-minute pass.
- `NotificationTemplate` model (`key` unique, `subject`, `body`, `updatedAt`, `updatedByUserId`) — SCRUM2-45.
- `Notification` model (`userId`, `type`, `message`, `link`, `read`, `createdAt`) — SCRUM2-31, one row per (user, event).
- **User must run `npx prisma migrate dev --name add_notifications_and_reminders` (or equivalent) themselves, then the seed script below.** Until then the real Prisma client doesn't have these models, which is the *only* remaining category of `tsc --noEmit` error in the backend right now (confirmed — see verification note below) — every other line typechecks clean.

**SCRUM2-29 (interview reminders) — `backend/src/jobs/interviewReminders.ts`:** `node-cron` (added to `backend/package.json` — user needs to run `npm install` in `backend/`, not run from this sandbox against the real `node_modules` given the earlier corruption incident this session), checked every 15 minutes, catches any `InterviewSlot` starting within the next 24 hours with `reminderSentAt` still null. Sends one combined reminder per panelist (looping per-interview would double-send if a slot has multiple candidates) and one per candidate in the slot, via the new template system, then marks the slot reminded regardless of individual send failures (same "don't retry-storm on a transient email failure" contract as every other `sendEmail()` call site in this app). 24h/15min are defaults, not locked requirements — trivially adjustable in the file. Started from `server.ts`, not `app.ts`, since `app.ts` is imported directly by the Vitest+supertest suite and a cron job ticking during a test run would be a real problem.

**SCRUM2-45 (configurable templates) — `backend/src/utils/notificationTemplates.ts` + `notificationTemplate.controller/routes.ts` + `frontend/src/pages/admin/NotificationTemplatesPage.tsx`:** Scoped deliberately to only the fully-automatic sends nobody edits at send time — interview scheduled (candidate + panelist, 3 call sites in `interview.controller.ts`), hiring decision (hire/reject), password reset, and the two new reminder templates. The three manual Follow-Ups sends (feedback reminder, manual candidate/panelist invite in `followUp.controller.ts`) are explicitly NOT template-backed — HR already free-types that content per send, which is already more configurable than a fixed template would be, so folding them in would be a downgrade. Templates are read via `renderTemplate(key, vars)`, which falls back to the exact original hardcoded string if no DB row exists yet (so behavior is byte-identical until IT Admin actually edits something) — `{{placeholder}}` substitution, not a templating library. Keys are a fixed, code-referenced set (`TEMPLATE_KEYS`); IT Admin can edit/reset but not add new keys, since a new key wouldn't have anything wired to read it. `backend/scripts/seed-notification-templates.ts` (idempotent upsert, run manually: `npx tsx scripts/seed-notification-templates.ts`) seeds the default text as real rows — not required for the app to work, just gives the editor screen a starting point instead of an empty "not customized" state. New `NOTIFICATION_TEMPLATE_UPDATED` audit action added (both `AuditAction` union and `auditLog.controller.ts`'s `EVENT_TYPE`/`describeAction` — that `Record<AuditAction,string>` is exhaustive, so both needed updating together or `tsc` would fail).

**SCRUM2-31 (in-app notifications) — `backend/src/utils/notify.ts` + `notification.controller/routes.ts` + `frontend/src/components/NotificationBell.tsx`:** Single `notifyUser(userId, type, message, link?)` choke point, same pattern as `writeAuditLog()`, called alongside every STAFF-facing `sendEmail()` (interview scheduled → panelists, the two manual Follow-Ups panelist sends, both new reminder sends) — never for candidate-facing emails, since candidates aren't app users and have no inbox. Shared `NotificationBell` component dropped into all 6 role layouts' sidebars (HR/HM/Interviewer/Management/Leadership/IT Admin), positioned directly above the logout button in each — bell icon + unread badge, dropdown list, mark-one/mark-all read. Deliberately no polling or websocket layer: fetches on mount and again when the dropdown opens, matching this app's existing fetch-on-load pattern everywhere else (no other screen here has realtime infra either).

**Verification performed from this sandbox (no DB access, so this is the real ceiling — see the standing constraint):**
- `backend`: `npx tsc --noEmit` — caught and fixed two real bugs unrelated to the missing-generated-client noise: (1) `interviewReminders.ts` accessed `slot.interviews[0]` without a type-narrowing guard (fixed by destructuring into a checked `firstInterview` and using that consistently instead of re-indexing); (2) `notificationTemplates.ts`'s `substitute()` returned `string | undefined` from the regex replacer callback under `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` (fixed with an explicit `undefined` check instead of relying on `in`). After both fixes, the *only* remaining errors are the expected/unavoidable ones: `Property 'notification'/'notificationTemplate' does not exist on PrismaClient` (resolves once the user runs the migration — Prisma client regeneration needs schema+DB, not just the schema file) and `Cannot find module 'node-cron'` (resolves once the user runs `npm install`). Confirmed no other file in the backend picked up a stray error.
- `frontend`: `npx tsc --noEmit` — clean, zero errors.
- `backend`: `npx vitest run` — all 43 existing tests still pass (confirms `app.ts` loads fine with the two new routers registered, nothing broke).
- `frontend`: `npx vitest run` — could not run; blocked by a pre-existing sandbox environment issue (`rolldown` missing a `wasm32-wasi` native binding for this Linux sandbox's architecture), unrelated to anything touched this session. Not fixed by re-running `npm install` from this sandbox against the real `node_modules`, per the standing lesson from earlier this session (an earlier sandbox `npm install` against this same real, non-disposable folder is what corrupted `react-router` in the first place). Frontend `tsc --noEmit` passing clean is the available substitute check.

**What the user needs to run themselves, in order, before any of this is live:**
1. `cd backend && npm install` (pulls in `node-cron`/`@types/node-cron`).
2. `npx prisma migrate dev --name add_notifications_and_reminders` (creates the two new tables + the new `InterviewSlot` column — this needs a real DB connection, not available from this sandbox).
3. `npx tsx scripts/seed-notification-templates.ts` (optional — seeds default template text; the app works without it, just falls back to hardcoded defaults).
4. Re-run `npx tsc --noEmit` in `backend/` to confirm the last 17 (now-expected) errors clear.

**Confirmed working end-to-end by the user** (ran `npx prisma generate` after `migrate dev` skipped it silently, then `backend/scripts/demo-new-features.ts`, a new one-shot demo script that seeds a real vacancy/candidate/interview slot 2 hours out and triggers the reminder check immediately instead of waiting for the cron tick): reminder emails fire and are audit-logged, the interviewer's notification bell shows the reminder, and templates are seeded/editable. One real bug this surfaced and fixed: `demo-new-features.ts` itself indexed an array element (`chunks[0].length`) without a type-narrowing guard under `noUncheckedIndexedAccess` — same class of bug as the two caught during the main pass, fixed by holding the buffer in its own variable instead of re-indexing.

**Sandbox tsc broken after the user's own `npm install`, not by anything in this codebase**: after the user ran `npm install` on their Windows machine against the same real `node_modules` this sandbox also reads from, this sandbox's `npx tsc` started failing with "Unable to resolve @typescript/typescript-linux-x64" -- TypeScript 7.x ships a native-binary-per-platform package, and their Windows install swapped in the Windows binaries where this sandbox's Linux binaries used to be, since it's one shared folder. This is expected friction from the two environments sharing a real `node_modules`, not a code regression -- the fix is running `tsc --noEmit` on the user's own machine (which has the correct Windows binaries), not attempting another install from this sandbox (which would just flip the problem back, and risks the same kind of corruption flagged earlier this session).

---

## UI polish pass: notification bell placement, toast wording/positioning, forgot-password made real

User reviewed the built features live and sent screenshots with concrete UI feedback, plus one real functionality gap spotted along the way (Forgot Password was a static "not built" placeholder despite the backend endpoint existing and working).

**Notification bell relocated to a fixed top-right position, every page.** It previously lived inside each role layout's sidebar (above Log out); its dropdown was getting clipped by the sidebar's own edge. `NotificationBell.css` rewritten to `position: fixed; top/right` instead of sitting in the sidebar's flex flow -- since `position: fixed` escapes ancestor layout regardless of which of the 6 layout files actually mounts the component, no changes were needed to where `<NotificationBell />` is called from. Dropdown now opens downward-right (`top: calc(100% + 10px); right: 0`) instead of upward-left, matching its new anchor point.

**New behavior: unread notifications get a one-time top-right toast, separate from the bell's own read/unread state.** Extended the shared `Toast` component with a `position` prop (`"bottom-center"` default, unchanged for every existing call site; `"top-right"` new). `NotificationBell` now pops a `top-right` toast for any unread notification it hasn't already toasted this browser session (tracked via `sessionStorage`, not the read flag itself -- dismissing or missing the toast must never mark anything read). If multiple are new at once, one summary toast ("N new notifications") instead of stacking several. Auto-dismisses after 5s regardless -- the item itself stays unread (dot, bold row, counted in the badge) until the user actually opens the bell and clicks it, or hits "Mark all read". This was a real design fork worth stating plainly since it's easy to build wrong: the toast is a heads-up, the dropdown is the source of truth.

**Three smaller, purely cosmetic fixes, per the user's screenshots:**
- Audit Logs: removed the "Showing the most recent 200 entries..." hint paragraph entirely (`AuditLogsPage.tsx`).
- CandidatesPage's duplicate-match toast (SCRUM2-30): reworded away from the "--" separator style and the more clinical original phrasing ("already exists in the system -- previously applied to: X. Linked...") to plain prose ("X already has a profile with us, so we linked this CV to their existing application for Y."). Toast font-size reduced app-wide (13.5px -> 12.5px, `Toast.css`) since the request was about the popup reading as smaller/lighter-weight generally, not just this one message.
- Notification Templates page: removed the explanatory subtitle paragraph entirely: removed `max-width: 780px` from `.tmpl-list` so the row list now stretches to match the full-width divider line below the heading, instead of stopping short of it.

**Altrium logo dark-mode legibility.** The polygon's fill (`#111111`, near-black) has no border, so on a dark background it can blend in almost entirely, leaving only the white arrow and gold dot visible -- which is what "looks weird" was describing. Added a `stroke="#f5a623"` (the existing brand gold) around the hexagon so it stays legible against literally any background color, dark or light, rather than depending on contrast against whatever's behind it. Flagged directly to the user: the app itself has no real dark theme and already declares `color-scheme: light` globally (`index.css`) specifically so browsers don't auto-invert it -- if the page still renders dark, that's very likely a browser-level forced-dark extension (e.g. Dark Reader) repainting the page outside the app's control, not something this codebase is doing. The gold stroke is a real, permanent improvement either way, just flagging that it may not fully neutralize a third-party extension's own recoloring.

**Forgot Password: built for real, not just visually.** The backend (`POST /auth/forgot-password`, `POST /auth/reset-password`) already worked -- generates a hashed, time-limited reset token, emails a reset link via the templated `auth_password_reset` send, and verifies/consumes the token on reset. The frontend never called any of it: `Login.tsx`'s "Forgot Password?" button just toggled a static "not built into the interface yet" message. Fixed:
- `frontend/src/api/auth.ts`: added `forgotPasswordRequest(email)` / `resetPasswordRequest(token, newPassword)`.
- `Login.tsx`: "Forgot Password?" now expands an inline mini-form (email + Send/Cancel) that actually calls the endpoint and displays the backend's own generic response message (same message whether or not the account exists, by design -- the frontend doesn't infer success/failure from it, just shows it verbatim).
- New `frontend/src/pages/ResetPasswordPage.tsx` (route: `/reset-password?token=...`, matching the link the backend actually emails) -- new-password + confirm form, client-side length/match checks before hitting the API, success state with a link back to `/login`. Reuses `Login.css`'s classes rather than a parallel stylesheet.
- **Real bug found and fixed while wiring this up, unprompted:** `forgotPassword`'s reset link was hardcoded to `http://localhost:5173/...`. Fine for local dev, but would have silently sent broken links to real users the moment this app is deployed (production serves frontend+backend from one shared origin per the earlier cloud-deployment fix -- not `localhost:5173`). Auto-deriving from the request's own host was considered and rejected: in local dev the request arrives at the backend's own port (:4000 behind Vite's proxy), not the frontend's port (:5173), so that would have broken local dev to fix production. Landed on an explicit `FRONTEND_URL` env var, defaulting to the working `localhost:5173` when unset -- zero behavior change for local dev, one env var to set on deploy. Documented in `backend/.env.example`.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean. Backend `tsc --noEmit` could not be run from this sandbox for this batch -- see the note above about the shared `node_modules`'s native-binary conflict introduced by the user's own `npm install`; this needs to be checked on the user's machine instead. Manually re-read the touched backend function for correctness in lieu of a compiler check, given the small, low-risk surface area of the change (one ternary added to an existing function).

## Login/notification-bell polish round 2, HTML email formatting, seed-account domain rename

Follow-up round after the user actually test-drove the forgot-password flow end to end (real SMTP configured against their own Gmail).

**Forgot-password UI, second pass.** The inline mini-form still showed the Password field and "Log in" button while in reset-request mode, which didn't make sense (you don't have a password to type if you're resetting it). `Login.tsx` now conditionally hides the Password field and Log in button entirely when `forgotOpen` is true -- only the Email field (reused, no separate input) plus Cancel/Send reset link show. `handleSubmit` was split so Enter-key and the submit button both route to whichever mode (login vs. forgot) is active, instead of always attempting a login. Also removed `AdminLogin`'s placeholder text (`name@company.com`, `••••••••`) so its inputs are blank like the regular login -- box sizing was already shared via the same `.login-field`/`.login-password-wrap` CSS classes, so no separate sizing fix was needed.

**Notification bell reposition, twice.** First pass pushed every role layout's main-content top padding from 32px to 64px so page headers would clear the bell -- user said no, revert, and instead nudge the specific colliding element (Users page's "Create User" button) left with `margin-right`. Reverted `ITAdminLayout.css`'s `.admin-main` back to 32px. Second pass: user asked to also bring the bell itself left ("inside the line", i.e. flush with the same right-padding boundary the page content already uses) and down a bit to roughly align with a header's button row. `NotificationBell.css`'s `.ntf-bell-container` moved from `top:16/right:20` to `top:28/right:40` (site-wide, since it's a single shared component); `UsersPage.css`'s `.usr-create-btn` margin-right bumped from 40px to 60px to keep a visible gap now that the bell sits closer.

**Real SMTP tested successfully** (user's own Gmail app password) -- forgot-password email actually arrived. User asked two follow-ups:
1. Whether one SMTP sender account can deliver to any recipient provider (Gmail from account -> Outlook/Yahoo/etc. recipients) without per-person configuration. Verified against actual code rather than taking the premise at face value: every `sendEmail()` call site (`interview.controller.ts`, `application.controller.ts`, `followUp.controller.ts`, `interviewReminders.ts`, `auth.controller.ts`) passes `to:` from that specific `candidate.email`/`user.email` DB field -- `SMTP_USER`/`MAIL_FROM` only ever populate `from`. Confirmed correct, with two caveats flagged that weren't in the user's original framing: Gmail's free SMTP has a ~500/day send cap, and mail from a personal Gmail claiming to be "Altrium Recruitment Tracker" is more spam-filter-prone than a real transactional provider (SendGrid/SES/Postmark) with your own domain -- fine for a student project, not what you'd want at real scale.
2. The reset email looked too plain (raw text, bare URL). `backend/src/utils/mailer.ts` now renders a proper branded HTML layout at send time (dark header bar with "Altrium Recruitment Tracker", body paragraphs, a bare-URL paragraph rendered as a gold pill-button instead of raw link text, footer note) and passes both `text` and `html` to `sendMail`. Deliberately a presentation-only wrapper -- the stored template text IT Admin edits on the Notification Templates screen is untouched, and the DEV EMAIL console fallback still prints the plain text as before. Existing `mailer.test.ts` assertions use `expect.objectContaining`, so the added `html` field doesn't break them (couldn't confirm by actually running vitest -- see the pre-existing rolldown `wasm32-wasi` sandbox limitation noted earlier in this log).

**Seed-account domain: `@altrium.test` -> `@altrium.com`.** User wanted the 7 canonical seed accounts (`hr@`, `interviewer@`, `management@`, `hiringmanager@`, `itadmin@`, `leadership@`, `disabled@`) to look like a real company's addresses for demos rather than the reserved `.test` TLD. Renamed across every file that references them: `backend/prisma/seed.ts`, `backend/scripts/{seed-hiring-manager-screens,seed-leadership-screens,seed-management-screens,seed-followups-screen,demo-new-features,add-staff,delete-staff,rename-staff}.ts`, `automated-tests/selenium/helpers.py`, `automated-tests/selenium/sprint1/test_login.py`, `automated-tests/selenium/sprint2/test_it_admin_users.py`, `automated-tests/selenium/README.md`, `docs/cloud-deployment-guide.md`, `regression-test.ps1`. Left this log's own historical entries on `@altrium.test` untouched -- narrative record of what was actually true at the time, not something to rewrite retroactively.

Flagged to the user: `.test` is one of a handful of TLDs (with `.example`, `.invalid`, `.localhost`) IANA reserves specifically so it can never resolve to a real mail server -- guaranteed-safe test data. `@altrium.com` is a real-looking domain that might actually be registered by someone; with real SMTP now configured, a demo send to one of these addresses that doesn't exist as an actual mailbox there would bounce back to the sender rather than misdeliver to a stranger, so the practical risk is low, but it's a real, deliberate tradeoff being made for a more convincing demo, not a fixed improvement.

**Critical follow-through this domain rename required:** `seed.ts` upserts by email, so just editing the domain there would NOT rename the 7 existing seeded users already in the user's real dev database on their machine -- it would create 7 brand-new duplicate rows on the new domain while the old `@altrium.test` rows (and everything already linked to their user IDs -- vacancies, applications, interviews, audit log entries) stayed put. Wrote `backend/scripts/rename-seed-domain.ts`, a one-off idempotent migration that updates the `email` column in place on the existing 7 rows by user ID, so all linked data carries over untouched. Passwords are unchanged (`password123` for all 7). Could not run this myself -- no DB access from this sandbox -- so the user needs to run `npx tsx scripts/rename-seed-domain.ts` from `backend/` themselves.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean after every change in this round. Backend `tsc`/`vitest` still can't run from this sandbox (same pre-existing issues); spot-checked `seed.ts` and the sed-renamed files by hand, and confirmed via `grep` that zero `@altrium.test` references remain outside this log.

## "Corrections to be made to the frontend.docx" -- full pass across HR, Interviewer, Hiring Manager, Management, Leadership

User uploaded a Word doc with ~24 correction bullets (plus 8 reference images) spanning nearly every role. Per "check this doc and ask any questions if needed," read the doc via `pandoc`, extracted and viewed the 8 embedded images, cross-checked the ambiguous bullets against actual code before asking anything, then ran two rounds of `AskUserQuestion` for the genuinely forked decisions (the rest were resolvable by reading the code). Answers locked in:

1. Interviewer **My Candidates** tab: keep the tab, but replace its grouped-by-vacancy-stage content with what used to live on **My Interviews** (the flat sortable table), plus a new vacancy filter. My Interviews itself becomes a calendar.
2. "THE MANAGEMENT HAS TO ATTEND THE LAST INTERVIEW": **enforce it**, not just context.
3. HM **Decision History** tab: **additive** to Pending Decisions, not a replacement.
4. HM "Proceed" button: confirmed via code read that it submitted and silently `navigate(-1)`'d with no feedback -- user wants that fixed.

Two things resolved without asking, flagged rather than guessed: "remove department option in create vacancy" reads as removing the redundant dropdown from the *create* flow only (the value is already implied by which department page you clicked "Create Vacancy" from) -- edit keeps it visible on *edit* in case HR needs to correct/move a vacancy. "Change stage label to day (or something)" -- the doc hedges on wording itself, went with "Day (optional)" for the free-text round-label field (its own placeholder examples read like day/session descriptors, not the required Stage dropdown).

**HR / Vacancies & Interviews (`frontend/src/pages/hr/{VacanciesPage,InterviewsPage}.tsx`):**
- Removed the "Interview Panel (optional)" section from the Create/Edit Vacancy modal entirely (`VacanciesPage.tsx`) -- confirmed via grep it was a straight duplicate of HR Interviews' own "Assign Interview Panel" modal, which does the identical `assignInterviewerToVacancy`/`listVacancyInterviewers` job properly. Removed the now-dead `panel`/`staffOptions`/`refreshPanel`/`handleAddPanelist`/`handleRemovePanelist` state and the `listAssignableStaff`/`roleLabel` imports that only existed for it.
- Department dropdown hidden on create (see above), kept on edit.
- Enter key in the "new round name" input now submits the stage add, same as clicking +.
- Added a close (X) button to the Create/Edit Vacancy modal, and to Schedule Interview / Add Candidate(s) in `InterviewsPage.tsx` (not Assign Panel -- the doc named those three specifically).
- `InterviewsPage.tsx`'s `AssignPanelModal`: "Staff" label -> "Interviewers"; empty-state copy updated to match.
- `ScheduleInterviewModal`: "Stage label (optional)" -> "Day (optional)"; native `<input type="time">` replaced with two selects (Hour: 9 AM-5 PM enumerated with correct AM/PM baked into each option so there's no invalid combination to construct; Minute: :00-:55 in 5-minute steps) that compose into the same 24h `HH:MM` string the save call already expected.
- **Backend, `interview.controller.ts`**: added a 9:00 AM-5:00 PM check to the shared `validatePanelistsAndConflict()` (used by both `scheduleInterview` and `createInterviewSlot`, so it can't be bypassed via the legacy single-candidate schedule path) -- server-side enforcement, not just the frontend dropdown.
- Status colors: `SHORTLISTED` was already green everywhere it's shown. `HIRED` had a defined-but-unused `.cnd-status-yellow` class in `CandidatesPage.css` -- `statusFor()` was falling through to the colorless "plain" badge for HIRED specifically. Wired it up.

**Shared: notification bell (`components/NotificationBell.{tsx,css}`).** Replaced the 🔔 emoji glyph (`filter: grayscale(1)`) with an inline SVG bell outline, `color: var(--altrium-black)`. Reasoning: the emoji's rendering (color, internal baseline/padding) varies by OS/font and never quite centers the same way in the circular button -- likely the real source of "not aligned properly," not just "not black and white." Left the bell's fixed `top:28/right:40` position untouched -- that was already tuned twice in the previous round against direct user screenshots, and nothing in this doc pointed at a *new* collision to fix.

**Sitewide: "Back" buttons bigger.** Found and bumped every literal back-navigation control: `.vac-back` (Vacancies), `.cnd-back-link`/`.vc-back-link`/`.myc-back-link` (breadcrumb-style text links, 13px -> 14.5px + padding for a bigger hit target), and the shared `.cnd-cancel-btn`/`.cd-cancel-btn` pill buttons (also used for "Cancel" in the same files, so both got bigger -- reasonable either way).

**Interviewer -- My Interviews / My Candidates rebuild (`frontend/src/pages/interviewer/`):**
- `MyInterviewsPage.tsx` rewritten from a flat filterable table into a month-grid calendar, structurally identical to HR's `InterviewsPage.tsx` pattern, scoped to `listMyInterviews()` (already server-side-scoped to the logged-in interviewer). Clicking a day opens a side panel listing that day's interviews; clicking a row still goes to the Feedback page.
- `MyCandidatesPage.tsx` rewritten to hold what used to be on My Interviews (the flat table, pending-first/submitted-last sort, All/Submitted/Pending filter) plus a new vacancy-filter dropdown built from whatever vacancies actually appear in the interviewer's own rows.
- `MyCandidatesGroupPage.tsx` (the old grouped-by-vacancy-stage drill-down) deleted -- its route and import removed from `App.tsx`. The backend endpoints it used (`/interviewer/vacancy-stages`, `/interviewer/candidates`) were left in place, unlinked, rather than deleted -- smaller blast radius, no other consumer.

**Hiring Manager:**
- `CandidateDecisionPage.tsx`: `handleAdvance`/`handleDoNotProgress`/`handleHire`/`handleReject` used to submit then immediately `navigate(-1)` with zero confirmation of the actual outcome. Now they set an on-page outcome message ("X was advanced to the next round." / "Recorded - X will not proceed." / "X was marked as Hired." / "...Rejected.") and swap in a single "Back to Candidates" button (routes explicitly to `/hiring-manager/vacancies/:vacancyId/candidates`, not history-back) instead of leaving silently. Same fix mirrored in `PendingDecisionsPage.tsx`'s modal flow, via a dismissible `Toast` after the modal closes, for consistency across both places that expose the same actions.
- **New "Decision History" tab**, additive to Pending Decisions. Backend: `getMyDecisionHistory` in `hiringManager.controller.ts` (route `GET /hiring-manager/decision-history`). Reads every application assigned to this HM that's `HIRED`, `REJECTED`, or `SHORTLISTED`-with-at-least-one-`ADVANCE`-recommendation-on-record. Bucketing logic worth documenting precisely, since it's not obvious from the schema alone: `HIRE`/`REJECT` (final-round decisions, via `recordHiringDecision`) write `hiringDecision`/`decidedAt` straight onto `CandidateApplication` and never create a `StageRecommendation` row; `ADVANCE`/`DO_NOT_PROGRESS` (non-final, via `submitStageRecommendation`) do the opposite, and `DO_NOT_PROGRESS` *also* sets `stage: REJECTED` -- meaning a bare `stage === "REJECTED"` check can't tell a final-round straight Reject apart from a mid-pipeline Do Not Proceed. The presence of a `DO_NOT_PROGRESS` `StageRecommendation` row is the only signal that distinguishes them, so that's what buckets an application as PROCEED (grouped with Do Not Proceed, per the doc's stated order: Hired, then Proceed/Do Not Proceed, then Rejected) instead of REJECTED. A hired candidate only ever appears in the Hired bucket even though they necessarily have earlier ADVANCE recommendations on record ("they have excelled from proceed and now in hired," per the doc) -- the bucket-by-application (not by decision-event) design handles that automatically. Frontend: `DecisionHistoryPage.tsx`, grouped tables per bucket, reusing the existing HIRED/green/amber/red badge palette.
- `CandidateComparisonPage.tsx`/`hiringManager.controller.ts` -- **re-scoped from an earlier wrong guess.** Initially assumed the doc's "KPI cards/charts background color," "don't show 9.0, just 9," and "top candidates bigger/green" bullets targeted Leadership (their images looked like generic style references at first glance). Grepped the actual panel titles ("Numeric Score Distribution", "Top Candidate Score Ranking") before touching anything and found they're literal screenshots of *this* page, not Leadership. Fixed: `SCORE_BUCKETS` labels in `hiringManager.controller.ts` changed from `"9.0-10"`/`"8.0-8.9"`/`"7.0-7.9"`/`"Below 7.0"` to `"9-10"`/`"8-8.9"`/`"7-7.9"`/`"Below 7"` (only the whole-number padding removed -- the genuinely fractional bounds like 8.9 stay); `.cc-kpi-tile`/`.cc-panel`/`.cc-comments-panel` given a soft background tint; `.cc-kpi-value` 28px->32px, `.cc-kpi-label` 12.5px->13.5px; ranking-list candidate names get a new `.cc-candidate-name-rank` modifier (15px, `#1e7a34` green) applied only in the ranking row, so the Comments panel's names (which share the base `.cc-candidate-name` class) are unaffected.
- `DashboardPage.tsx`/`.css` (HM Dashboard): removed the "Needs Attention" panel and its now-single-column `.hmd-lower-grid` wrapper (Recruitment Progress now spans full width alone); `data.attentionItems` is still returned by the backend but no longer rendered -- backend left as-is, smaller change. KPI/progress tiles given a `#faf5ea` background and bumped text sizes (44px->48px value, 12.5px->13.5px label), per "make the KPIs and dashboard like this."

**Management:**
- **Backend enforcement** of "Management must attend the final interview," confirmed as a hard rule via AskUserQuestion: `checkFinalRoundHasManagement()` in `interview.controller.ts`, called from both `scheduleInterview` and `createInterviewSlot` (same shared-function pattern as the conflict/9-5 checks) -- looks up the vacancy's actual last configured round, and if the round being scheduled is that one, requires at least one panelist with role `MANAGEMENT` or rejects with 400. Confirmed Management is already selectable as a panelist (`staff.controller.ts`'s `ASSIGNABLE_ROLES` already includes it), so the rule is satisfiable through the existing UI without further changes.
- `UpcomingInterviewsPage.tsx`/`.css` rebuilt from a flat filterable table into a month-grid calendar (same HR/Interviewer pattern), scoped by the existing `getUpcomingInterviews()` department scoping. The old date-range filter (7/30 days) is replaced by month navigation; the vacancy filter carries over and now applies live (no separate Apply button needed since there's no second filter to batch it with).
- **New "My Candidates" tab**, mirroring Interviewer's final design exactly (flat table + vacancy filter) per the doc's explicit "the same as interviewer tabs." New file `management/MyCandidatesPage.tsx` reuses `listMyInterviews()` as-is -- that endpoint was already role-agnostic server-side (scoped by panelist membership, not role), so no backend change was needed there. Rows link to `/management/interviews/:id/feedback`.
- **Backend permission gaps found and fixed while wiring this up** (would have silently 403'd or shown the wrong candidate's feedback as "already submitted" otherwise): `POST /interviews/:id/feedback` and `PATCH /feedback/:id` were `requireRole(Role.INTERVIEWER)`-only -- added `Role.MANAGEMENT`. `listFeedbackForInterview` only self-scoped the returned feedback list to `interviewerId = req.user.id` when the caller's role was `INTERVIEWER`; for every other role (including the newly-added Management) it returned the *entire panel's* feedback unfiltered, and `FeedbackPage.tsx`'s `fb[0] ?? null` "am I already on record" check would have picked up an arbitrary other panelist's entry. Extended the self-scoping condition to `INTERVIEWER || MANAGEMENT`.
- `frontend/src/pages/interviewer/FeedbackPage.tsx` made role-generic rather than duplicated: it was already 100% role-agnostic in its API calls, so the only hardcoded thing (the "My Interviews" breadcrumb link and the Back button, both pointed at `/interviewer/interviews`) now derive from `useLocation()` (`/management/...` -> back to `/management/candidates` labeled "My Candidates"; anything else -> the original Interviewer behavior). Reused directly under a new `/management/interviews/:id/feedback` route rather than forking a near-duplicate file.

**Leadership:** removed the "Recruitment Progress" page entirely -- nav item, route, `RecruitmentProgressPage.tsx`/`.css` deleted. Its backend endpoint (`/leadership/recruitment-progress`) left in place, unlinked.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean after every single edit in this pass (checked incrementally, not just at the end). Backend `tsc` still can't run from this sandbox (same pre-existing `@typescript/typescript-linux-x64` resolution failure from the user's own `npm install` on Windows, disclosed repeatedly earlier in this log) -- every backend change in this round was instead re-read by hand afterward specifically checking brace balance, null-safety on values guarded earlier in the same function, and Prisma relation/field names cross-checked directly against `schema.prisma` rather than assumed.

## Seed data reset: patched 4 scripts for the Management-final-round rule

Flagged proactively after the Management enforcement rule above shipped: `checkFinalRoundHasManagement()` is enforced by the API (`scheduleInterview`/`createInterviewSlot`), but every seed/demo script writes `interviewSlot`/`interviewPanelist` rows directly via Prisma, bypassing that check entirely -- so the rule doesn't actually *break* re-running old seed scripts. The real problem is narrower but still real: no seed script ever put `management@altrium.com` on an interview panel, so (a) the brand-new Management "My Candidates" tab (`listMyInterviews()`, scoped by panelist membership) would demo empty, and (b) any final-round interview in the seed data visibly contradicts a rule the demo is meant to show off. User confirmed: wipe and regenerate.

**Deletion:** `backend/scripts/wipe-candidates.ts` already existed and is already correct for this -- deletes every vacancy/candidate/application/interview/slot/panelist/feedback/stage-history/recommendation row in FK-safe order plus uploaded CV files, leaves all `User` rows (including the 7 canonical logins) untouched. No changes needed there.

**Regeneration -- patched 4 of the 5 panelist-creating scripts** to add `management@altrium.com` as a panelist on the one interview each script uses as its actual "final round" demo (found by tracing each script's `VacancyStage` orders and matching the interview against the vacancy's highest `order`):
- `seed-hiring-manager-screens.ts` -- Devon Walsh's stage-3 "Final Interview" (Backend Engineer, 3-stage vacancy). Added a `mgmt` lookup (same guarded pattern as the existing `hm` lookup) and `{ userId: mgmt.id, score: 9, comments: ... }` to the panelist array.
- `seed-management-screens.ts` -- Olga Petrova's stage-2 "Final Interview" (DevOps Engineer). This script already had `mgmt` in scope (the seeded Management user's own record) -- just added `{ userId: mgmt.id, score: -1, comments: "" }` to the existing panelist array. This is the main demo of the rule and of Management's own My Candidates tab, since Management is now literally on their own panel.
- `seed-leadership-screens.ts` -- Elise Fontaine's stage-2 "Final Interview" (Platform Engineer). Added a guarded `mgmt` lookup + panelist entry, same pattern.
- `seed-followups-screen.ts` -- Mateo Reyes's stage-2 "Final Interview" (Customer Success Associate, the "Calls" demo candidate). Added a guarded `mgmt` lookup + panelist entry. Note: this vacancy's department is "Customer Service" while `management@altrium.com`'s seeded department is "Engineering" -- a minor narrative mismatch, accepted since no screen in this codebase filters interview panel membership by department, and the alternative (skipping this one) would mean one of the four "Final Interview"-labeled rounds stays inconsistent with the rule for no real benefit.

**Deliberately left untouched** (judgment call, flagging rather than silently deciding): `demo-new-features.ts`'s single-stage "SCRUM2 Demo Role" vacancy, and the single-stage "Product Designer" / "Support Specialist" vacancies inside `seed-hiring-manager-screens.ts` (used for the Candidate Comparison ranking screen, 8 candidates total). Under `checkFinalRoundHasManagement`'s actual logic, a vacancy's *only* stage is technically its "final round" too, so these are strictly rule-inconsistent as seeded. Not fixed because: `demo-new-features.ts` exists purely to demo the notification/reminder job, unrelated to Management or panels; the Comparison-page candidates are deliberately still mid-round (not decided yet) and adding Management to 8 separate candidate panels would be a lot of unrelated churn for a screen whose entire point is comparing Interviewer scores, not demonstrating the Management rule. If this inconsistency comes up in a demo, the honest answer is "single-round pipelines were out of scope for this fix."

**Run order given to the user** (sandbox has no DB access, so these must be run by the user from the `backend` folder): `npx tsx scripts/wipe-candidates.ts`, then in any order -- `seed-hr-department-vacancies.ts`, `seed-hiring-manager-screens.ts`, `seed-management-screens.ts`, `seed-leadership-screens.ts`, `seed-followups-screen.ts` -- then optionally `demo-new-features.ts` (notification/reminder demo only, independent of the above). All five are idempotent find-or-create, so re-running any of them again later is safe.

**Verification:** all 4 edited scripts hand-reviewed for brace balance and correct Prisma field usage (backend `tsc` still broken in this sandbox, same pre-existing issue). Not run against a real database -- no DB access from this sandbox; the user runs the commands above and confirms the result.

## Fourth follow-up correction pass (feedback submit UX: redirect + toast, not inline message)

`FeedbackPage.tsx` used to stay on the page after a successful submit/edit, showing an inline "Feedback saved." paragraph next to the form (score/comments/reason inputs still visible, "Save Changes" button unchanged). Follow-up correction: a successful submit should leave the page entirely -- back to My Candidates -- with a one-shot popup confirming what happened, distinct wording for first-time submit vs. a later edit.

- `handleSubmit()`: on success, `navigate(backTo, { state: { toast: "..." } })` instead of `setSaved(true)` + reloading the form in place. Message is `"Feedback submitted successfully."` for a first-time submit, `"Feedback updated successfully."` when editing (`isEditMode`, captured from `existing` at the point of submission, before navigating away) -- so the wording matches "submit once, later re-open the same candidate and edit" exactly as described. Removed the now-dead `saved` state and the inline `.fb-success` message.
- Both `interviewer/MyCandidatesPage.tsx` and `management/MyCandidatesPage.tsx` (Feedback's only two entry points, per the previous pass) now read a one-shot `toast` off `location.state` on mount, show it via the shared `Toast` component (dismissible, 6s), and immediately clear the state with a `replace` navigate so a refresh or browser-back doesn't re-show a stale popup. Same small pattern duplicated in both files rather than extracted into a hook -- these two pages are otherwise near-identical already (an existing, accepted duplication in this codebase, not new).
- Table row still shows the interview's list badge (Pending/Submitted) -- since navigating back to My Candidates remounts it, `refresh()` re-fires on mount and the badge reflects the just-submitted feedback immediately.

## Eighth follow-up correction pass (font legibility, 5-way score buckets, Pending Decisions delay)

- **Candidate Comparison legibility, second pass:** `.cc-kpi-value` (Top Candidates / Average Numeric Score / Highest Numeric Score tile numbers) 32px -> 36px. `.cc-candidate-name-rank` (#1-ranked candidate's name, green/bold) 16px -> 19px. Same file/classes as the previous legibility pass, just pushed further per follow-up feedback that the first bump wasn't quite enough.
- **Score Distribution buckets split further:** the previous pass fixed the label text but still grouped 9 and 10 into one `"9-10"` bucket. `SCORE_BUCKETS` in `hiringManager.controller.ts` now has 5 buckets instead of 4 -- `"10"`, `"9"`, `"8"`, `"7"`, `"Below 7"` -- each an exact-equality bound (`min === max`) rather than a range, since `isValidScore` only ever allows whole integers 1-10 so there's nothing fractional to bucket. Frontend (`CandidateComparisonPage.tsx`) needed no changes -- the distribution list and bar-width calculation already map over whatever the backend returns.
- **Pending Decisions load delay, investigated and partially fixed:** asked "why does Hire/Reject/Proceed/Do Not Proceed take a moment to load" (flagged as minor, not necessarily wanting a fix). Two distinct causes found:
  1. **Hire/Reject specifically** -- `recordHiringDecision` (`application.controller.ts`) was awaiting the full notification-email pipeline (`renderTemplate` -> `sendEmail` -> `writeAuditLog`) *before* calling `res.json(application)`, so the HTTP response sat blocked on mail delivery every time. Fixed: moved `res.json(application)` up, then fire the email/audit-log block without awaiting it (still wrapped in the same try/catch, still logs failures) -- response now returns as soon as the decision itself is recorded, email sends in the background same as before functionally, just no longer on the request's critical path.
  2. **All four actions** -- `PendingDecisionsPage.tsx`'s handlers call the action endpoint, then `await refresh()`, which is a second full round-trip re-fetching the entire pending-decisions list. That's inherent to the current confirm-then-refetch UX pattern (not a bug, just two sequential network calls instead of one) and applies equally to Proceed/Do Not Proceed, which have no email step at all -- left as-is since the user flagged this as low-priority and an optimistic-update rewrite is a more invasive change than the ask called for.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean. Backend changes (`SCORE_BUCKETS` 5-way split, `recordHiringDecision` fire-and-forget email) hand-reviewed -- backend `tsc` still unusable in this sandbox, same pre-existing issue.

## Thirteenth follow-up correction pass (Follow Ups is an HR-only concept; Management's tab removed)

Architectural realization from the user, applied across the system: "Follow Ups" as a screen belongs to the role doing the chasing, not the roles being chased. HR's Follow Ups page is literally HR reaching out to interviewers/Hiring Managers/Management ("Send Email" reminders for pending feedback, pending invites, etc.) -- it was never meant to be a pattern every role gets its own copy of. Management's only real responsibility is attending the final round and submitting their own score/comments; that's a personal obligation, not something to "follow up" on in the HR sense.

- **Management's Follow Ups tab removed outright** -- not reduced, not renamed, gone. Deleted `management/FollowUpsPage.tsx`/`.css`, the nav item, the `/management/follow-ups` route (`App.tsx`), the `getManagementFollowUps` handler and route (`management.controller.ts`/`management.routes.ts`), and the now-unused `ManagementAttentionItem`/`ManagementFollowUps` types and `getManagementFollowUps` fetch function (`api/management.ts`).
- **No replacement tab built, deliberately.** Checked `MyCandidatesPage.tsx` (Management's existing tab) before deciding: it already has an All/Submitted/Pending filter, sorts pending-first, and every row is clickable straight to the Feedback form -- that already *is* Management's personal to-do for their one actual responsibility. A second tab (whether called "Follow Ups" or "To Do") would have just duplicated it under a different name. "My Interviews" (the calendar) already covers the other half -- knowing when to show up. Between the two existing tabs, nothing was missing.
- **The org-wide stats that used to be Management's Follow Ups content** (interviewers with overdue feedback anywhere, vacancies open too long) aren't surfaced anywhere in Management's UI now, on the Dashboard or otherwise -- recommended and applied the same reasoning just used for Leadership: if a role can't act on a stat, it doesn't need to watch it, and HR's own Follow Ups page already tracks and lets HR act on exactly this (its "Pending Feedback" section already covers every interviewer/role, not just Management's).
- `buildDashboardData` in `management.controller.ts` still computes `attentionItems` internally (just never returned by any endpoint now) -- left in place rather than stripped, same "don't rip out the data layer" precaution taken with Leadership, in case this needs revisiting.
- Net result: **Follow Ups now exists only on HR.** Confirmed via grep across `frontend/src` -- no remaining `follow-ups` route or `FollowUpsPage` reference outside `pages/hr/`.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean; grepped `pages/management/` for leftover `FollowUps`/`follow-ups` references (none). Backend changes hand-reviewed -- backend `tsc` still unusable in this sandbox, same pre-existing issue.

## Fourteenth pass: HM Dashboard filters + corrected "one HM for the company" premise + Leadership 403 diagnosis

- **Corrected a stated premise, not applied as-is.** User's request was framed as "we decided there's one hiring manager in charge of the whole company." No such decision exists in this log -- the opposite is explicitly documented (see the "Hiring Manager displayed as a vacancy-level attribute" entry): `hiringManagerId` lives on `CandidateApplication`, not `Vacancy`, *because* one vacancy can have different HMs across different candidates. `CreateUserPage.tsx`'s `ROLES` list also confirms Hiring Manager is an ordinary, freely-creatable role, not a singleton. Filters were still added, but for a different, verifiable reason below -- not the stated one.
- **HM Dashboard (`getMyDashboard`) now takes optional `dateRange` / `vacancyId` / `department` query filters**, same shape as Management's Dashboard filters. Justification: `VacanciesPage.tsx` (HM) already has a working Department filter, and `seed-hiring-manager-screens.ts` gives one HM account vacancies across Engineering/Data/Design/Support simultaneously -- a single HM's own assigned vacancies can legitimately span multiple departments, unlike Management (locked to one department, no Department filter offered there on purpose -- see that file's comment).
- Filtering is server-side (mirrors Management's pattern, not VacanciesPage's client-side pattern) because `getMyDashboard` returns pre-aggregated counts (readiness gates, missing-feedback nudges, compare-candidate nudges) built from business logic that isn't safe to re-derive in the browser. `getMyVacancyIds` now takes the filters and joins through the `vacancy` relation; `myApplicationsFull` and `getPendingDecisionsForHm` are now also scoped to the filtered vacancy set, so a Department filter narrows every number on the page, not just the vacancy-count tile.
- Frontend: `DashboardPage.tsx` (HM) now has a filter bar identical in structure/CSS to `VacanciesPage.tsx`'s (`hmd-filter-bar`/`hmd-filter-field`/`hmd-apply-btn`, mirroring `hmv-*`), fetching `getMyVacancies()` once for the Vacancy/Department option lists.
- **Leadership Department Performance 403, diagnosed (not a code bug):** `requireRole` is applied identically and only at the router level for every leadership route (`dashboard`, `department-performance`, `hiring-trends`, etc.) -- no route-specific auth difference exists, ruled out by reading `leadership.routes.ts` and `getDepartmentPerformance`'s controller body. The JWT's `role` claim is baked in at login (`jwt.ts`) and never re-checked against the DB per request, and the token lives in `localStorage`, which is shared across every tab on the same origin. Recruitment Overview succeeding while Department Performance 403s in the same browser session is consistent with a second login (e.g. testing Hiring Manager or Management screens in another tab, per this session's dummy-data request) silently overwriting the shared `token` entry with a different role's JWT. Told the user to re-log-in as `leadership@altrium.com` in whichever tab tests Leadership screens, and to avoid keeping multiple role logins open across tabs of the same browser (use separate browser profiles/incognito windows instead) when checking several roles side by side.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean. Backend changes hand-reviewed (checked all other `getMyVacancyIds`/`getPendingDecisionsForHm` call sites -- both new params are optional, no other caller needed changes) -- backend `tsc` still unusable in this sandbox, same pre-existing issue.

## Fifteenth pass: login crash bug fix ("Failed to execute 'json' on 'Response'")

- **Root cause, found via code inspection (not guessed):** `backend/src/controllers/auth.controller.ts` was the one controller file in the entire codebase with zero `try/catch` around its DB calls -- every other controller (management, leadership, hiring manager, etc.) wraps its Prisma calls and returns a proper JSON 500 on failure; this file didn't. There's also no global Express error-handling middleware in `app.ts`, so nothing catches an unhandled rejection from a route handler. If `prisma.user.findUnique`/`bcrypt.compare` ever threw during login (DB hiccup, connection-pool blip, dev-server restart mid-request), the connection could be torn down with no response body. On the frontend, `api/auth.ts`'s `postLogin` did a bare `res.json()` with no `.catch()` guard -- the one raw-fetch spot in the codebase without one (every other one already has `.catch(() => null)`, including the shared `apiFetch`). An empty body there throws the browser's own `"Failed to execute 'json' on 'Response': Unexpected end of JSON input"`, which bubbled straight up onto the login screen unformatted.
- **Fix:** wrapped `login`, `adminLogin`, `verifyPassword`, `forgotPassword`, `resetPassword` in `auth.controller.ts` in try/catch, each returning a proper `{ error }` JSON body with a 500 on failure -- consistent with every other controller. Hardened `postLogin` in `frontend/src/api/auth.ts` to guard the response parse the same way `apiFetch` already does, with a clean fallback message ("Could not reach the server. Please try again.") instead of ever surfacing a raw browser exception.
- **Residual risk, disclosed, not fixed:** there is still no global Express error-handling middleware in `app.ts`. Every controller now happens to try/catch its own DB calls (auth.controller.ts was the only gap, now closed), but any *future* route added without one would reproduce the same failure class. Not fixed in this pass since it's a larger structural change (would need a catch-all middleware plus auditing every route) and wasn't what broke -- flagged here for awareness before the viva, not treated as blocking.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean. Backend changes hand-reviewed (plain try/catch additions, no signature/type changes) -- backend `tsc` still unusable in this sandbox, same pre-existing issue. Requires a backend dev-server restart to take effect (not hot-reloaded automatically depending on the user's setup).

## Sixteenth pass: real backend crash fix + global error handler added

- **Real bug, my own regression:** the earlier `getPendingDecisionsForHm` edit (fifteenth/fourteenth pass, adding an optional `vacancyIds` filter param) collided with an unrelated pre-existing local `const vacancyIds` declared later in the same function body (used to resolve `isFinalRound` across returned rows, nothing to do with filtering). This is a genuine duplicate-identifier compile error -- `tsx watch` refused to start the backend at all (`esbuild` "already been declared"), which is why login kept failing after reseeding: the server process wasn't running, not a credentials/DB issue. Fixed by renaming the local variable to `resultVacancyIds`. Could not verify with this sandbox's `tsc`/`esbuild` (both are Windows-native binaries here, pre-existing sandbox limitation) -- caught by manual scope inspection instead, user confirmed by restarting `npm run dev` on their end.
- **Global Express error handler added** (`app.ts`, registered last, after the static-frontend fallback). User asked "should I add it" after the above crash -- recommended yes: every controller already try/catches its own DB calls (the `auth.controller.ts` gap from the previous pass being the one exception, already fixed), but that's a per-file discipline, not a structural guarantee. A catch-all is cheap (5 lines, no Prisma/DB coupling) and converts any future forgotten `try/catch` into a clean JSON 500 instead of a dropped connection -- exactly the failure class that produced the original login-screen crash. Standard 4-arg Express error-handler signature (`err, req, res, next`), guards `res.headersSent` before writing.

**Verification:** could not run backend `tsc`/`esbuild` in this sandbox (Windows-native binaries mounted, pre-existing issue disclosed all session) -- both changes hand-reviewed for scope/type correctness. Frontend unaffected, `tsc --noEmit` not re-run (no frontend files touched this pass). User must restart `npm run dev` for both fixes to take effect.

## Eighteenth pass: auth switched from localStorage to sessionStorage (fixes recurring cross-tab 403)

- **Same bug as the Leadership 403, now confirmed structural, not a one-off.** The Hiring Manager Dashboard threw the identical "You do not have permission to do this" while the sidebar/title still correctly said "Hiring Manager Dashboard" -- proof this wasn't a role-gate bug (identical symptom pattern to the earlier Leadership diagnosis, now reproduced on a second role). Root cause fully traced this time: `AuthContext.tsx` read `token`/`user` from `localStorage` exactly once on mount into React state, never re-synced afterward. `localStorage` is shared across every tab on the same origin, so logging into a second role in another tab silently overwrote the token everyone else was using. The affected tab's UI kept rendering its stale in-memory role (hence the correct-looking page title/nav), but every API call re-reads the token fresh from storage on each request (`api/client.ts`, plus 4 other files reading it directly) -- so it silently sent the *new* role's token, and `requireRole` correctly 403'd it for not being the role the page was built for.
- **Fixed at the source instead of re-diagnosing per-page:** switched every read/write of the auth token and user object from `localStorage` to `sessionStorage` across 6 files (`AuthContext.tsx`, `api/client.ts`, `api/candidates.ts` x2, `api/vacancy.ts`, `api/management.ts`, `api/leadership.ts`). `sessionStorage` is scoped per-tab, not per-origin -- a new tab gets its own empty copy, so logging into a different role in one tab no longer touches any other tab's session at all. This directly supports the actual workflow this project needs (multiple roles open and usable simultaneously across tabs for testing/demo), which `localStorage` structurally cannot do no matter how carefully each page guards against it.
- **Trade-off, accepted:** a login no longer survives fully closing and reopening the browser (`sessionStorage` clears then, `localStorage` wouldn't have) -- normal "log in again" behavior, not a concern for this project.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean. Grepped for remaining `localStorage` references in `frontend/src` -- only the two explanatory comments left, no functional reads/writes remain.

## Nineteenth pass: full seed-data reset (`seed-full-demo.ts`) + department taxonomy unified

User asked (after approving the plan via AskUserQuestion: keep the 7 login accounts as-is, use realistic/professional naming) to wipe all recruitment data and rebuild it so every page across every role has real content, before moving on to automated+manual testing and cloud deployment.

- **New consolidated script:** `backend/scripts/seed-full-demo.ts`. Reuses the proven find-or-create helper shape from the existing seed scripts (ensureUser/ensureVacancy/ensureStage/ensureCandidate/ensureApplication/ensureInterviewAt), generalized into a single `seedVacancyPipeline()` builder applied to a data-driven `VACANCY_SPECS` array instead of hand-writing each vacancy's candidates individually.
- **Department taxonomy unified onto HR's 8 canonical departments** (HR, Finance and Accounting, Operations, Marketing, Sales, IT, Customer Service, Legal) -- fixes the pre-existing split flagged in the "Seventeenth pass" (Management/HM/Leadership's old seed data used a disconnected freeform set: Engineering/Data/Design/Support). `backend/prisma/seed.ts`'s `management@altrium.com`/`interviewer@altrium.com`/`hiringmanager@altrium.com` department fields moved from "Engineering" to "IT". Also fixed a real bug in that same file while touching it: the upsert's `update: {}` meant rerunning `npx prisma db seed` never actually updated an existing account -- now syncs name/department/isActive on every rerun (still never touches passwordHash, so it can't silently undo a real password reset).
- **16 vacancies, 2 per department**, spanning On Track/Delayed/Overdue fill status. Every vacancy gets the same archetype spread via `seedVacancyPipeline`: fresh-applied (Pending CV Review), mid-round-not-ready, partial-feedback (Pending Feedback), ready-for-decision, final-round-ready (multi-stage vacancies only, includes Management + HM as panelists when the vacancy is in Management's own department), hired, and rejected. "Backend Engineer" (IT) and "Content Marketing Specialist" (Marketing) get 4 extra scored candidates each for Candidate Comparison. "Support Team Lead" (Customer Service) is fully decided (allDecided). IT additionally gets a "final-round-ready but Management's own feedback still pending" candidate, so Management's My Candidates tab has both a Pending and a Submitted row to show.
- **Every real login account is an actual participant, not just a viewer:** `hiringmanager@altrium.com` is the hiring manager on all 16 vacancies (matches the existing "one HM's vacancies can span multiple departments" decision); `interviewer@altrium.com` panelists across every department (previous scripts sometimes only used throwaway extra interviewer accounts, leaving the real login's own pages empty); `management@altrium.com` panelists on IT's final rounds specifically, since Management's Dashboard is scoped to exactly one department.
- **IT Admin's Audit Logs and everyone's notification bell** were empty after every previous seed script, since seeding writes directly via Prisma and bypasses the controllers that normally call `writeAuditLog()`/create a `Notification` as a side effect of a real action. Added one `AuditLog` row per the 10 wired event types and two `Notification` rows (one read, one unread) per real staff account. Not idempotent (no find-or-create guard) -- disclosed in-file, harmless if the script is rerun, just adds another copy.
- **Superseded scripts marked, not deleted:** `seed-hiring-manager-screens.ts`, `seed-leadership-screens.ts`, `seed-management-screens.ts`, `seed-hr-department-vacancies.ts`, `seed-followups-screen.ts` now each have a header notice pointing to `seed-full-demo.ts` and warning they'll reintroduce a non-canonical department if run afterward. `demo-new-features.ts` kept (still useful for exercising the reminder cron job) with a similar caveat on the one vacancy it creates. `seed-notification-templates.ts` untouched -- unrelated, seeds `NotificationTemplate` rows, not recruitment data.
- **Run order given to the user** (this sandbox has no DB access, so the user runs these themselves): `npx tsx scripts/wipe-candidates.ts` -> `npx prisma db seed` -> `npx tsx scripts/seed-full-demo.ts`, all from `backend/`.

**Verification:** could not run backend `tsc`/`esbuild` in this sandbox (Windows-native binaries mounted, pre-existing issue disclosed all session). Hand-reviewed carefully against `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`/`exactOptionalPropertyTypes` (both on in `backend/tsconfig.json`) since a real regression from missing this exact check broke the backend earlier this session (the `vacancyIds` collision, "Sixteenth pass") -- every array/record index access in the new script (`stages[0]`, `SECOND_PANELIST[dept]`, `NAME_POOL[i]`, `scores[i]`, `outcomes[i]`) now has an explicit non-null assertion, each one verified safe by construction (bounds always satisfied given how the loops are built). User must run this themselves and report any actual compiler/runtime error, since it's unverified against the real compiler.

## Twentieth pass: fixed Leadership "Best Fill Rate" showing 0% for every department

User asked what the "Legal"/"Customer Service" sub-labels on Department Performance meant (departments, correctly answered) -- while checking that, re-read `buildDepartmentPerformanceData` in `leadership.controller.ts` and confirmed `fillRate = closedVacancies / totalVacancies`, and that `seed-full-demo.ts` (Nineteenth pass) created every one of its 16 vacancies as `OPEN`. That's a real, self-inflicted data gap, not a UI or backend logic bug -- with every vacancy open, every department's fill rate was 0%, and "Best Fill Rate" picked an arbitrary winner among a field of ties.

Gives a realistic spread (Sales 100%, four departments at 50%, three departments still at 0%) instead of a flat tie.

- Idempotent to rerun -- `closeVacancy` looks the vacancy up by `title_department` and updates its status; no need to re-wipe first.

## Twenty-first pass: corrections-doc batch (8 items across HR, Hiring Manager, Management)

User uploaded a `Corrections.docx` with a flat list of UI/bug notes (some with screenshots) spanning HR Vacancies/Interviews, Hiring Manager, and Management. Extracted via `pandoc` + unzipping the docx for its 3 embedded screenshots (viewed directly to identify the exact screens referenced, since the text alone was ambiguous on a couple of items).

- **HR Interviews day-popup (the "+" menu with Assign Interview Panel / Schedule Interview / Add Candidate(s)) had no visible close affordance.** Clicking the backdrop already dismissed it, but that wasn't obvious from the menu alone. Added an explicit `&#10005;` close button (`ivw-plus-menu-close`, reusing the existing `.ivw-modal-close` styling), and gave `.ivw-plus-menu` `position: relative` + top padding so the absolutely-positioned X lands inside the small card instead of the full-screen backdrop.
- **Edit Vacancy panel still showed the Department dropdown** after it was removed from Create Vacancy (Sprint-era correction). Removed it from Edit too -- `form.department` is still set under the hood from `openEditForm`, so validation/save are unaffected, there's just no UI to change it (a vacancy's department shouldn't change post-creation).
- **The "&larr; Departments" back link (HR Vacancies, department drill-down) was small, easy to miss.** Bumped `.vac-back` from 14.5px/500 weight to 17px/600 weight, matching the "make back buttons bigger sitewide" precedent from earlier in the project.
- **Candidate Comparison's "Top Candidate Score Ranking" only ever showed 2 candidates, not 5.** `TOP_N` in `hiringManager.controller.ts` was already 5 and the frontend renders every row it's given -- not a code bug. The real cause: `comparisonBoost` (which adds 4 extra scored candidates) was only set on 2 of the 16 seeded vacancies (Marketing, IT), so every other department's flagship vacancy only had the 1-2 candidates that naturally stay `SHORTLISTED`-with-feedback out of the base archetypes. Added `comparisonBoost: true` to one vacancy per department in `seed-full-demo.ts` (HR Business Partner, Senior Financial Analyst, Operations Manager, Account Executive, Customer Success Manager, Corporate Counsel, on top of the existing Content Marketing Specialist and Backend Engineer) so every department has at least one vacancy that genuinely ranks 5.
- **"Hiring Manager doesn't have any interviews" -- root-caused, not just patched.** `listMyInterviews` is scoped strictly by `InterviewPanelist` membership. `hm.id` (the seeded Hiring Manager account) was only ever added as a panelist on a final round when `mgmt` was truthy, and `mgmt` is only non-null for IT (`mgmtForThisVacancy` in `main()`) -- so the HM was a panelist on exactly 2 of 16 vacancies. There's no actual business rule requiring an HM to attend any interview (checked `interview.controller.ts` -- `checkFinalRoundHasManagement` only cares about the `MANAGEMENT` role); this was purely a seed-data gap. `hm.id` is now added to every multi-stage vacancy's final-round panel unconditionally, so My Interviews has real entries across all 8 departments after a reseed.
- **"Can't see the Proceed option" -- investigated, not reproduced.** Read through the full chain (`VacancyCandidatesPage` -> `CandidateDecisionPage`, and `PendingDecisionsPage`, both frontend and their backend `isFinalRound`/`awaitingDecision` logic in `hiringManager.controller.ts`). Proceed/Do Not Proceed only renders when `awaitingDecision` is true and `isFinalRound` is false -- correct and consistent everywhere it's used; single-stage vacancies and genuinely-final rounds correctly show Hire/Reject instead, which is by design, not a bug. Could not find a code path that would hide Proceed for a real non-final, ready-for-decision candidate. Left open -- flagged to the user to ask for a screenshot or the exact candidate/vacancy next time this comes up, rather than guessing at a fix for a bug that couldn't be located.
- **HM Decision History: added candidate-profile viewing, deliberately did NOT add Proceed/Do Not Proceed.** Rows now navigate to the existing `CandidateDecisionPage`, which already renders correctly read-only for a decided application (shows the "already hired/rejected" banner or historical round, plus full feedback history, no action buttons since `awaitingDecision` is false). Chose not to add decision actions to this page: letting someone re-decide an already-made, logged decision from a history view (with no undo) would undermine the point of keeping a decision log -- if a decision needs revisiting, that should go through Pending Decisions' normal flow.
- **Combined Management's "My Candidates" and "Candidate Progress" into one page.** These were two different datasets (My Candidates = interviews where Management is personally a panelist and owes feedback; Candidate Progress = a read-only, department-wide oversight table), so this merges them as two sections on one page rather than picking one. Kept the route at `/management/candidate-progress` and the `.cp-section-title` selector specifically because `automated-tests/selenium/sprint2/test_management_reports.py` navigates there and asserts on that selector -- `/management/candidates` now redirects to it instead of being removed outright. Old `MyCandidatesPage.tsx` left in place with a "SUPERSEDED" header comment rather than deleted, matching this project's existing convention for retired seed scripts.

All 5 screenshots turned out to be either an already-hired read-only view (no action buttons at all, not evidence of the bug) or a candidate who had already walked every round including the final one (correctly final, correctly Hire/Reject). Working theory, not confirmed: the user has likely already actioned every round-1 "Proceed-eligible" candidate during earlier testing sessions, so what's left in Pending Decisions right now skews toward final-round items -- `seed-full-demo.ts` alone won't undo an already-advanced application (it's find-or-create), so only a full wipe (`wipe-candidates.ts` -> `prisma db seed` -> `seed-full-demo.ts`) would produce a guaranteed-fresh non-final example to check against. Also cleaned up `interviewer/FeedbackPage.tsx` (shared by Interviewer/Management/HM), which still had an `isHiringManager` branch computing a `backTo`/`backLabel` for a route that no longer exists anywhere -- removed, now just Management vs. Interviewer.

Removed that dependency entirely: `comparisonBoost: true` is now on all 15 non-`allDecided` vacancies (only `Support Team Lead` is deliberately excluded -- `allDecided` vacancies return out of `seedVacancyPipeline` before ever reaching the comparisonBoost block, since every candidate is already HIRED/REJECTED and none stay `SHORTLISTED`, so boosting it would be a silent no-op). Every open vacancy's Candidate Comparison page now genuinely has 5+ scored candidates, regardless of which one is opened.

Added `display: flex; align-items: center; justify-content: center; padding: 0;` -- fixes every close button on this page, not just the one that was screenshotted.

Every row now has a real word explaining its state instead of a dash.

## Twenty-fifth pass: Action column styling/ordering; hired/rejected candidates seeded with real feedback

- **Action column text now matches "Awaiting your decision"'s size/weight, colored by urgency.** "Done" and "Waiting on panel feedback" were plain `.vc-muted` (smaller, unstyled gray) from the Twenty-fourth pass. Added `.vc-action-done` (same 12px/600 as `.vc-awaiting`, green `#1e7a34`) and `.vc-action-waiting` (same size/weight, gray `var(--altrium-ink-soft)`) -- color is now the only thing that differs between the three states, not size.
- **Rows now sort by urgency:** awaiting-your-decision first, then still-waiting (panel feedback or not yet interviewed), done (hired/rejected) last. `actionFor()` returns a `priority` alongside its label/class; a `useMemo`'d stable sort orders `rows` by it before rendering, so ties keep the API's original order.
- **Root-caused and fixed "the panel feedback is not there" on an already-hired candidate's Panel Feedback page.** Archetypes 6 ("Already hired") and 7 ("Already rejected") in `seed-full-demo.ts` never had any interview/feedback records at all -- realistic recruiting doesn't hire or reject someone with zero recorded feedback. Both now walk real interview rounds: the hired archetype mirrors archetype 5's per-round shape ending in strong, unanimous final-round scores (interviewer/panelist2/mgmt when applicable); the rejected archetype gets one round of below-bar, mixed scores at the round they were rejected on. Also applied the same fix to the `allDecided` branch (Support Team Lead's 3 HIRE/REJECT/REJECT candidates), which had the identical gap.
- **Removed the "No panel feedback recorded yet." fallback from `CandidateDecisionPage.tsx`.** With hired/rejected candidates now always seeded with real feedback, this only fires for a genuinely feedback-less mid-round candidate -- and the warning banner already above it ("Waiting on the panel's feedback for X...") already covers that case on its own, so the line was redundant rather than informative.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` clean (exit 0) after the `VacancyCandidatesPage.tsx`/`.css` and `CandidateDecisionPage.tsx` edits.
