## Twenty-sixth pass: CV-review handoff indicator on Candidates list; status ordering; Reject/Shortlist restyled

Surfaced from the Sprint 1 doc audit's US-15 ("last reviewed CV") discussion: `getCandidate` sets `lastCvReviewedByUserId`/`lastCvReviewedAt` on every view of the HR Candidate Detail page (not gated behind a shortlist/reject decision -- see the schema comment and the `Pending CV Review` note below it). The Candidates *list* page never surfaced this at all, even though `listCandidates` already fetched it (`candidate: { include: { lastCvReviewedBy: true } }`). User wants a real handoff workflow: if an HR officer is mid-review and has to step away, the next HR officer opening the list should see which CV a colleague was just looking at, without opening candidates one at a time.

- **`CandidateSummary` type (`frontend/src/api/candidates.ts`) gained `lastCvReviewedAt`/`lastCvReviewedBy`.** No backend change -- `listCandidates` already returns these on every `candidate` object via the existing `include`, the frontend type just hadn't declared them (they were previously only declared on `CandidateDetail`, which already got the identical data from the same backend field).
- **`CandidatesPage.tsx` sort rewritten.** Was: Rejected sinks to the bottom, then by round order, then by most-recently-applied. Now: explicit stage rank -- In Progress -> Shortlisted -> Hired -> Rejected (top to bottom), per user's explicit ordering. Within In Progress specifically, most-recently-reviewed floats to the top of that bucket (`candidate.lastCvReviewedAt` descending, nulls/never-reviewed last) -- this is the actual handoff mechanism. Shortlisted keeps the old "further along the round order floats up" tiebreak; Hired/Rejected fall back to most-recently-applied.
- **Handoff indicator added to the Status cell**, shown only on In Progress rows: "Reviewed by {name} · {relative time}" (`.cnd-reviewed-note`, new `relativeTime()` helper -- "5m ago" / "3h ago" / "2d ago", falls back to a plain date past a week).
- **Reject/Shortlist buttons on `CandidateDetailPage.tsx` restyled from a thin outlined pill to a solid filled pill** matching the Hiring Manager's Reject/Hire buttons on Pending Decisions (`pd-action-btn`/`-positive`/`-negative`: `border-radius: 999px; padding: 9px 18px; color: white`, green `#1e7a34` / red `#b91c1c`) -- same colors as before, just filled instead of outlined. Placement went through three rounds: bottom-of-page (original) -> top-right of the header row -> `position: fixed` bottom-right of the viewport -> back to bottom-of-page, per explicit user correction ("not floating, just put it at the end of the screen once they scroll down"). Ends up in the same spot it started, right-aligned with a top border, just with the new solid-pill styling. The now-unused `.cnd-detail-decision-row-bottom`/`-floating` CSS rules were both removed along the way (single call site each, no longer referenced).
- **Investigated but explicitly not changed:** whether `GET /candidates/:id` should be role-gated to HR only. Confirmed via `frontend/src/pages/interviewer/FeedbackPage.tsx` that no non-HR frontend page actually calls this endpoint (Interviewer/Management/HM feedback flows call `GET /interviews/:id` instead) -- the "any role can overwrite the reviewer" risk is real at the raw API layer (no `requireRole` on that route) but not reachable through the built UI today. Left as-is; noted here in case a future page reuses this endpoint for a non-HR role.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` clean (exit 0). No backend changes this pass (data was already being returned; only the frontend type/rendering were missing).

## Twenty-seventh pass: forced password change on first login (new gap, not in either doc)

Surfaced from a direct question while testing `CreateUserPage`: an IT-Admin-created account logs in fine on whatever initial password IT typed into the form, forever, with nothing ever prompting a change. Confirmed before building anything that this genuinely wasn't built (grepped the whole repo for `mustResetPassword`/`forcePasswordChange`/`tempPassword`/`firstLogin` -- zero matches; `User` had exactly one password field). Also confirmed it isn't a doc contradiction either way -- searched the submitted Sprint 1 doc, the AC doc, and every test-case file for "reset password"/"temporary password"/"first login"/"must change": no mentions anywhere. The doc is silent on this, so adding it doesn't create a new documented inconsistency; it closes a real, previously-unflagged security gap.

- **Schema:** `User.mustChangePassword Boolean @default(false)` added to `schema.prisma`. Not yet migrated in this environment -- sandbox has no DB access, user needs to run the migration (see below).
- **`createUser` (`user.controller.ts`)** now sets `mustChangePassword: true` on every account it creates -- the IT-Admin-shared initial password is deliberately one-time-usable-before-a-change, not permanent.
- **New endpoint, `POST /api/auth/change-password`** (`requireAuth`, any role -- deliberately generic like `verifyPassword`, not IT-Admin-specific, since the account being forced to change could be any role). Takes `currentPassword`+`newPassword`, re-verifies the current password via `bcrypt.compare` (same as `login`), 8-char minimum on the new one (same as `resetPassword`), overwrites `passwordHash` and clears `mustChangePassword` in one update. Distinct from the existing `resetPassword` (email-token flow for a genuinely forgotten password) and `verifyPassword` (re-confirm without changing anything) -- this is the "I know my current password, I'm changing it" case, which nothing in the codebase covered before.
- **`login`/`adminLogin` responses** now include `mustChangePassword` on the returned `user` object, so the frontend knows to force the redirect without a second round-trip.
- **Frontend:** new `ChangePasswordPage.tsx` (`/change-password`, mirrors `ResetPasswordPage.tsx`'s look via the same `Login.css` classes, but takes current+new+confirm rather than a reset token). `AuthContext` gained `updateUser(patch)` to flip `mustChangePassword` to `false` in `sessionStorage`+state immediately after a successful change, without needing a fresh token. `ProtectedRoute` now redirects to `/change-password` whenever `user.mustChangePassword` is true, ahead of rendering any role-gated screen -- so closing the tab or navigating away doesn't let the initial password keep working. `Login.tsx`/`AdminLogin.tsx` both check the flag straight off the login response and route to `/change-password` instead of the normal role landing page when it's set.
- **Scope decision:** `mustChangePassword` only ever gets set `true` by `createUser`. `resetPassword` (forgot-password flow) does not set it back to `true` -- a user who forgot their password and reset it via email has already proven they control the account and picked their own new password in the process, so forcing a second change immediately after would be redundant, not safer.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc -b` (project-referenced build, matching the real `npm run build` script) clean, exit 0. Backend `tsc --noEmit` shows exactly two errors, both expected and not real bugs: `mustChangePassword` not yet recognized on the Prisma `User` type, because the generated client (`backend/generated/prisma`) is stale until the migration below is run -- `prisma generate` needs to talk to the network for its query-engine binary and reliably times out in this sandbox, so it wasn't run here; running the migration on the user's own machine regenerates the client automatically. One unrelated pre-existing error in `hiringManager.controller.ts` (`exactOptionalPropertyTypes` complaint) predates this pass entirely (confirmed via `git diff` showing zero changes to that file) and isn't part of this change.

**User needs to run themselves** (sandbox has no DB access): from `backend/`, `npx prisma migrate dev --name add_must_change_password` -- this both writes the migration SQL and regenerates the Prisma client, which will also clear the two expected `tsc` errors above. Every *existing* seeded/demo account defaults `mustChangePassword` to `false` (the column's default), so nobody currently in the system gets forced into this on their next login -- only accounts created after the migration via `CreateUserPage` going forward.

## Twenty-eighth pass: real per-field validation on Create User

`CreateUserPage.tsx` accepted anything -- single-letter names, non-email strings in the Email field, any text in Contact Number, and the "Department (optional)" label meant an IT Admin could create an account with no department and no error at any point, only to have to come back and add one later. User asked for this to actually validate before hitting Create, with errors shown per field, not just a generic banner.

- **Name:** required, must match `/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z' -]{1,}$/` -- letters, spaces, hyphens, apostrophes only (covers "Mary-Jane," "O'Brien"), minimum 2 characters so a single keystroke can't pass.
- **Email:** required, checked against a standard `local@domain.tld` pattern instead of just non-empty.
- **Contact Number:** required, validated against real Sri Lankan phone format -- researched rather than guessed (Sri Lanka is 10-digit domestic numbers starting with `0`, e.g. `0771234567`, or `+94` followed by 9 digits internationally, e.g. `+94771234567`; digits only either way). Regex: `/^(?:0\d{9}|\+94\d{9})$/`. Placeholder text on the field now shows the expected shape.
- **Department:** changed from optional to required (label dropped "(optional)"), matching the user's reasoning -- letting HR/Interviewer/etc. accounts get created with no department just means someone has to come back and edit it later, which is more error-prone than asking for it up front. This is a frontend-only requirement; the backend (`createUser`) still accepts `department` as optional at the API level, unchanged, since nothing else in the system requires it structurally.
- **Password:** kept the minimum at 8, not lowered to 5 as initially asked -- the backend (`createUser`, `resetPassword`, and the new `changePassword` from the previous pass) all hard-enforce `password.length < 8` server-side. Setting the frontend to 5 would let a 5-7 character password pass client validation and then get rejected by the backend with a generic error anyway, which is a worse experience than what existed before, not better. Flagged this directly rather than silently building what was asked.
- **Error display:** each field now shows its own inline red message under the input (new `.cru-field-error`/`.cru-input-error` CSS) instead of one shared banner at the bottom; a field's error clears the moment the user edits that field again, not only on the next Create click. The old single `.cru-error` banner is kept as a "fix the highlighted fields" summary line when any field fails.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc -b` (project-referenced build) clean, exit 0. No backend changes this pass.

## Twenty-ninth pass: createUser now degrades gracefully if the migration hasn't been run yet

User hit "Could not create user" (the generic 500 fallback) while testing Create User. Root cause: they hadn't yet run the Twenty-seventh pass's migration, so `mustChangePassword` doesn't exist as a real column/Prisma Client field on their database, and `createUser`'s `baseData` unconditionally included it -- Prisma threw `PrismaClientValidationError: Unknown argument \`mustChangePassword\``, uncaught (the existing retry logic only special-cased `phoneNumber` by name), landing in the outer catch's generic message. Worth noting: this had nothing to do with the password itself -- the "Your Password" field in that flow (`PasswordConfirmModal`) is the *IT Admin's own* login password via `verify-password`, not the new account's initial password; it had already passed by the time the real error showed.

- **Generalized `createUser`'s retry logic** (`user.controller.ts`) from a hardcoded two-deep try/catch that only knew about `phoneNumber` into a bounded loop: parse the unknown-argument name out of Prisma's own error message (`Unknown argument \`(\w+)\``), strip that field from the create payload, retry, repeat (capped at 5 attempts). Handles `phoneNumber`, `mustChangePassword`, or any future migration-pending field on this form without needing another special case added by name each time.
- **Effect while unmigrated:** account creation still succeeds, just without `mustChangePassword` being set -- meaning the forced-first-login-change doesn't apply to accounts created before the migration runs. Not a security regression (this enforcement didn't exist at all before the Twenty-seventh pass), just scoped to "not enforced yet on this specific un-migrated database," same category as the existing `phoneNumberSaved` degradation.

**Verification:** backend `tsc --noEmit` shows the same two expected `mustChangePassword`-on-Prisma-type errors as the Twenty-seventh pass (stale generated client, resolves once the migration runs) plus the one pre-existing unrelated `hiringManager.controller.ts` error -- no new errors from this change. The migration is still the real fix; this just stops account creation from hard-failing in the meantime.

## Thirtieth pass: root-caused the "didn't force a change" report; Department -> dropdown; forced-change now ends in a relogin, not a silent continue

User ran the Twenty-seventh pass's migration (confirmed: `prisma/migrations/20260909105326_add_must_change_password/migration.sql` exists, dated today, `ALTER TABLE user ADD COLUMN mustChangePassword BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false`), created a test account, logged in with its initial password, and it did not force a change. Root-caused rather than guessed: `backend/generated/prisma/index.d.ts` was still dated Sep 1 -- the migration itself ran, but the generated Prisma Client was never regenerated afterward (`migrate dev` normally does this as its last step; didn't complete it here, same category of issue as this sandbox's own `prisma generate` timing out on its engine-binary download in the Twenty-seventh pass). Consequence: the Twenty-ninth pass's own resilience fix (strip any argument Prisma reports as unknown, retry) did exactly what it was built to do -- caught `mustChangePassword` as unrecognized and silently created the account without it, so it landed on the column's plain SQL default (`false`) instead of the intended `true`. Not a new bug; the fallback worked as designed, the client was just still stale. **User needs to run `npx prisma generate` (or re-run `migrate dev`) and recreate that test account** -- the one already created will stay `false` permanently, the flag is only set at creation time.

- **`CreateUserPage.tsx`: Department changed from free-text to a `<select>`**, reusing the exact same 8-item canonical list (`HR, Finance and Accounting, Operations, Marketing, Sales, IT, Customer Service, Legal`) already defined in `VacanciesPage.tsx` for its department-browse cards -- same vocabulary across the app rather than IT Admin being able to type a department no vacancy will ever match. Since a `<select>` can never be empty, the field-level "Department is required" validation from the Twenty-eighth pass was removed as no longer reachable (mirrors Role, which never had that error path either).
- **Form grid reordered** so Role and Department sit in the same row (both are now fixed-list dropdowns) -- moved Initial Password up to pair with Email instead, Role+Department now the final row together.
- **`ChangePasswordPage.tsx` flow changed:** previously called `updateUser({ mustChangePassword: false })` and continued straight into the app on success. Changed to match `ResetPasswordPage.tsx`'s existing pattern instead -- on success, log the account out (`logout()` from `AuthContext`) and show a "Password changed. Log back in with your new password." confirmation with a button back to `/login`, rather than silently continuing the existing session. This is what the user actually described wanting ("let them reset and relogin with the new password"), and it's more consistent with the sibling reset-password screen already doing exactly this.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc -b` clean, exit 0. No backend changes this pass -- the createUser resilience logic from the Twenty-ninth pass already covers this scenario correctly; nothing there needed changing, only the generated client the user's own machine hadn't regenerated.

## Thirty-first pass: Edit User consolidated to match Create User's fields; double password-confirm on edit; close button

User asked for the Edit User popup to carry the same fields as Create User (previously: Name/Email/Department only, with Role change split off into its own separately-launched "Change Role" popup and no Contact Number field at all), plus explicit password confirmation both when *opening* an edit and again when *saving* it (Deactivate already had this from an earlier pass), plus a close/X on the modal for a misclick.

- **Backend (`user.controller.ts`): `updateUser` now also accepts `phoneNumber`**, written the same conditional way as the other fields. (`department`'s migration and `phoneNumber`'s migration had both already run on the user's machine by this point, confirmed by the Prisma type errors from the Twenty-seventh/Thirtieth passes no longer appearing at all in this pass's `tsc` run -- the client is now current.)
- **`UsersPage.tsx` restructured:** the separate "Change Role" modal/state (`roleTarget`/`roleForm`/`openRoleForm`/`handleRoleSave`) is gone -- Role is now one more field inside the single Edit User modal, alongside Name, Contact Number, Email, and Department. Saving diffs the role against the original and only calls the separate `setUserRole` endpoint (still its own audit event, `ROLE_CHANGED`, unchanged) when it actually changed. The table's Role cell is now plain text instead of a clickable button, since there's no longer a separate role-only flow to launch from it. Self-role-change is still blocked (disabled `<select>` + tooltip) since the backend still rejects it.
- **Department in the edit form is now the same 8-item canonical `<select>`** as Create User and Vacancies, not free text -- consistent vocabulary everywhere a department gets picked.
- **Two password gates on Edit, not one:** clicking the pencil now opens a `PasswordConfirmModal` first (`pendingEditUser`); only after that succeeds does the actual Edit modal open (`editingUser`, populated from the confirmed user). Clicking Save no longer saves directly -- it validates the form, then opens a second `PasswordConfirmModal` (`confirmingSave`); the real `updateUser`/`setUserRole` calls only fire from that second modal's `onConfirmed`. Deactivate/Activate is unchanged (already gated from an earlier pass). This is deliberately more friction than a single confirm -- explicit, repeated ask, not an oversight -- worth knowing if it ever needs simplifying to one confirm covering the whole edit.
- **Added an X close button** (`.usr-modal-close`) to the Edit User modal's header, next to the title, alongside the existing Cancel button and backdrop-click-to-close.
- **Modal widened from 420px to 640px with a 2-column grid** (`.usr-edit-grid`/`.usr-edit-field`, mirrors `CreateUserPage`'s `.cru-form-grid` pattern) to fit 5 fields without an unreasonably tall single-column stack.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc -b` clean, exit 0. Backend `tsc --noEmit` now shows *only* the one pre-existing unrelated `hiringManager.controller.ts` error -- the `mustChangePassword` errors from the Twenty-seventh/Thirtieth passes are gone, confirming the user's Prisma Client is now fully current.

## Thirty-second pass: Edit User fully locked for self, not just Role/Deactivate

User caught a real inconsistency in the Thirtieth pass's merged Edit User modal: editing your own account already disabled Role and Deactivate (server-enforced -- self-role-change and self-deactivation are both blocked in `user.controller.ts`, deliberately, so a single IT Admin can never lock themselves and everyone else out with no one left able to undo it), but Name/Contact Number/Email/Department were still freely editable on yourself, and Save was still enabled. Inconsistent halfway state, not a deliberate one.

- **`UsersPage.tsx`:** the Edit User modal body is now wrapped in an IIFE that computes `isSelf` once and applies it everywhere -- all five fields (`disabled={isSelf}`), the Save button (`disabled={editSaving || isSelf}`), and Deactivate (already `isSelf`-gated, unchanged). A one-line note now shows at the top of the modal when editing yourself: "You can't edit your own account here. Ask another IT Admin to make changes, including deactivating it."
- **`.usr-modal input:disabled` / `select:disabled` styling added** (grey fill, muted text, not-allowed cursor) so disabled fields read as visibly locked, not just non-interactive with no visual cue.
- **Answered the operational question this surfaced ("how do we retire an IT Admin and replace them"):** it has to be done by a *second* IT Admin account, not the one being retired -- that's the actual purpose of the self-locks, not an oversight. Workflow: create the incoming person's IT Admin account first (while the outgoing one is still active), have them log in via `/admin-login`, and have *them* deactivate the old account. Repurposing the same account (editing name/email to the new person) was explicitly not recommended -- it would misattribute the outgoing admin's audit log history to someone who never took those actions.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc -b` clean, exit 0. No backend changes -- this was UI-only, the server-side self-locks it now matches were already correct and untouched.

---

## Thirty-third pass: viva-readability tweaks (Interview Stages, Assign Panel ordering) + named/reusable Interview Panels

Six-screenshot batch. Three small UI tweaks so the lecturer has less to probe, plus one real architecture addition the user explicitly asked my opinion on twice ("what do u think").

**Small tweaks (`VacanciesPage.tsx`, `InterviewsPage.tsx`):**
- Removed the visible "Locked - a candidate has already entered a round on this vacancy..." banner text from Interview Stages. The underlying lock behavior (no add/rename/reorder/delete of rounds once any candidate has entered one, derived from `currentVacancyStageId` -- see `VacancyStage` schema comment) is untouched; only the explanatory paragraph was removed. Reasoning given to user: it protects candidates already mid-pipeline from having their round structure change under them.
- Removed the "(optional)" suffix from the "Interview Stages" label on Create/Edit Vacancy. Rounds are still functionally optional (nothing blocks saving a vacancy with zero configured stages) -- only the label text changed, to avoid an easy "why are stages optional if HR always fills them in" lecturer question.
- Assign Interview Panel's staff checklist now sorts Interviewer -> Hiring Manager -> Management (alphabetical-by-name as tiebreaker within each role) via a `PANEL_ROLE_ORDER` map, instead of the raw order `/staff` returns.

**Named, reusable Interview Panels (real feature, new schema entity):**

Previous model: one flat `VacancyInterviewer` pool per vacancy, added to via Assign Interview Panel, with no grouping or naming -- Schedule Interview always used the *entire* pool as the slot's panelists automatically, no per-interview selection existed at all.

User wanted: create multiple named panels per vacancy ("Panel 1", "Panel 2", ...), pick one or more by name at Schedule Interview time, and no blocking on reusing the same staff member across different panels.

Given my pushback on scope (this is a new Prisma model + backend endpoints + two reworked screens, not documented in any submitted Sprint doc, this close to viva prep) the user chose to build it anyway.

Design chosen to minimize blast radius: `InterviewPanel` (`id, vacancyId, name, createdAt`, unique on `[vacancyId, name]`) + join table `InterviewPanelMember` (`panelId, userId`, unique on `[panelId, userId]`) added **additively** alongside `VacancyInterviewer`, not replacing it. `VacancyInterviewer` still exists unchanged and still means "everyone assigned to this vacancy across all its panels" -- `createInterviewPanel` upserts each member into that pool in the same transaction, so the existing scheduling validation (`validatePanelistsAndConflict` in `interview.controller.ts`, which checks panelists belong to the `VacancyInterviewer` pool) needed zero changes. `CandidateDetailPage.tsx`'s separate quick single-candidate schedule flow, which also reads the whole pool, is likewise unaffected.

Staff are never disabled/blocked when building a panel -- the same person can legitimately be a member of two different named panels for one vacancy. This is distinct from the *pool's* own duplicate-prevention (adding the same person to the flat pool twice was always a no-op, not a "bug" -- that's a different, correct constraint at a different level than panel-to-panel reuse).

- **Backend:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma` -- `InterviewPanel`, `InterviewPanelMember` models added (needs `npx prisma migrate dev --name add_interview_panels` + `npx prisma generate` run by the user, sandbox has no DB/network access for this). New `backend/src/controllers/interviewPanel.controller.ts` (`createInterviewPanel`, `listInterviewPanelsForVacancy`), wired at `GET/POST /vacancies/:id/panels` in `vacancy.routes.ts`.
- **Frontend:** new `frontend/src/api/interviewPanels.ts`. `AssignPanelModal` (`InterviewsPage.tsx`) reordered to Panel Name -> Vacancy -> Staff (staff checkboxes never disabled), shows existing panel names for the selected vacancy as a hint against accidental name collisions (name is unique per vacancy, server returns 409 on duplicate). `ScheduleInterviewModal` replaced its old read-only "whole pool" summary with a checklist of the vacancy's named panels (one or more selectable, each expandable to show members); submission sends the union of selected panels' member user IDs as `panelistUserIds`, same endpoint/shape as before.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc -b` clean, exit 0. Backend `tsc --noEmit` shows the expected stale-client errors on `prisma.interviewPanel` (new model not yet in the generated client -- same situation as the Twenty-seventh pass's `mustChangePassword` field, resolves once the user runs migrate+generate) plus one pre-existing, unrelated `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` error in `hiringManager.controller.ts` that predates this batch (confirmed via `git status` -- that file has no uncommitted changes).

## Thirty-fourth pass: Hiring Managers excluded from interview panels (stale role list)

User caught a real, pre-existing inconsistency that the Thirty-third pass's new panel feature had just copied forward rather than fixed: the role-eligibility checks for "who can be assigned to an interview panel/pool" (`vacancy.controller.ts`'s `assignInterviewerToVacancy`, and the new `interviewPanel.controller.ts`'s `PANEL_ELIGIBLE_ROLES`) both listed `INTERVIEWER, MANAGEMENT, HIRING_MANAGER` -- but this project's actual, already-decided design is that Hiring Managers never sit on interview panels. That was settled explicitly in the 23rd pass: an HM "My Interviews" page was built (21st pass), then removed outright once the user clarified the HM's real job is deciding Proceed/Do Not Proceed/Hire/Reject from *other people's* interview feedback via Pending Decisions, not attending interviews themselves. Confirmed via code/seed-script audit before changing anything: `checkFinalRoundHasManagement` only ever checks for `MANAGEMENT`, no seed script ever creates an `InterviewPanelist` row for an HM-role user, and `hiringManager.controller.ts` only ever touches `StageRecommendation`/decision fields, never `InterviewPanelist`/`Feedback`. The eligibility arrays were simply stale leftovers from before that reversal, never cleaned up.

- **`backend/src/controllers/interviewPanel.controller.ts`:** `PANEL_ELIGIBLE_ROLES` narrowed to `["INTERVIEWER", "MANAGEMENT"]`.
- **`backend/src/controllers/vacancy.controller.ts`:** `assignInterviewerToVacancy`'s role check narrowed the same way, for consistency -- this older endpoint owns the same underlying `VacancyInterviewer` pool table that panel creation upserts into.
- **`frontend/src/pages/hr/InterviewsPage.tsx`:** `AssignPanelModal`'s staff checklist now filters out `HIRING_MANAGER` before sorting/rendering. Deliberately filtered client-side rather than at the shared `/staff` endpoint -- `listAssignableStaff` is dual-purpose (also used by `CandidateDetailPage.tsx` to pick a Hiring Manager *for* an application, which still needs to return HMs), so narrowing it there would have broken that unrelated flow.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc -b` clean, exit 0. Backend `tsc --noEmit` now shows only the one pre-existing, unrelated `hiringManager.controller.ts` error noted in the prior pass -- the `interviewPanel` stale-client errors are gone now that the eligible-role narrowing didn't require any new Prisma fields (no new migration needed for this fix, on top of the one from the Thirty-third pass).

## Thirty-fifth pass: Shortlist/Reject confirmation toast; Candidate Detail's ad-hoc Schedule Interview form removed

**Reported as a bug, confirmed not one:** user scheduled a round-1 interview for a candidate via the main Interviews page (Assign Panel -> Schedule Interview -> Add Candidate(s) to Interview) and the candidate didn't show up in Follow Ups' "Send Invite Email" list, though they did show in "Upcoming Calls." Traced through `followUp.controller.ts`: `pendingInvites` (the manual invite list) deliberately filters to `vacancyStage.order > 1` only -- round 1 always auto-sends the candidate their "interview scheduled" email immediately, from both scheduling entry points (`scheduleInterview()`'s candidate-invite block and `addCandidatesToSlot()`'s matching block, `interview.controller.ts`, same `interview_scheduled_candidate` reason string either way). So a round-1 candidate correctly has nothing pending to manually invite -- it already went out. `upcomingCalls` has no such round filter, which is why the same candidate appeared there. Not a bug; explained to user, no code change.

**Real, actioned request:** two Candidate Detail page fixes.

- **`CandidatesPage.tsx`/`CandidateDetailPage.tsx`:** clicking Reject or Shortlist previously navigated back to the candidates list with zero feedback -- no confirmation of what had just happened. Fixed using the same one-shot router-state toast pattern already established elsewhere (`FeedbackPage.tsx` -> `MyCandidatesPage.tsx`): `handleReject`/`handleShortlist` now `navigate("/hr/candidates", { state: { toast: "<Name> was rejected/shortlisted." } })`; `CandidatesPage.tsx` reads `location.state.toast` once on mount, clears it via `replace` so a refresh doesn't re-show it, and renders it with the existing `Toast` component.
- **`CandidateDetailPage.tsx`:** removed the page's own "Schedule Interview" section entirely (stage/date/panel form, `scheduleInterview()` call, and all its state). It was a redundant second path to the same action already available via the main Interviews page, and a stale one at that -- it used the old raw per-person panel checklist (`VacancyInterviewer` pool), not the Thirty-third pass's named/reusable Panels, and didn't have the Thirty-fourth pass's Hiring-Manager-exclusion applied, so it would have silently let an HM be picked as a panelist if used. Rather than duplicating the new panel-picker UI onto a third screen, removed the form outright -- HR can still schedule (or add this candidate to an existing slot) from the Interviews page. Replaced the page's single flat "Interviews" list with two: **Upcoming Interviews** and **Completed Interviews**, split client-side on `scheduledAt` vs now (there's no separate "completed" flag -- a past-dated interview just means it already happened), so HR can see at a glance what's ahead and what's already been done for this candidate as they move further into the stages.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc -b` clean, exit 0 -- confirmed no leftover references to the removed state/handlers/imports (`detailStages`, `detailPanel`, `scheduleStageId`, `scheduleInterview`, `roleLabel`, etc.) anywhere in the file.

## Thirty-sixth pass: stale "US-20" story citation in schema.prisma corrected

User quoted a real AC-doc row for US-20 ("Edit own interview feedback... ownership-based authorization for feedback records") that didn't match what `schema.prisma`'s `ApplicationStageHistory` comment cited US-20 for ("every stage transition is logged..."). Cross-checked against this session's own earlier doc audit (see the "AC numbering skips US-20, US-40, and US-41 entirely" / "zombie story" findings above): the schema comment's "US-20" was a leftover from the *original, uncorrected raw backlog*, where "As an Interviewer, I want to update candidate progression stages after each interview" sat informally in slot 20 -- never real AC-doc content, and already flagged as a zombie entry in a prior pass but never actually cleaned out of the code comment itself. The comment's "US-37" half was also imprecise -- US-37 is "vacancy-specific reports" (the PDF report screens), not this table.

**Fix:** `backend/prisma/schema.prisma` -- `ApplicationStageHistory`'s comment now cites **US-33** ("Bottleneck identification (time in stage)," confirmed against `story-implementation-table.md`) as the real story this table supports, with a note explaining what the old citation was and why it was wrong, so this doesn't get miscited again. No functional/schema change, comment-only.

Also confirmed for the user (via code read, not memory): automatic round-1 interview-invite emails **do** appear in a candidate's Email History. `getCandidate()` (`candidate.controller.ts`) builds `emailHistory` from `AuditLog` rows where `action === "NOTIFICATION_SENT"` and `metadata.recipient` matches the candidate's email -- both the automatic send (round 1, fired from `scheduleInterview()`/`addCandidatesToSlot()`) and a manual send from Follow Ups write the identical audit-log shape (`reason: "interview_scheduled_candidate"`), so they're indistinguishable in that list, both correctly labeled "Interview invitation." Not a gap -- Email History is a record of what was actually sent, regardless of which code path sent it.

**Where item 29 lives:** the "Applicant History" section on the Candidate Detail page (HR > Candidates > click a candidate row > `/hr/candidates/:applicationId`) -- confirmed directly in `CandidateDetailPage.tsx`, sits between Review Notes and Email History.

## Thirty-seventh pass: full 30-story Sprint Backlog audit against actual code

User pasted the complete Sprint Backlog table (30 user stories with full AC) and asked whether it was all built and whether there were contradictions. Verified every story against the real code (not doc memory) via a dedicated research pass; all 30 map cleanly onto the existing US-01..US-50 set in `story-implementation-table.md` and all 30 are Built, with one gap and five real AC-wording contradictions found:

**Gap:** assigning someone to a vacancy's interviewer panel/pool does not notify them -- no `notifyUser()` call exists in `vacancy.controller.ts` or `interviewPanel.controller.ts`. Notifications only fire at interview *scheduling* time (`interview.controller.ts:246`) and via manual Follow-Ups reminders. If the AC bullet means "notified when added to the panel," that's unbuilt.

**Contradictions (AC text vs. actual behavior), each confirmed directly in code, not just via the research pass:**

1. **Deactivated-login message.** AC claims a distinct "Your account has been deactivated" message. Actual: `auth.controller.ts` returns one `GENERIC_ERROR = "Invalid email or password"` for wrong password, nonexistent user, AND deactivated account (deliberate, to prevent account-enumeration -- see the Sprint 1 doc audit entry above where this was first found and fixed for the test-case docs, but this AC row wasn't part of that reconciliation). No distinct deactivation message exists anywhere.
2. **Status marker colors.** AC claims green=shortlisted / red=rejected / yellow=unreviewed. Actual (`CandidatesPage.tsx` `statusFor()`): APPLIED(unreviewed)=blue, SHORTLISTED=yellow, HIRED=green, REJECTED=red. Only "red=rejected" matches; the other two are swapped/wrong relative to this AC's wording (current scheme was set deliberately in an earlier pass -- "Swapped per follow-up correction: Shortlisted now reads yellow, Hired now reads green").
3. **Interview double-booking scope.** AC claims "error if two interviews for the same vacancy overlap." Actual (`validatePanelistsAndConflict`, `interview.controller.ts`): the real rule is per-*panelist* (a specific person double-booked), not per-vacancy -- two different candidates on the same vacancy with non-overlapping panelists CAN be scheduled at the same time with no error.
4. **Reports gated on vacancy closure.** AC claims reports "can only generate after the vacancy is closed." Actual (`reports.controller.ts`, explicit code comment): no closed-only gate exists -- an earlier decision in this project deliberately overrode that AC clause and reports work on open-or-closed vacancies.
5. **Reapplication after rejection.** AC claims that if a candidate reapplies after a prior rejection, both application records show in their history. Actual: `CandidateApplication` has `@@unique([candidateId, vacancyId])` (`schema.prisma`), and `applyCandidateToVacancy` returns 409 "This candidate has already applied to this vacancy" on the resulting Prisma unique violation -- a second application row for the same (candidate, vacancy) pair is schema-impossible. This AC bullet's own scenario cannot happen in the current design.

Not logged as a fix -- this was a verification/reporting pass, no code changed. Flagged here so these are known talking points (or fix candidates) rather than surprises if raised in the viva.

---

## Thirty-eighth pass: three follow-ups from the backlog audit -- panel-assignment notification, status label, clickable duplicate-CV match

**Notification gap closed.** `vacancy.controller.ts`'s `assignInterviewerToVacancy` and `interviewPanel.controller.ts`'s `createInterviewPanel` now call `notifyUser()` for each person added to a vacancy's interviewer pool / a named panel -- previously silent, the only prior notification fire points were interview *scheduling* and manual Follow-Ups reminders. Best-effort (never blocks the assignment itself), same contract as every other `notifyUser()` call site.

**Status label:** `CandidatesPage.tsx`'s `statusFor()` -- APPLIED-stage candidates now read "Unreviewed" instead of "In Progress" (color unchanged, still blue), matching the AC doc's own wording for this state and reading less like something is actively happening when really nobody's screened it yet. Filter dropdown option text updated to match; the internal `"IN_PROGRESS"` bucket/filter value is unchanged (not user-visible). Scoped narrowly to this one status marker -- other unrelated "In Progress" text elsewhere (vacancy fill-status on Management/Leadership dashboards, HM's candidate-list section title) is a different concept and was left alone.

**Duplicate-CV match is now clickable.** Previously, uploading a CV that matched an existing candidate (by email) showed a plain-text toast naming them. Now `duplicateMatches` state holds one entry per match (not a joined string), each linking to `/hr/candidates/:applicationId` for the application this CV just got attached to -- HR can click through and confirm it's really the same person instead of taking the match on faith. Rendered as a custom panel reusing the shared `.toast`/`.toast-message` CSS classes directly (not the `<Toast>` component itself, which only supports one plain-text message) since a batch upload can produce more than one match at once.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc -b` clean, exit 0. Backend `tsc --noEmit` unchanged from the prior pass (only the pre-existing, unrelated `hiringManager.controller.ts` error).

Also clarified for the user (not a code change): the interview double-booking conflict check (`validatePanelistsAndConflict`, `interview.controller.ts`) is a **same person + exact same `scheduledAt` timestamp** match -- there's no interview duration/end-time modeled anywhere in the schema, so it's an exact-time collision, not a true overlapping-range check. A person interviewing 10:00-10:30 and another slot logged at 10:15 would NOT be caught, since 10:00 != 10:15. Same nuance already flagged in the Thirty-seventh pass's contradiction #3 (the rule is per-panelist, not per-vacancy) -- restated here since the user re-described it as "same person + overlapping time," which is close but not exactly what's enforced.

---

## Thirty-ninth pass: clickable duplicate-on-same-vacancy link; "Reconsider" for early-rejected applications

Two follow-ups from a screenshot showing the CV-upload "already been uploaded to this vacancy" dead-end and the Candidate Detail Reject/Shortlist row.

**Same-vacancy duplicate is now clickable too.** The Thirty-eighth pass only handled a CV matching an existing candidate applying to a *different* vacancy (`result.matched`, success path). The screenshot showed the other case -- re-uploading the same person's CV to the vacancy they'd *already* applied to, which throws a 409 in the `matched` loop's catch block and previously fell through to a plain-text `confirmFailNotice` ("This CV has already been uploaded to this vacancy") with nowhere to go.

- **`frontend/src/api/client.ts`:** new `ApiError extends Error` class carries the full parsed error response body (`data`), not just the message string -- `apiFetch` now throws this instead of a plain `Error`. Still passes every existing `err instanceof Error` check unchanged.
- **`backend/src/controllers/application.controller.ts`:** `applyCandidateToVacancy`'s P2002 (duplicate) branch now looks up and returns the existing application's id (`existingApplicationId`) alongside the error message.
- **`CandidatesPage.tsx`:** the matched-loop's catch reads `existingApplicationId` off the caught `ApiError` and routes it into `duplicateMatches` (extended with an `alreadyOnThisVacancy` flag for correct wording) instead of `failReasons` -- same clickable "View their profile" treatment as a cross-vacancy match. Adjusted the success/failure bookkeeping so a batch that's *entirely* same-vacancy repeats (successCount 0, failReasons empty) doesn't show a false "Successfully uploaded 0 CVs" toast -- the duplicateMatches panel alone explains what happened. Also dropped the now-dead `isAllDuplicates` special-case branch in the failure-notice text, since that error string can no longer reach `failReasons` at all.

**"Reconsider" -- the real gap this surfaced.** User asked how to re-shortlist someone after rejecting them, expecting the flow the AC doc describes (reapply after rejection). Traced it: `updateApplicationStatus` (`application.controller.ts`) hard-blocked any transition except from stage `APPLIED` -- so once REJECTED, an application was a dead end. Combined with the schema's `@@unique([candidateId, vacancyId])` (can't create a second application either, see Thirty-seventh pass contradiction #5), a CV-screening rejection was **permanent and irreversible** for that vacancy, full stop. This is a real structural gap, not a UI oversight -- `transitionApplicationStage` itself (`stageTransition.ts`) is a generic state-writer with no restriction on direction, so nothing at the data layer prevented fixing this.

- **`application.controller.ts`:** `updateApplicationStatus` now also allows `SHORTLISTED` from stage `REJECTED`, but **only** when `hiringDecision` is null. That field distinguishes an early CV-screening rejection (HR rejected before ever shortlisting -- `hiringDecision` stays null) from a real post-interview final outcome (`recordHiringDecision` sets `hiringDecision: "REJECT"` *and* `stage: "REJECTED"` -- same stage value, different meaning). Deliberately does NOT let HR reopen a genuine final hiring decision through this endpoint -- `recordHiringDecision` already blocks re-deciding once stage is HIRED/REJECTED, and reopening a completed interview process is a bigger, separate action than "reconsider a CV that was never shortlisted."
- **`frontend/src/api/candidates.ts`:** added `hiringDecision: "HIRE" | "REJECT" | null` to `CandidateApplicationRow` -- the field was already in the raw JSON (Prisma's `include` returns all scalars by default) but not declared in the frontend type.
- **`CandidateDetailPage.tsx`:** new `handleReconsider()` (mirrors `handleShortlist`, distinct toast wording: "X was reconsidered and moved back to Shortlisted."). New decision row, shown only when `stage === "REJECTED" && !hiringDecision`, explaining there's no way to reapply and offering the single "Reconsider" action instead.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc -b` clean, exit 0. Backend `tsc --noEmit` unchanged (only the pre-existing, unrelated `hiringManager.controller.ts` error).

## Fortieth pass: duplicate-match notice reworded, then moved from toast to centered modal

Two rounds of feedback on the same notice from the Thirty-ninth pass.

**Wording cleanup (no functional change).** "Make the pop up info more professional, remove unnecessary wording and '--'." The two message templates in `CandidatesPage.tsx` (`duplicateMatches` block) had their trailing explanatory clause ("-- this CV wasn't uploaded again. View their profile →") and all "--" separators removed, leaving plain sentences ("X has already applied to this vacancy." / "X already has a candidate profile and has also applied to Y.").

**Toast -> centered modal.** "This should not come as a pop, it should come in the middle of the screen and pretty." The notice was still a bottom-fixed, dark-background panel reusing the shared `<Toast>` component's raw `.toast`/`.toast-message` CSS classes -- easy to miss and read like a transient error rather than something worth reading. Converted to a real centered modal, reusing this file's existing `.cnd-modal-backdrop`/`.cnd-modal` pattern (the same one the Upload CV modal already uses) rather than inventing a new one.

- **`CandidatesPage.tsx`:** `duplicateMatches` now renders as `.cnd-modal-backdrop` > `.cnd-modal.cnd-duplicate-modal`, with a heading ("Existing candidate(s) found"), an icon, one card per match (message + "View their profile →" link), and a Close button in `.cnd-modal-actions` -- backdrop click and Close both clear `duplicateMatches`.
- **`CandidatesPage.css`:** removed `.cnd-duplicate-toast`/its old row styling; added `.cnd-duplicate-modal`, `.cnd-duplicate-modal-icon`, `.cnd-duplicate-list`, and restyled `.cnd-duplicate-row` as a bordered card (was an inline toast row) with `.cnd-duplicate-link` in the existing gold-dark link convention.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc -b` clean, exit 0.

## Forty-first pass: duplicate-modal wording trimmed; Email History now shows real sent content

Feedback on the same duplicate-match modal plus a separate ask to make Email History actually show what was sent.

**Duplicate modal: dropped the intro line.** "This CV wasn't uploaded as a new candidate." sat between the heading and the match cards and risked reading as an error/failure rather than an FYI. Removed outright rather than reworded -- the heading ("Existing candidate found") plus each card's own sentence already say what happened. `CandidatesPage.tsx`'s `duplicateMatches` block, `CandidatesPage.css`'s now-dead `.cnd-duplicate-modal-intro` rule removed. Also centered each card's content (message + "View their profile" link) since the modal itself is centered -- left-aligned text inside a centered card looked lopsided.

**Email History: subject/body now persisted and shown, not just "an email was sent."** Previously (Task #44) `AuditLog.metadata` for a `NOTIFICATION_SENT` entry only ever stored `{recipient, channel, reason}` -- deliberately documented at the time as "what kind, when, not the actual content." User asked to see the real content on click.

- **Backend, three candidate-facing send sites** now also write `subject`/`body` into that same metadata blob (the values were already in scope at each call site, nothing new to compute): `application.controller.ts`'s `recordHiringDecision` (hire/reject email), `interview.controller.ts`'s `scheduleInterview` and `addCandidatesToSlot` (round-1 auto-invite), and `followUp.controller.ts`'s manual "send invite" endpoint. Panelist-facing sends (`interview_scheduled_panelist`, `manual_feedback_reminder`) were left as-is -- they never reach a candidate's Email History (filtered by recipient email) so there's nothing to surface there yet.
- **`candidate.controller.ts`'s `getCandidate`:** `emailHistory` mapping now also reads `subject`/`body` off each log's metadata, `null` if absent (i.e. any email sent before this change -- old AuditLog rows don't have these fields and aren't backfilled).
- **`frontend/src/api/candidates.ts`:** `CandidateEmailHistoryEntry` gains `subject: string | null` / `body: string | null`.
- **`CandidateDetailPage.tsx`:** each Email History row is now a `<button>` that toggles an inline expansion showing the subject and full body, or "Content not available for this email." when both are `null` (pre-existing sends). Kept inline rather than a modal -- multiple rows can be open independently, no reason to force one-at-a-time.

**Also answered, no code change (both are working as designed, not bugs):**
- **Applicant History showing "No other applications from this candidate"** on a candidate who only has one application total is correct -- that section explicitly filters out the current application (`a.id !== row.id`) to show *other* vacancies this person applied to. A candidate whose only application is the one being viewed will always show empty there; it's not a history-of-this-application log.
- **No Reconsider button visible on a Shortlisted candidate** is also correct -- `CandidateDetailPage.tsx`'s decision row only renders Reject/Shortlist when `stage === "APPLIED"`, or Reconsider when `stage === "REJECTED" && !hiringDecision` (Thirty-ninth pass). A candidate already at Shortlisted has no action to take on this page by design; nothing to reconsider.

**Verification:** frontend `tsc -b` clean, backend `tsc --noEmit` unchanged (only the pre-existing, unrelated `hiringManager.controller.ts` error).

## Test-case script for Email History content (`backend/scripts/seed-email-history-demo.ts`)

User asked to actually see the Forty-first pass's Email History content feature working end to end, with a fully-populated candidate rather than a mostly-empty one. Sandbox has no DB access, so this is a script for the user to run, not something built and demoed directly.

Builds one candidate ("Noah Bennett") with every Candidate Detail section non-empty: Review Notes filled (reviewed by Hannah HR, with a note), two applications ("Backend Engineer" -- SHORTLISTED, Hiring Manager assigned, one completed + one upcoming interview; "Product Designer" -- already HIRED), so Applicant History has a real second row to show. Critically, both emails in Email History are sent through the *actual* production code path (`renderTemplate` + `sendEmail` + `writeAuditLog`, same calls `interview.controller.ts` and `application.controller.ts` make) rather than hand-written fake rows -- so the subject/body shown really is what the real send path produces, not a mock. Guarded by an existing-audit-log check per entity, so it's safe to re-run without duplicate sends.

Run from the backend folder: `npx tsx scripts/seed-email-history-demo.ts`. Then: Candidates -> search "Noah Bennett" -> open either application -> Email History has two real entries to expand.

## Forty-second pass: Candidate Detail locks Review Notes + Hiring Manager once an application has a final decision

Prompted by the seed script above -- viewing Noah Bennett's already-HIRED "Product Designer" application still showed a fully editable Review Notes textarea and Save Note button, as if there were still something to decide.

**New: `isFinalDecision = row.hiringDecision !== null`** (`CandidateDetailPage.tsx`), deliberately the same distinction the Reconsider row already relies on (Thirty-ninth pass) -- `hiringDecision` set means a real post-interview HIRE/REJECT decision, not an early CV-screening rejection (which stays fully editable/reconsiderable, correctly). When true:
- **Review Notes:** textarea + Save Note button replaced with a static read-only box showing the note (or "No notes were left."), plus a one-line reason why it's locked.
- **Hiring Manager:** the reassignment dropdown + Assign button replaced with the same one-line locked note; "Currently: X" still shows.
- **Reject/Shortlist/Reconsider row:** already correctly hides itself once `hiringDecision` is set (Thirty-ninth pass's existing stage-based conditions) -- no change needed there, confirmed rather than assumed.

**Flagged to user, not silently decided:** `lastCvReviewNote` lives on the `Candidate` record, not the `CandidateApplication` -- it's one field shared across everything that person has ever applied to. Locking it here only locks THIS application's view of it; if the same candidate has another still-open application, the identical note stays editable from there (e.g. Noah Bennett's "Backend Engineer" application, still Shortlisted, can still edit the note that's now read-only on his concluded "Product Designer" application). This is treated as correct, not a bug -- the note is about screening the CV in general, not a per-vacancy field, so a true candidate-wide lock would block editing even while a different application for the same person is still actively being screened.

- **`CandidatesPage.css`:** added `.cnd-review-note-locked` (same box dimensions/padding as the textarea it replaces, so the card doesn't visually jump) and `.cnd-locked-hint` (small italic explanatory line).

**Verification:** frontend `tsc -b` clean, exit 0.

## Forty-third pass: candidate-side interview scheduling conflict check (real gap found via user's own instinct)

User asked whether a candidate being active in two vacancies at once was addressed anywhere in the Sprint 01 docs/diagrams. It isn't, and it's fine that it isn't -- searched the full Sprint 01 submission doc, `Product-Backlog-Raw-Corrected.docx`, the ERD (`Altrium_HR_ERD_Corrected.drawio`), and both Manual Test Cases docs for "multiple vacancies" / "same time" / "simultaneous" / "concurrent". The only real hits are US-29 (Applicant History, which explicitly assumes and depends on a candidate having several applications across vacancies -- see the Thirty-seventh pass's separate, already-flagged reapply contradiction, a different issue) and BUG-03 (a display bug, already fixed). Nothing anywhere restricts or even discusses a candidate being actively pursued for two vacancies concurrently -- it's simply allowed, same as any real-world ATS.

Chasing it down anyway turned up a real, previously undiscovered gap: `validatePanelistsAndConflict` (`interview.controller.ts`) only ever checked the PANELISTS for a double-booking at the exact scheduled timestamp -- it never looked at the candidate being interviewed at all. So a candidate active in two vacancies (or even two rounds of the same vacancy) could be scheduled into two interviews at the identical date/time with zero warning, since nothing on the candidate side was ever checked.

- **New `findCandidateScheduleConflict(candidateId, scheduledDate)`** (`interview.controller.ts`, next to `validatePanelistsAndConflict`): looks for any existing `Interview` belonging to the same candidate (via `application.candidateId`) whose slot has the identical `scheduledAt`. Same 409 shape as the panelist conflict, naming the candidate and the other vacancy they'd clash with.
- **`scheduleInterview`:** calls it right after computing `scheduledDate`, before the panelist check -- hard-blocks with a 409 same as any other scheduling conflict.
- **`addCandidatesToSlot`:** calls it per-candidate inside the existing loop, pushing into `failed` (not aborting the whole batch) -- consistent with how every other per-candidate validation in that loop already behaves.

Confirmed via code read that this genuinely was never checked anywhere else in the file (only fix, not a duplicate of existing behavior). No unit tests currently exercise either function directly (`grep` came back empty), so nothing to update there.

**Also answered, no code change:** the Reconsider button not showing on Noah Bennett is correct, not a bug -- Reconsider only renders when `stage === "REJECTED" && !hiringDecision` (Thirty-ninth pass). Noah's two applications are Shortlisted and Hired; neither is an early CV-screening rejection, so there's nothing to reconsider on either one.

**Verification:** backend `tsc --noEmit` unchanged (only the pre-existing, unrelated `hiringManager.controller.ts` error).

**Update -- actually tested, not just tsc'd.** Added `backend/src/__tests__/interviewScheduling.test.ts` (mocked-Prisma style, matching `interviewReminders.test.ts`) covering `scheduleInterview`: (1) a same-timestamp candidate conflict returns 409 naming the candidate and the other vacancy, and short-circuits before the panelist check or the `$transaction` create -- confirms it doesn't silently fall through; (2) a non-conflicting schedule reaches the actual create and returns 201 -- confirms the new check doesn't false-positive on legitimate scheduling. This sandbox's local `node_modules` (Windows-installed) can't run vitest at all here -- same root cause as the pre-existing `tsc` platform mismatch, but this time in vitest's own `rolldown`/`vite` native bindings, not the project's dependencies. Worked around by installing a throwaway vitest into `/tmp` (correct Linux bindings) and pointing it at a copy of the project's `vitest.config.ts` from that same throwaway location, since `vitest/config` resolves relative to wherever the config file importing it lives -- running it from the project's own directory kept pulling in the broken local copy regardless of which vitest binary invoked it. Ran the full backend suite this way: **52 passed, 1 failed** -- the failure (`cvExtraction.test.ts`, `DOMMatrix is not defined`) is `@napi-rs/canvas` missing a native binding for this sandbox, unrelated to this change and pre-existing (same native-binary-platform-mismatch category as the vitest/tsc issues above, not something introduced here).

**Answered the follow-up ("there's only one row for a candidate, how would HR know which vacancy they shortlisted, what about the other") -- premise checked against the actual code, and it's not accurate.** There are two separate rows already (US-13/BUG-03), and the existing design already answers both questions without any change needed:
- `CandidatesPage.tsx`'s table has a **Vacancy** column right next to every row -- Noah's two rows show "Backend Engineer" and "Product Designer" respectively, so which application a Shortlist/Reject click applies to is never ambiguous.
- The **Candidate ID** column intentionally shows the same `C-00XX` value across a person's multiple rows -- this is deliberate, not a collision: it's a visual signal that two rows belong to the same human, which is exactly useful in this scenario. Checked, not assumed, before treating it as correct.
- `handleShortlist`/`handleReject`/`handleReconsider` (`CandidateDetailPage.tsx`) call `updateApplicationStatus(row.id, ...)` -- `row.id` is the application id, never the candidate id, so acting on one application can never bleed into another.
- "What about the other": the **Applicant History** section on the Candidate Detail page already lists every other application for that candidate with its own stage -- HR viewing either of Noah's two applications sees the other one's status right there, no extra click needed. The duplicate-CV-match modal (Thirty-eighth/Fortieth pass) also names the other vacancy inline for the same reason.

The one real structural risk this scenario introduced was the scheduling-conflict gap fixed above -- everything else already worked as intended once traced through, so no further code change here.

## Viva-demo login: added a real-inbox seeded account, purely additive

User's concern: every seeded login uses `@altrium.com`, a fictional domain nobody owns a mailbox for. Fine for every other demo, but it means live Forgot Password in a viva can never show a real received reset email -- if a lecturer picks any existing account, there's nothing to open and show.

- **`backend/prisma/seed.ts`:** added an 8th `testUsers` entry, `{ name: "Demo Account (Viva)", email: "dulzxitzy@gmail.com", password: "password123", role: Role.HR, department: null, isActive: true }`, with a comment explaining why. Deliberately additive only -- `hr@altrium.com` itself was **not** repointed, since that login identifier is referenced throughout this project's own docs/screenshots and changing it would break those references for no benefit (the new account covers the same need without that cost).
- Requires the user to run `cd backend && npx tsx prisma/seed.ts` (or their usual seed command) themselves -- sandbox has no DB access.
- **Direct answer on "can any email be entered":** yes, the Forgot Password form accepts any typed string client-side. Server-side (`auth.controller.ts`'s `forgotPassword`), it does `prisma.user.findUnique({ where: { email } })` -- a real reset token/email is only generated and sent when that string exactly matches an existing `User.email`. The HTTP response is identical either way (`GENERIC_RESET_MESSAGE`) to prevent account enumeration, so the UI never reveals whether the address matched. Whether a lecturer can actually *see* an email arrive depends entirely on whether the address they pick has a real, checkable inbox -- not an app limitation, a seed-data limitation, which is exactly what the new account above fixes for a live demo.

**Verification:** backend `tsc --noEmit` unchanged (only the pre-existing, unrelated `hiringManager.controller.ts` error).

## Forty-fourth pass: approximate "Active Now" view on IT Admin's Users page

User's concern: "the concurrent user shouldnt HR be seeing all the users who are logged in active as of now because i dont see how the current logic trumps that." Checked first, not assumed: grepped the full Sprint 01 submission doc, product backlog, ERD, and both Manual Test Cases docs for "logged in" / "active session" / "concurrent user" -- no hits. This was never a documented requirement anywhere in this project's own docs. Also checked the schema and auth flow: auth is stateless JWT (`backend/src/utils/jwt.ts`, token kept in frontend `sessionStorage`) -- there is no server-side session table anywhere, so there is no way to answer "who is logged in right now" precisely. Put this choice to the user via `AskUserQuestion` rather than silently building or silently refusing; user chose to build an approximate view.

Built as an approximation, not a real session registry -- documented as such at every layer:

- **`schema.prisma`:** added `User.lastActiveAt DateTime?`, nullable, with a comment spelling out the approximation and the ~10-minute "active now" window.
- **`auth.middleware.ts`'s `requireAuth`:** on every authenticated request, fire-and-forget updates `lastActiveAt` to now -- throttled to once per 60s per user (in-memory `Map`, resets on server restart, harmless) so this doesn't write to the DB on every single request. Runs even if a later `requireRole` check 403s the request, since an authenticated request is still "activity." Wrapped in its own inner try/catch (separate from the JWT-verification try/catch) so a hypothetical failure here can never masquerade as an "Invalid or expired token" 401 on a valid token -- caught and fixed this exact risk in an early draft before finalizing.
- **`frontend/src/api/users.ts`:** `User` type gains `lastActiveAt: string | null`, with the same approximation caveat in comment.
- **`UsersPage.tsx`/`.css`:** new `isActiveNow(u)` helper (last-active within 10 minutes) and a local `relativeTime()` (no shared utility exists in this codebase for this -- confirmed via grep -- so replicated the same pattern `CandidatesPage.tsx` already uses locally, consistent with this project's existing per-page-utility convention). Added a new **Active Now** table column, deliberately separate from the existing **Status** column (`isActive` = account enabled/disabled by IT Admin -- a different concept; an account can be enabled but nobody's around, or briefly disabled mid-session) -- a live dot + text rather than a filled pill, so it visually reads as ephemeral rather than a stored account property. Edit modal's "Created:" line also now shows last-active status inline.

**Flagged to user, not silently decided: this was placed on IT Admin's Users page, not HR's.** User said "HR" in their message, but this project's existing role separation already puts all user-account visibility (Users list, Audit Logs) behind IT_ADMIN exclusively -- HR has no equivalent page anywhere to add this to without either duplicating the Users page under HR or widening HR's permissions into IT Admin's territory, neither of which was asked for. Went with the existing pattern rather than guessing; user should confirm whether IT Admin is an acceptable stand-in for "HR should see this," or whether they actually want a second, HR-facing view built.

**Requires migration:** `schema.prisma` changed -- user must run `npx prisma migrate dev` and `npx prisma generate` themselves before this works (sandbox has no DB access).

**Verification:** frontend `tsc -b` clean, exit 0. Backend `tsc --noEmit` clean except the pre-existing, unrelated `hiringManager.controller.ts:73` error -- also fixed an unrelated type-only issue found during this same verification pass: `interviewScheduling.test.ts`'s `makeRes()` helper had an implicit non-`any` return type that only `tsc --noEmit` (not vitest's esbuild transform, which skips type-checking) caught -- annotated the function `: any` since it's a deliberate minimal stand-in for Express's `Response`, not a full mock of it.

## Forty-fifth pass: "Active Now" moved from Users to System Monitoring; Users sorted inactive-last

User pushed back on the Forty-fourth pass placement: didn't want a per-row "Active Now" column cluttering the Users table -- wanted it on the System tab instead, alongside the page's existing "how healthy/busy is the system" signals (Server Load, Response Time, Concurrent Users).

- **`UsersPage.tsx`/`.css`:** removed the "Active Now" column, the `isActiveNow`/`relativeTime` helpers, and the edit modal's inline last-active line -- this page is back to pure account management (identity, role, enabled/disabled), same as before the Forty-fourth pass. `User.lastActiveAt` itself, the schema field, and the backend tracking middleware are untouched -- only this page's UI use of it moved.
- **Also fixed in the same pass: inactive users now sort to the bottom of the table**, not interleaved with active ones. `sortedUsers = [...users].sort((a,b) => Number(b.isActive) - Number(a.isActive))` -- JS's `Array.prototype.sort` is spec-guaranteed stable, so this only reorders across the active/inactive boundary and preserves whatever order the backend returned within each group.
- **`system.controller.ts`'s `getSystemMetrics`:** new `activeUsers` array in the response -- `prisma.user.findMany` filtered to `lastActiveAt` within the last 10 minutes, ordered most-recent-first. Deliberately kept as a **separate, differently-defined signal** from the page's existing `concurrentUsers` KPI (distinct AuditLog actors -- write actions only -- in the last 5 minutes): flagged in comments on both the controller and the frontend type that these two numbers can legitimately disagree, since `lastActiveAt` updates on every authenticated request (including plain reads) while AuditLog only fires on audited write actions. Didn't fold the new list into the existing KPI or silently redefine "Concurrent Users" -- that would have been a bigger, unrequested change than what was asked.
- **`frontend/src/api/system.ts`:** new `ActiveUser` type, `SystemMetrics.activeUsers: ActiveUser[]`.
- **`SystemPage.tsx`/`.css`:** new "Active Users" table (Name/Email/Role/Last Active) between the KPI tiles and the Backups section, reusing the existing `.sys-backup-table` styling rather than inventing new table CSS. Local `ROLE_LABELS`/`relativeTime` duplicated here rather than shared -- same per-page-utility convention already established elsewhere in this codebase (confirmed via grep in the Forty-fourth pass: no shared utility exists for either).

**Verification:** frontend `tsc -b` clean, exit 0. Backend `tsc --noEmit` clean except the pre-existing, unrelated `hiringManager.controller.ts:73` error.

User needs to demo Sprint 1 live and wanted the whole system wiped and reseeded from scratch, with three concrete problems fixed along the way: role-labelled emails (hr@altrium.com etc.) read as an obvious test fixture in front of an audience; every account sharing the password "password123" triggers Chrome/Google's leaked-password warning mid-demo (it's one of the most common passwords in public breach corpora); and seeded candidates' CVs 404 when clicked.

Every candidate gets a real generated CV via `saveFile()`, not a phantom filename.

**Not fixed:** `seed-full-demo.ts`'s own CV bug is still there -- out of scope since the user isn't running that script for the viva, flagged here so it isn't mistaken for fixed if it's reused later.

Read `followUp.controller.ts` in full rather than guessing at what would populate each section -- it computes five independent lists on every request (nothing stored as "pending"): Pending CV Review (APPLIED applications), Pending Feedback (a past interview where at least one assigned panelist hasn't submitted), Interview Invites - Candidates / - Interviewers (round 2+ interviews where the manual invite was never sent -- only round 1 auto-sends), and Upcoming Calls (future-dated interviews). - **4 new vacancies, one per remaining canonical department:** HR Business Partner (HR), Senior Financial Analyst (Finance and Accounting), Operations Manager (Operations), Corporate Counsel (Legal) -- alongside the existing Backend Engineer (IT), Content Marketing Specialist (Marketing), Account Executive (Sales), Customer Success Manager (Customer Service). Elias Vogt (Backend Engineer) is advanced to a scheduled round-2 Final Interview with the invite not yet sent -- Interview Invites - Candidates AND - Interviewers (same interview covers both, since neither the candidate nor the panelists were notified).
