## Fifty-eighth pass: Multer error-message fix, Selenium credential rebuild, Management calendar seeding + display/drill-down fixes

**Oversized-CV-upload error message fixed.** User uploaded a 5MB test PDF and got the generic "Something went wrong. Please try again." instead of a size-specific message. Root cause: `multer`'s own upload-limit errors (`MulterError`, e.g. `LIMIT_FILE_SIZE`) are thrown by the upload middleware itself, before any controller code runs, so they bypass every controller's try/catch and land straight in `app.ts`'s last-resort global error handler, which had no special-casing. Fixed by adding a `multer.MulterError` branch there (checked `err.code` for `LIMIT_FILE_SIZE` and `LIMIT_FILE_COUNT`, generic fallback otherwise) ahead of the generic 500. Not yet re-tested by the user.

**Selenium suite's hardcoded login credentials were stale and would have failed live.** Found proactively (not reported by the user) while working through the automated-test demo: `automated-tests/selenium/helpers.py`'s `ACCOUNTS`/`PASSWORD` constants still pointed at an old roster (`hr@altrium.com` / `password123`) that stopped existing once `prisma/seed.ts` was rewritten with real per-person names/passwords (Forty-fifth-ish pass onward -- `sharon@altrium.com` / `Sharon@2026`, etc.). Every Selenium test would have failed at login if run as-is. Fixed: `helpers.py` gained an `ACCOUNT_PASSWORDS` map (real email -> real password) and `login_as()` now looks a password up there, falling back to the old `PASSWORD` constant only for an email not in the map -- chosen so no call site elsewhere needed to change. `sprint1/test_login.py` and `sprint2/test_it_admin_users.py` had every direct `send_keys(PASSWORD)` replaced with the correct per-account lookup; `test_login.py`'s stale "Priya Fernando" docstring (leftover from the old roster) corrected to "Rachel Kim". Verified via grep afterward that no `send_keys(PASSWORD)` or stale hardcoded email remains anywhere in the suite -- not verified by actually running it (sandbox dead all session). User needs to run the suite once themselves before the viva.

**Management calendars were empty (no red dates) for 7 of 8 departments.** Root cause, found by reading `getUpcomingInterviews`/`buildUpcomingInterviewsData` in `management.controller.ts`: it only shows interviews with `scheduledAt >= now`, and while every department's Management account was already added to their vacancy's interviewer pool (`ensurePoolMember` in `seed-viva-demo.ts`), only Marketing's Bianca actually had a *scheduled* interview -- and that one was deliberately dated in the past for a different demo purpose (Email History). New standalone script, `backend/scripts/seed-management-calendars.ts`, added rather than editing `seed-viva-demo.ts` directly (smaller blast radius, nothing to test either way with the sandbox dead). For each of the 8 Management accounts it finds their pooled vacancy, takes the final round (highest `VacancyStage.order`), picks a `SHORTLISTED` candidate, and creates/reschedules an `InterviewSlot` + `Interview` a few business days out with the Management account as a panelist -- idempotent (reschedules only if stale, adds the panelist only if missing, never duplicates). User ran it and confirmed red dates now appear.

**Two follow-up display/interaction fixes on that same calendar**, from screenshots the user posted after running the script:

1. The day-detail panel was showing the vacancy's literal stage name ("Backend Engineer - Round 3") instead of a correct final-round label. Fixed server-side, not by hardcoding a label against a specific stage name (every department names its final round differently) -- `buildUpcomingInterviewsData` now includes each application's `vacancy.stages`, computes that vacancy's own max `order`, and returns `round.isFinal: boolean` (true when this slot's stage order equals that max). Frontend (`MyInterviewsPage.tsx`, Management) now renders `"Final Interview"` when `isFinal`, otherwise the stage's real name (not the old generic "Round N"), with HR's optional free-text `roundLabel` appended when set -- same convention as Follow Ups elsewhere.
2. Time slots weren't clickable and had no richer detail view; user wanted them to match the Interviewer role's own My Interviews calendar (`frontend/src/pages/interviewer/MyInterviewsPage.tsx`), which opens a second panel on click: full date/time, a "Panel" list of panelist names, and a "Candidates" line with feedback-submission status. Ported that same two-level drill-down into Management's page. Required extending the backend response too -- `buildUpcomingInterviewsData` now also includes `slot.panelists` (with `user.name`) and, mirroring `listMyInterviews`'s exact semantics, this viewer's own `Feedback` row for the interview (`feedbackSubmitted: boolean`) -- meaningful for Management specifically because `feedback.routes.ts` already lets `MANAGEMENT` submit/edit feedback via `PATCH /feedback/:id` (Management attends final rounds under the existing enforced rule). `frontend/src/api/management.ts`'s `UpcomingInterviewRow` type extended with `round.isFinal`, `panelists[]`, `feedbackSubmitted`. CSS: `.mi-slot-row` changed from a static `div` to a clickable button (hover state added), plus new `.mi-section-heading`/`.mi-plain-list` classes ported from the Interviewer page's stylesheet under the `mi-` prefix.

**Verification:** none possible -- sandbox wedged the entire session (13 consecutive Bash failures at the point of this entry, explicitly told not to retry further). All four changes above (Multer handler, Selenium credentials, seed script, Management calendar backend+frontend) are grounded in actual code/schema reads (confirmed `Interview.feedback` relation, `Vacancy.stages` relation, `feedback.routes.ts`'s role gate) but unverified by `tsc` or execution. User must run `npx tsc --noEmit` in both `backend/` and `frontend/`, and click through the Management calendar live, before relying on this in the viva.

## Fifty-ninth pass: full 18-table ERD rebuild + feedback edit-history surfaced on Hiring Manager screens

**ERD file (`docs/Altrium_HR_ERD_Corrected.drawio`) rebuilt from scratch, same filename, full 18-model scope.** The existing file (audited "correct" in an earlier pass, before this session) was stale specifically because that audit predated the Interview/InterviewSlot schema split -- its `Interview` box still carried the old `applicationId`/`vacancyStageId`/`scheduledAt` shape directly, no `InterviewSlot` box existed at all, and `InterviewPanelist` pointed at `interviewId` instead of `slotId`. User was offered a choice (script-matching 13-table scope vs. every model in `schema.prisma`) and picked the full 18. Every `[PK]`/`[FK]`/nullable/enum/`@@unique`/`@@index` annotation was pulled directly from `schema.prisma`, not carried over from the old file. Deliberately used plain `[PK]`/`[FK]` bracket notation instead of the original's angle-bracket `<PK>` style -- traced through drawio's HTML-in-XML escaping and concluded the original's single-escaped `&lt;PK&gt;` most likely renders as an invisible unknown HTML tag rather than visible text (a probable latent bug in the original, never caught because a content-level "audit" of the doc wouldn't have caught a rendering-only defect). Brackets carry no such risk. Not independently rendered/screenshotted to confirm -- no draw.io renderer available in this session (sandbox dead) -- user should open it once in the actual app before relying on it.

**Real gap found and fixed: feedback edits were captured but invisible anywhere in the UI.** User edited a piece of interview feedback live (screenshot: Interviewer's Feedback page, "Reason for change" field) and asked who would ever see that it was edited. Investigation found `updateFeedback` (`feedback.controller.ts`) correctly writes a `FeedbackAuditLog` row on every edit (previous/new score, previous/new comments, reason, editor, timestamp) -- but nothing consumed it: IT Admin's Audit Logs page doesn't show it (edits never write to the general-purpose `AuditLog` table, only `FeedbackAuditLog` -- no `FEEDBACK_EDITED` action type exists), and the dedicated `GET /feedback/:id/audit-log` endpoint (`listFeedbackAuditLog`) had zero frontend callers anywhere in the codebase despite being fully built and auth-gated.

User asked to check the Sprint 01 submission doc for the documented access rule before fixing -- that file isn't present in this repo/session (searched, not found; likely only ever existed as an upload in an earlier session), so the fix is grounded directly in `feedback.controller.ts` instead, which is authoritative anyway. Confirmed rule (`listFeedbackForInterview`/`listFeedbackForVacancy`'s `scopeToSelf` check): INTERVIEWER, MANAGEMENT, and HIRING_MANAGER (the three panelist-capable roles) are each self-scoped to their own feedback only, never a peer panelist's; HR/IT_ADMIN/LEADERSHIP_MANAGEMENT see everyone's. Separately, the only place feedback *content* (scores/comments) is actually rendered anywhere in the frontend today is `feedbackHistory` on the Hiring Manager's Pending Decisions modal and Candidate Decision drill-down page (`hiringManager.controller.ts`, US-25) -- HR's own Candidate Detail page doesn't show feedback scores at all currently. So the edit-history feature was wired into those same two existing HM screens rather than a new page, which also means its real-world visibility today is: Hiring Manager (for applications on vacancies they're assigned to) only.

Implementation: `hiringManager.controller.ts`'s two `feedbackHistory` builders (`getPendingDecisionsForHm`, `getApplicationForDecision`) now include `feedback.auditLog` (id-only select) and expose `feedbackId`/`edited: boolean` per entry. `frontend/src/api/feedback.ts` gained `getFeedbackAuditLog()` wiring up the previously-dead endpoint, plus a `FeedbackAuditLogEntry` type. `CandidateDecisionPage.tsx` and `PendingDecisionsPage.tsx` both got an "Edited - view history" toggle per feedback entry (only rendered when `edited` is true) that expands a fetched-on-demand panel showing every edit's previous/new score, previous/new comments, reason, editor, and timestamp. New CSS classes (`cd-edited-toggle`/`cd-audit-log`/etc., `pd-` equivalents) added to both pages' existing stylesheets, matching their established naming conventions.

**Verification:** none possible -- sandbox still wedged. Traced the Prisma relation (`Feedback.auditLog`) and both controller functions' exact object shapes by hand rather than assuming, and re-read both edited `.tsx` files afterward to confirm JSX brace/tag structure was correct, but this is unverified by `tsc` or a live click-through. User needs to verify by editing a feedback entry as an interviewer, then checking Pending Decisions / Candidate Decision as the assigned Hiring Manager for that application before demoing it live.

## Sixtieth pass: feedback edit-history extended to the editor's own view; real data-leak found and fixed in the same endpoint

**Follow-up gap, caught by the user, not by me:** after the Fifty-ninth pass shipped edit-history visibility to the two Hiring Manager screens, user asked "shouldn't the interviewer who edited see it too?" -- correct catch. The interviewer/panelist who actually made an edit had no way to see their own edit history: `FeedbackPage.tsx` (the same component used by both `/interviewer/candidates/:id/feedback` and `/management/candidates/:id/feedback` routes) silently saved an edit and navigated away, with nothing surfaced back to the editor. Fixed by extending the identical pattern to that page: `load()` now fetches `getFeedbackAuditLog(mine.id)` in parallel (non-blocking -- a failure there doesn't break the form itself) whenever existing feedback is found, and a "Previously edited N times - view history" toggle was added inside the edit-mode section, right after the "Reason for change" field, only rendered once the fetch resolves with a non-empty array. No extra access check was needed: `getMyFeedbackForInterview` is already self-scoped server-side, so whatever feedback ID reaches this page is always the current user's own. New CSS (`fb-edited-toggle`/`fb-audit-log`/`fb-audit-entry`/`fb-audit-meta`) added to `FeedbackPage.css` matching the `cd-`/`pd-` classes from the prior pass.

**Real bug found while re-verifying the endpoint this wiring depends on:** `listFeedbackAuditLog` (`feedback.controller.ts`) was built with `include: { editedBy: true }` -- an unscoped Prisma include that returns the *entire* `User` row, including `passwordHash` and `resetTokenHash`, in the JSON response. This existed before this session's changes but was harmless in practice because the endpoint had zero frontend callers until the Fifty-ninth pass. It is not harmless now: it is actively wired into three screens as of these last two passes, so every edit-history fetch would have shipped every editor's password hash and reset-token hash straight to the browser. Fixed by narrowing the include to `select: { id: true, name: true, email: true }`, matching the shape the frontend `FeedbackAuditLogEntry` type already declared and actually uses. This should have been caught during the Fifty-ninth pass and wasn't -- flagging that gap plainly rather than glossing over it.

**Verification:** none possible -- sandbox still wedged for the entire session. Confirmed the leak by reading `schema.prisma`'s `User` model directly (`passwordHash`, `resetTokenHash` both present, both plain `String`/`String?`, no `@sensitive`-style guard in Prisma to catch this class of mistake automatically) rather than assuming. Re-read the full edited `FeedbackPage.tsx`/`FeedbackPage.css` afterward for structural correctness. User should verify by: (1) editing a feedback entry as an interviewer, reopening the same Feedback page, confirming the "Previously edited N times" toggle now appears and expands correctly; (2) opening the browser's network tab on that request and confirming the response no longer contains `passwordHash`/`resetTokenHash` fields for `editedBy`.

## Sixty-first pass: SCRUM2-30's real gap fixed (CV-discard on duplicate match); fuzzy matching deliberately deferred, scoped as its own future story

**The dilemma, restated precisely:** the viva prompted a fresh look at duplicate-candidate detection (SCRUM2-30, "warn, don't block"). User raised a real scenario the existing design didn't handle: a candidate re-applies years later with an updated CV under the same email. Reading `confirmCvUpload` (`candidate.controller.ts`) confirmed the exact gap: on an email match, the code's own comment said the newly-uploaded CV "is left in storage unused" -- true, and the actual bug. The file was never deleted, but nothing ever exposed it back to HR. It just sat there, permanently unreachable, even though it might carry updated contact details or a materially different CV.

**Two options were laid out, not one:** (1) surface and act on the discarded CV -- no schema change, contained scope; (2) fuzzy matching on name+phone to catch the *other* failure mode (same person, changed email, so no exact match ever fires at all). These are different problems -- (1) is a data-loss bug on an already-detected duplicate, (2) is a detection gap for duplicates that are never detected in the first place. User asked for a recommendation; given viva timing at the time, Option 1 was recommended first: small, well-understood, no design unknowns. Fuzzy matching would need a similarity threshold, false-positive handling (common names), and a real HR-facing resolution UI (compare/merge/dismiss) -- meaningfully more scope and risk, and better suited to its own story once designed properly rather than folded into this fix.

**Post-viva follow-up:** user asked, now that the viva pressure is off, whether to build Option 1 first or go straight for fuzzy matching "assuming Option 1 is also created" inside it. Pushed back on that framing: building the fuzzy resolution UI first and assuming it subsumes Option 1 is optimistic scope-bundling, not a real plan -- fuzzy matching doesn't exist yet in any form (no similarity function, no threshold, no persisted "not a duplicate, stop asking" state, which would need a schema change unlike Option 1). Sequencing was reaffirmed: ship Option 1 now as its own complete fix, scope fuzzy matching as a separate, later SCRUM2 story once its design (especially the persisted-dismissal question) is actually worked out.

**What was built (Option 1, `candidate.controller.ts` + `candidate.routes.ts` + frontend `CandidatesPage.tsx`/`api/candidates.ts`):**
- `getPendingCv(fileId)` (`GET /candidates/pending-cv/:fileId`, HR-only): serves the still-unattached new CV a duplicate match left in storage, so HR can actually look at it. Trusts `fileId` alone (a server-generated `randomUUID().pdf`, never guessable, only ever handed back to the HR session that produced it) -- same trust boundary `extractCvFiles`/`confirmCvUpload` already use for pre-confirm files. Confirmed this can't leak an attached candidate's real CV: once a file is attached (on create or via replace, below) it's renamed to `${candidateId}_name_...pdf`, so a bare `<uuid>.pdf` on disk is always either mid-review or an orphaned matched-duplicate, never a live candidate record's CV.
- `replaceCandidateCv(id)` (`POST /candidates/:id/replace-cv`, HR-only): the actual fix. Given the matched `fileId`, renames it to the final filename, updates the existing `Candidate.cvUrl` (and `name`/`phoneNumber` if the new extraction differed), deletes the now-superseded old CV file (best-effort -- a cleanup failure doesn't fail the request, the DB update already succeeded), and writes a new `CV_REPLACED` audit action (added to `AuditAction`/`EVENT_TYPE`/`describeAction` in `auditLog.ts`/`auditLog.controller.ts`, categorized under "Candidate Management" alongside `CV_UPLOADED`). No schema change -- every field touched already existed and was already editable via `updateCandidate`.
- Frontend: the duplicate-match modal (`CandidatesPage.tsx`) now carries `candidateId`/`fileId` through both push sites in `handleConfirmAndApply` (the newly-applied match and the already-applied-to-this-vacancy 409 case), and each match row gets "View new CV" (opens the PDF in a new tab via a blob URL, same auth pattern as the existing CV viewer) and "Replace existing CV with this one" buttons, swapped for a plain "CV on file has been replaced" note once done. `confirmCvUpload`'s in-code comment was corrected to stop saying the file is "left... unused" now that it's reachable.

**Answering the user's direct follow-up on ERD impact:** confirmed before answering, not assumed -- Option 1 touches zero tables, fields, or relationships (`cvUrl`, `name`, `phoneNumber` all pre-existed on `Candidate`). The already-finalized 18-table ERD (`Altrium_HR_ERD_Corrected.drawio`) and the Activity/Use Case diagram corrections are unaffected and don't need to pause or wait on this or any other Option-1-shaped change. Fuzzy matching, if and when built, is the one that could touch the ERD (a persisted "dismissed as not-a-duplicate" state would likely need a new field or small table) -- flagged now so it isn't a surprise later.

**Verification:** none possible -- sandbox still wedged for the entire session (confirmed still broken again this pass, identical Windows-update RPC error). No `tsc` run, no manual click-through. Read every touched file in full before and after editing (`candidate.controller.ts`, `candidate.routes.ts`, `auditLog.ts`, `auditLog.controller.ts`, `CandidatesPage.tsx`, `api/candidates.ts`, `CandidatesPage.css`) rather than assuming the edits composed correctly. User should verify by: (1) uploading a CV whose email already matches an existing candidate, confirming the duplicate modal now shows "View new CV" / "Replace existing CV with this one" per match; (2) clicking "View new CV" and confirming the correct (new) PDF opens, not the old one; (3) clicking "Replace," confirming the toggle becomes "CV on file has been replaced," then opening that candidate's detail page and confirming `downloadCv` now serves the new PDF; (4) checking Audit Logs for a new "Replaced the CV on file for &lt;name&gt;" entry under Candidate Management.

## Sixty-second pass: two real bugs the user found by actually testing the Sixty-first pass's fix; duplicate-match modal replaced with a persisted per-candidate banner

**Context: viva is over, this is now genuine Sprint 2 QA, not demo prep.** User tested the Sixty-first pass's fix live (uploaded a real CV, created a candidate, rejected it, then re-uploaded to trigger the matched-duplicate path) and found two real problems the modal-based design didn't survive contact with actual use, plus asked a sharp design question about when the warning should fire at all.

**Bug 1: the duplicate-match modal couldn't survive navigation.** Clicking "View their profile" cleared `duplicateMatches` immediately (its own `onClick` did this) and navigated away; coming back to the Candidates list had nothing left to show, even though the underlying situation (an unresolved new CV) was still real. Root cause wasn't really "state got lost" -- it's that the modal was pure ephemeral React state tied to one upload action, with nothing in the database remembering "this candidate has a pending CV to review." It was never going to survive a page transition.

**Bug 2 (the bigger one, caught while testing bug 1): the modal fired on every email match regardless of whether the CV had actually changed.** User uploaded the *same* CV to a *third*, brand-new vacancy and got warned again ("Freddie Sandhu already has a candidate profile and has also applied to HR Intern, Marketing Inter") -- correctly identified this as noise: a person re-applying to a different vacancy with an unchanged CV isn't a duplicate-data problem at all, `CandidateApplication` already handles "one person, many applications" correctly. Initially wondered whether to remove the same-vacancy uniqueness check entirely -- corrected that this is a different, older, correct feature (`CandidateApplication`'s `@@unique([candidateId, vacancyId])`, "can't apply to the same vacancy twice") and is not the source of the noise; talked through it and landed on the real fix instead: only warn when the file content actually differs, not just when the email matches.

**Design decided:** email stays the sole identity key (candidate finds you exist; is not itself worth a warning). Whether an email match is worth telling HR about now depends on comparing the new upload's bytes against what's already on file:
- **Identical hash** -> silently link the application to the existing candidate, exactly like nothing happened. No flag, no banner, no popup. The redundant duplicate file is deleted from storage instead of accumulating forever.
- **Different hash** -> still silently links the application (email is the identity key, not a second Candidate row), but now `Candidate.pendingCvFileId` is set -- a real, persisted flag, not upload-response-only state.

**Modal replaced with a persisted profile-page banner**, per user's explicit "yes build this" to that exact framing. `CandidateDetailPage.tsx` now reads `pendingCvFileId` straight off the fetched candidate record and shows a standing banner ("A new CV was uploaded for this candidate and hasn't been reviewed yet") for as long as it's set -- survives navigation, reloads, logging out, coming back next week, whatever. Three actions: View new CV (unchanged from the Sixty-first pass's endpoint), Replace existing CV with this one (clears the flag + swaps `cvUrl`/`name`/`phoneNumber`), and a new **Dismiss (keep current CV)** action -- added because without it the banner could only ever go away by replacing, which felt incomplete; dismissing just clears the flag via a new `POST /candidates/:id/dismiss-pending-cv` endpoint, touching nothing else.

**Schema:** `Candidate.pendingCvFileId String?` (nullable, no default). Deliberately just a single fileId pointer, not a full history -- see the deferred follow-up below.

**Backend (`candidate.controller.ts`):** `confirmCvUpload`'s response shape changed -- `matched` (now only genuinely-different-CV cases, sets `pendingCvFileId`) sits alongside a new `linked` array (identical-hash re-applies, silently attached, counted separately from `created`/`matched` in the response). Hash comparison reads both files off disk (`getFile` on the existing `cvUrl` and the new `fileId`) and SHA-256s them -- cheap, no new storage needed, byte-exact (not semantic: a CV re-exported from Word with an identical body but a different embedded timestamp would still register as "different" and warn -- acceptable, still strictly better than warning on literally everything). `replaceCandidateCv` now also clears `pendingCvFileId` on replace. New `dismissPendingCv` clears it without touching anything else.

**Frontend:** `CandidatesPage.tsx`'s entire `duplicateMatches` modal, its state, its handlers (`handleViewPendingCv`/`handleReplacePendingCv`), and its CSS (`.cnd-duplicate-*`) are gone. `handleConfirmAndApply` now applies `created`/`linked`/`matched` candidates to the vacancy uniformly (a shared `applyOrIgnoreDuplicate` helper that treats "already applied to this vacancy" as a silent no-op, not a failure), and the post-upload toast gets one extra sentence when any `matched` (genuinely-different-CV) entries occurred, pointing HR at the affected profile(s) instead of blocking with a popup. `CandidateDetailPage.tsx` gained the banner + its three handlers + new CSS (`.cnd-pending-cv-*`, replacing `.cnd-duplicate-cv-*`/`.cnd-duplicate-modal` in `CandidatesPage.css`, which `CandidateDetailPage.tsx` already imports).

**Deferred, logged as a follow-up rather than built now: a real multi-version CV history.** User's original ask was closer to "current CV, plus a 'past CVs' link below it, only shown if any exist" -- a genuine version history, not just a single pending-vs-current swap. That needs a new table (`CandidateCvHistory(candidateId, cvUrl, replacedAt, replacedByUserId)`, written to on every replace instead of deleting the superseded file, with the candidate page showing "Current CV" plus a conditional "Past CVs (N)" expandable list) and its own UI surface -- bigger than this fix, and explicitly not folded in now per user's own call ("remove this... put this as a next task"), given how deep into Sprint 2 this project already is. Recorded here so it isn't lost, not because it isn't worth doing.

**User also asked to remove the vacancies and candidate she created while testing this.** No DB access this session (sandbox still wedged) and vacancies specifically can't be hard-deleted through the UI at all -- that was a deliberate earlier design choice (US-22 candidate-record retention; "Delete Vacancy" was renamed to "Close Vacancy" for exactly this reason, see the wireframe-review entries above). User confirmed the three test vacancies (HR Intern / HR, Marketing Intern / Marketing, Finance Intern / Finance and Accounting) and that Freddie Sandhu was the only candidate on any of them. New `backend/scripts/delete-vacancy-by-title.ts`, modeled on the existing `delete-candidate-by-email.ts`'s dependency-order-inside-one-transaction pattern (FeedbackAuditLog -> Feedback -> Interview -> InterviewPanelist -> InterviewSlot -> ApplicationStageHistory -> StageRecommendation -> CandidateApplication -> VacancyInterviewer -> InterviewPanelMember -> InterviewPanel -> VacancyStage -> Vacancy), grounded directly in `schema.prisma`'s actual relation names and cascade annotations (confirmed `VacancyStage`/`InterviewPanelMember` cascade automatically on their parent, `VacancyInterviewer`/`InterviewPanel`/`CandidateApplication` do not, so those still need explicit deletion first). Matches vacancies by a case-insensitive `contains` on title rather than an exact match, since the real stored title for the Marketing one wasn't independently confirmed byte-for-byte (the UI consistently rendered it as "Marketing Inter" across two separate screenshots) -- defaults to a dry run that only prints what it matched, and only deletes once re-run with `--yes`, specifically so a title mismatch can't silently delete the wrong vacancy. Run order given to the user: `npx tsx scripts/delete-candidate-by-email.ts freddie.sandhu@example.com` first, then `npx tsx scripts/delete-vacancy-by-title.ts "HR Intern" "Marketing Intern" "Finance Intern"` (dry run), then the same command with `--yes` once the printed list looks right.

**Verification:** none possible -- sandbox still wedged, identical Windows-update error on retry. No `tsc`, no manual click-through, no migration run. Read every touched file in full (`schema.prisma`, `candidate.controller.ts`, `candidate.routes.ts`, `api/candidates.ts`, `CandidatesPage.tsx`, `CandidatesPage.css`, `CandidateDetailPage.tsx`) before and after editing rather than assuming composition was correct; specifically double-checked the `applyCandidateToVacancy` 409 response shape (`existingApplicationId`) against `application.controller.ts` directly rather than trusting memory, since the old duplicate-match catch logic depended on that exact field. User needs to run a real migration (`npx prisma migrate dev --name add_pending_cv_file_id` or equivalent) before any of this works, then verify: (1) uploading an unchanged CV to a new vacancy produces no banner anywhere; (2) uploading a genuinely different CV under an existing email shows the banner on that candidate's profile, and that it's still there after navigating away and back; (3) View/Replace/Dismiss each behave as described and the banner clears correctly after each.

## Sixty-third pass: two more real bugs from live testing -- silent no-op on same-vacancy re-upload; Shortlist/Reject reachable with zero review note

**User ran the Sixty-second pass's migration and cleanup scripts for real** (`npx prisma migrate dev`, `delete-candidate-by-email.ts`, `delete-vacancy-by-title.ts` dry run) -- first actual execution any of this session's work has had. Confirmed via her pasted terminal output that the dry run only matched `"HR Intern "` (id 1, note the trailing space in the real stored title) and `"Finance Intern"` (id 3) -- **not** the Marketing one. This confirms the earlier suspicion directly: the real stored title is `"Marketing Inter"`, not `"Marketing Intern"` -- the script's `contains: "Marketing Intern"` filter can't match a shorter stored string. Told her to re-run the dry run with `"Marketing Inter"` (or just `"Marketing"`) added before the `--yes` pass, rather than silently guessing and deleting the wrong thing.

**Real bug 1: re-uploading a candidate's CV against a vacancy they're already on produced total silence.** User uploaded the same CV to the same vacancy again as a test -- the confirm step correctly did nothing (right behavior: identical hash -> `linked`, then `applyCandidateToVacancy` 409s because they're already on this vacancy, correctly not counted as a failure) but the upload modal just closed with zero feedback of any kind, "nothing changes nor anything." Traced this precisely: `successCount` stayed 0 (nothing new), `failReasons` stayed empty (nothing actually failed), so the toast logic's `if (successCount > 0)` branch never fired and nothing else covered the "everything in this batch was already-applied" case -- a real regression introduced by the Sixty-second pass's modal removal, which correctly dropped the noisy warning for this case but didn't replace it with any positive confirmation that the no-op was intentional and not a broken button. Fixed: `applyOrIgnoreDuplicate` now increments a `noopCount` on that specific 409 instead of just swallowing it, and the toast logic gets a new branch -- when there were zero successes and zero failures but at least one no-op, show "No changes made -- this candidate has already applied to this vacancy" (or the plural form) instead of silently closing with nothing.

**Real bug 2 (new ask, not a follow-up of anything already tracked): HR could Shortlist or Reject a CV-review decision without ever writing down why.** Review Notes (`Candidate.lastCvReviewNote`) has existed since early in the project as an optional field HR could fill in while screening a CV, but nothing ever required it before the Shortlist/Reject buttons on the CV-review screen became clickable -- a real decision with zero recorded reasoning was always possible. Fixed at both layers, not just the UI:
- **Backend (`application.controller.ts`'s `updateApplicationStatus`):** when `existing.stage === "APPLIED"` (i.e. this is the actual first CV-screening decision, not the separate Reconsider path, which starts from `REJECTED` and already went through this check once), looks up the candidate's `lastCvReviewNote` and returns 400 ("Write and save a CV review note before shortlisting or rejecting this candidate.") if it's empty/unset. Checked against the *saved* field, not anything from the request body -- there's no way to pass a note through this endpoint that would bypass actually saving one via the existing `PATCH /candidates/:id` (`saveCandidateReviewNote`) flow first.
- **Frontend (`CandidateDetailPage.tsx`):** new `hasSavedReviewNote` derived from `candidateDetail.lastCvReviewNote`, mirroring the backend check the same way `isVacancyOnHold` already mirrors `assertVacancyNotOnHold` -- this is a UI convenience, not the real enforcement. Reject/Shortlist buttons disabled with an explanatory `title` when there's no saved note, plus a visible inline hint line above them (reusing the existing `.cnd-locked-hint` class already used for the on-hold/final-decision messages elsewhere on this page) so it isn't only discoverable by trying to click a disabled button.

**Verification:** none possible for the two fixes above -- sandbox still wedged, identical error. Confirmed the review-note gate's scope precisely against `updateApplicationStatus`'s existing `isReconsider` branch before writing it, specifically to make sure Reconsider (which also calls this same endpoint, with `status: "SHORTLISTED"`) wouldn't get wrongly blocked -- it starts from `existing.stage === "REJECTED"`, not `"APPLIED"`, so the new check's condition correctly excludes it without needing a separate carve-out. User should verify: (1) a fresh APPLIED candidate with no saved note shows both buttons disabled with the hint visible; (2) saving a note (not just typing one) enables them; (3) re-uploading a CV against a vacancy the candidate's already on now shows the "No changes made" toast instead of silent nothing; (4) the corrected `delete-vacancy-by-title.ts` dry run actually matches all three test vacancies once "Marketing Inter" is added to the argument list, before re-running with `--yes`.

## Sixty-fourth pass: informal "--" punctuation removed sitewide; SCRUM2-30 duplicate-CV flow rebuilt a third time, on the user's explicit choice, reversing part of the Sixty-second pass

**Part 1 -- punctuation cleanup.** User asked to remove the informal " -- " double-hyphen style from actual user-facing text (not code comments, which use it constantly and were left alone) and reword each hit more professionally. Scoped via four successive Grep passes (raw count, quoted-string-specific, non-comment-line-start, setter-call-specific) across the frontend, plus a follow-up pass over backend `error:` strings that also reach the UI verbatim through `err.message`. Fixed, all replaced with a period or colon depending on the sentence: `ResetPasswordPage.tsx`, `CandidatesPage.tsx` (the two pending-review/no-op toast fragments built in the Sixty-third pass), `CandidateDetailPage.tsx` (on-hold and not-yet-shortlisted locked-hints), four near-identical "no department set" messages across `management/{ReportsPage,CandidateProgressPage,DepartmentVacanciesPage,MyInterviewsPage}.tsx`, and six backend `error:` strings in `app.ts` (Multer file-count limit), `candidate.controller.ts` (x2, "CV no longer available"), `application.controller.ts` (x2, on-hold and HM-assign-stage-gate), `interview.controller.ts` (x2, on-hold), and `interviewPanel.controller.ts` (ineligible-panelist message). All comment-only "--" usage (the large majority of hits) was deliberately left untouched -- that's this codebase's normal comment style throughout the whole project, not something the user asked to change.

**Part 2 -- the CV-comparison redesign question, and why this reverses the Sixty-second pass.** User's screenshot showed the "No changes made -- this candidate has already applied to this vacancy" toast (the Sixty-third pass's fix) and asked directly whether the backend actually compared CV content for that case. Answer: yes -- SHA-256 hash-compared, found byte-identical, which is exactly why it silently took the `linked` (no-op) path rather than flagging anything. User then proposed a materially bigger flow instead: inform HR the candidate applied before, offer a CV comparison, ask whether to add the candidate with the new CV, and show CV history -- which is functionally the interactive duplicate-match modal the Sixty-second pass deliberately removed (for firing on every match including byte-identical noise), plus the full multi-version CV history table that same pass explicitly logged as a deferred follow-up rather than building. Flagged this tension directly rather than silently picking a side, and asked the user via a three-way choice (keep the current no-op-toast-plus-link approach / full rebuild reversing the Sixty-second pass / a narrower middle ground that just links the no-op toast to the existing application without a history table). **User chose the full rebuild.**

**What actually changed, backend:**
- **Schema:** `CandidateCvVersion` model added (`id`, `candidateId` FK cascade-delete, `cvUrl`, `uploadedAt`, `vacancyTitle` -- append-only, never edited/deleted, matching the US-22 retention posture used everywhere else). `Candidate` gained `pendingCvIsIdentical Boolean?` (informational -- no longer decides anything server-side) and `pendingCvVacancyId Int?` / `pendingCvVacancy` relation to `Vacancy` (which vacancy the pending upload was originally for, so resolving it can also apply the candidate to that vacancy in one action). `Vacancy` gained the inverse `pendingCvCandidates Candidate[]`.
- **`confirmCvUpload`:** the Sixty-second pass's "identical hash -> silently `linked`, no flag" shortcut is gone. Every existing-email match, identical or not, becomes a pending decision now -- `identical` is still hash-computed and returned, but purely as information for the banner to show ("this looks the same" vs "this is different"), not as a branch that skips HR review. Response shape simplified from `{linked, matched}` to a single `duplicates` array carrying `identical` per entry. Nothing is auto-applied to the requested vacancy for a duplicate anymore -- that decision moves to resolution time. Also now writes the first `CandidateCvVersion` row on every brand-new candidate create.
- **`replaceCandidateCv`/`dismissPendingCv`:** both gained an optional `applyToVacancy`/`vacancyId` pair -- resolving "use new CV" or "keep current CV" can now also add the candidate to the vacancy the upload was for (defaulting to `pendingCvVacancyId` if the caller doesn't pass one explicitly), sharing a small `applyToVacancyIfRequested` helper that mirrors `applyCandidateToVacancy`'s P2002-as-"already applied" handling rather than duplicating an Express handler. `replaceCandidateCv` also now writes a new `CandidateCvVersion` row (the CV actually changed); `dismissPendingCv` does not (it didn't).
- **`getCandidate`:** now includes `pendingCvVacancy` (id+title) and `cvVersions` (newest first) in its response.

**What actually changed, frontend:**
- **`CandidatesPage.tsx`'s bulk-upload flow:** `created` candidates still auto-apply to the vacancy immediately (nothing can conflict for a brand-new candidate). Duplicates of any kind no longer do -- the post-upload toast now says how many CVs matched existing candidates and points HR at their profiles to decide, rather than silently applying and only mentioning it in passing. The old `noopCount`/409-swallowing logic is gone from this function entirely, since a duplicate can no longer reach `applyCandidateToVacancy` from here -- that 409 handling now lives inside the resolve-pending-cv path instead.
- **`CandidateDetailPage.tsx`'s banner:** rebuilt from a three-button (View/Replace/Dismiss) banner into: a message distinguishing identical vs different content, "View CV on file" and "View new CV" buttons (opened as two separate tabs -- this is HR looking at both PDFs side by side, not an automated text diff; PDFs aren't practically diffable and a real diff view is out of scope), an "Also add this candidate to `<vacancy>`" checkbox (defaulted checked), and two resolution buttons ("Use new CV" / "Keep current CV") that both honor the checkbox. A result notice reports whether the apply actually happened or the candidate was already on that vacancy. Resolving with an application added triggers a full `getCandidateDetail` refetch so Applicant History picks up the new row; resolving without one just patches local state.
- **New read-only "CV History" section**, shown whenever a candidate has more than one CV version on file -- newest first, each row naming which vacancy it was uploaded for (if known) and when.

**Verification:** none possible -- sandbox still wedged, identical Windows-update error on every retry this entire session. Read every touched file in full before and after editing (`schema.prisma`, `candidate.controller.ts`, `candidate.routes.ts`, `application.controller.ts`, `api/candidates.ts`, `CandidatesPage.tsx`, `CandidatesPage.css`, `CandidateDetailPage.tsx`) and cross-checked relation names/field shapes by hand rather than assuming (e.g. `ResolvePendingCvResult` deliberately typed narrower than full `CandidateSummary`, matching the actual raw-Prisma-row shape `replaceCandidateCv`/`dismissPendingCv` return, not the joined shape `getCandidate` returns). User needs to run a real migration (e.g. `npx prisma migrate dev --name cv_history_and_pending_vacancy`) before any of this works, then run `npx tsc --noEmit` on both `backend` and `frontend` before trusting it further -- this is a genuinely large change (new table, three rewritten endpoints, two rewritten frontend flows) and I have had zero execution access to check it myself. Manual verification checklist once migrated: (1) uploading a CV under an existing email -- identical or different -- always produces the banner now, never a silent auto-link; (2) "View CV on file" and "View new CV" both open the correct PDF; (3) "Use new CV" with the checkbox on both swaps the CV and adds the application, with History showing two rows afterward; (4) "Keep current CV" with the checkbox on adds the application without touching the CV; (5) unchecking the box on either button does the CV action only, no application; (6) re-resolving against a vacancy the candidate's already on shows "Already applied... no new application was added" rather than erroring; (7) the CV History section only appears once a candidate has 2+ versions, newest first.

## Sixty-fifth pass: real Prisma-client-stale bug found via live testing; duplicate resolution moved into the upload modal itself, on direct user correction

**Real bug found: the generated Prisma Client was stale, not just the database.** User ran the Sixty-fourth pass's migration exactly as instructed (`npx prisma migrate dev --name cv_history_and_pending_vacancy`) and it applied cleanly ("Your database is now in sync with your schema"), but `confirmCvUpload` still crashed with `PrismaClientValidationError: Unknown argument pendingCvFileId` -- and critically, `pendingCvFileId` didn't even appear in the error's own list of *invalid-but-known* fields, meaning the loaded client had no idea these columns had ever existed, on either round of this feature. Root cause: `backend/generated/prisma` (this project's custom `generator client { output = "../generated/prisma" }` path) wasn't being regenerated, almost certainly because the running `tsx watch` dev server held a lock on the client's native query-engine binary on Windows, silently preventing `migrate dev`'s auto-generate step from actually overwriting it. Confirmed this diagnosis by having the user stop the dev server fully and run `npx prisma generate` on its own -- once isolated from a running process, this is the standard, well-documented Windows Prisma failure mode. Worth remembering for any future schema change on this project: `migrate dev`'s "in sync with your schema" success message is about the database only, not proof the client was regenerated -- always stop the dev server first.

**Real UX correction, direct from user testing:** after the fix above, `confirmCvUpload` worked, but its result was a toast reading "1 CV matched an existing candidate. Review it on their profile before adding them to this vacancy." User pushed back immediately and correctly: *"imagine that candidate at the very bottom of the list HR will have to look for it thats not user friendly, make it then and there and ask them then and there."* This is right -- routing every single duplicate match through "go find this person in a list that could have hundreds of rows" is worse UX than the interactive modal this whole feature line started by removing, and there was no good reason not to resolve it immediately when HR is already looking straight at the upload flow. Also asked directly whether an identical-vs-different CV indicator was "too advanced" -- it isn't; that hash comparison (`pendingCvIsIdentical`) already existed from the Sixty-fourth pass, it just hadn't been surfaced in this part of the flow yet.

**Fix: a new "duplicates" step added to the same upload modal**, reusing the Sixty-fourth pass's backend endpoints unchanged (`replaceCandidateCv`/`dismissPendingCv` with `applyToVacancy`) rather than building anything new server-side. `CandidatesPage.tsx`'s `handleConfirmAndApply` now checks `result.duplicates` immediately after confirming: if any exist, the modal moves to a new `"duplicates"` step instead of closing (created candidates still auto-apply and get their own success toast first, same as before). Each duplicate becomes a `DuplicateReviewRow` shown inline: identical-vs-different messaging (from `pendingCvIsIdentical`), "View CV on file" / "View new CV" buttons opening both PDFs in separate tabs (same "compare by opening two tabs, not an automated diff" approach as the profile banner), an "Also add this candidate to `<vacancy title>`" checkbox defaulted checked, and "Use new CV" / "Keep current CV" buttons that resolve that one row in place -- on success the row collapses to a short result line ("New CV saved. Added to this vacancy." or similar) instead of disappearing, so HR can see what happened to each one without losing the list. A "Done" button closes the modal once finished; if any rows are left unresolved (HR just closes without deciding), a toast names how many and says to check their profiles -- the **candidate-profile banner from the Sixty-fourth pass is untouched and still exactly the fallback for that case**, not replaced by this modal step.

**Verification:** none possible directly (still no execution access), but this round is on noticeably firmer ground than the last: the user's own terminal output confirmed the schema/migration/client chain is now genuinely working end-to-end (the `PrismaClientValidationError` is gone after regenerating with the dev server stopped), which is the first real confirmation any of the Sixty-fourth pass's backend work is actually sound. This pass only adds a new frontend step reusing already-verified-reachable endpoints, so the risk surface is smaller. User should verify: (1) uploading a CV that matches an existing candidate now shows the decision UI immediately in the same modal, not just a toast; (2) the identical/different wording matches the actual file relationship; (3) "Use new CV"/"Keep current CV" with the checkbox checked both add the application and update `Successfully uploaded`/history correctly; (4) closing the modal with an unresolved row still leaves the pending banner intact on that candidate's profile page.

**Immediate visual follow-up, same pass:** user saw the new "duplicates" step rendered (the yellow `.cnd-pending-cv-banner` styling, reused from the candidate-profile banner) and correctly called it out as "plain" and out of theme with the rest of the app's white-card/pill-button modal language. Restyled with new dedicated CSS (`.cnd-duplicate-card` and friends in `CandidatesPage.css`) matching `.cnd-review-row`'s white/bordered card look and `.cnd-save-btn`/`.cnd-cancel-btn`'s black-pill/outline-pill button pattern, plus a `.cnd-status-green`/`.cnd-status-blue` badge (reusing the existing status-pill styling used elsewhere on this page) for the identical/different indicator instead of an inline sentence. Copy also reworked per direct feedback: the per-card intro is now "This matches an existing candidate" (small label) above the name/email, "View CV on file" (called out as unprofessional wording) became "View Current CV" alongside a matching "View New CV", and a new "How would you like to proceed?" heading now sits directly above the centered Use New CV/Keep Current CV buttons. The candidate-profile banner's own styling (`CandidateDetailPage.tsx`) was deliberately left untouched -- this restyle is scoped to the upload-modal step only, since that's what was shown and critiqued.

**Second immediate follow-up, same pass:** user asked for a further trim -- remove the step's intro paragraph, remove the identical/different badge entirely, remove the "also add to vacancy" checkbox and its label, and swap the "Done" button for an X close icon matching the rest of the app's modals. All done: `DuplicateReviewRow` dropped `applyChecked` from its type (the checkbox is gone, so `replaceCandidateCv`/`dismissPendingCv` now always pass `applyToVacancy: true` -- correct default since this candidate was uploaded specifically for `uploadVacancyId`, nothing left to opt out of), the badge span and its wrapper flex row were removed, and a new `.cnd-modal-close` class (copied from `VacanciesPage.css`'s `.vac-modal-close` -- same circular X, `position: absolute; top: 14px; right: 14px`, matching every other modal in the app) replaces the bottom-right Done button, wired to the same `handleFinishDuplicateReview` handler so closing this way still refreshes the list and still warns about any rows left unresolved. Caught and fixed one CSS regression before it shipped: `.cnd-duplicate-card-header` was still `display: flex; justify-content: space-between` from when it held the name/email block on one side and the badge on the other -- with the badge gone, that flex row would have laid the name and email side-by-side on one line instead of stacked, so it's now a plain block.

**Third immediate follow-up, same pass:** user flagged two more things after seeing the trimmed version -- the bordered box around each duplicate card felt like unnecessary chrome between the "Review Matched Candidates" heading and the actual content, and with four buttons per card (View Current CV, View New CV, Use New CV, Keep Current CV) it wasn't obvious which to click first. Fixed both. `.cnd-duplicate-card` dropped its `background`/`border`/`border-radius` entirely so content sits directly in the modal body; when a batch has more than one duplicate, `.cnd-duplicate-card + .cnd-duplicate-card` adds a `border-top` (reusing `--altrium-line`, same as every other hairline divider in this app) so multiple entries stay visually distinct without each needing its own box. For the button confusion, established an explicit hierarchy instead of four equal-weight controls: "View Current CV"/"View New CV" became `.cnd-duplicate-view-link` -- small (12.5px) text links in `--altrium-gold-dark` with an inline middle-dot separator, styled after the page's existing `.cnd-link-btn` convention -- signaling they're optional, look-first actions, not the required next step. "Use New CV"/"Keep Current CV" (`.cnd-duplicate-btn-primary`/`-btn-secondary`) were bumped up to `padding: 11px 26px; font-size: 14.5px; font-weight: 600` (both now match in weight/size, differentiated only by fill vs. outline) so the two real decisions read as the obviously larger, more prominent pair. The now-unused `.cnd-duplicate-view-btn` pill-button rule was deleted rather than repurposed, since the JSX had already moved to `.cnd-duplicate-view-link`.

**Fourth immediate follow-up, same pass, tried and reverted:** attempted collapsing the label + name + email into one flowing sentence ("This matches an existing candidate: **Freddie Sandhu** &middot; freddie.sandhu@example.com") with the name bold black and email bold gold. User immediately rejected it ("no nvm thats bad"), so this was reverted back to the stacked `.cnd-duplicate-card-label`/`-header`/`-name`/`-email` layout from the third follow-up, unchanged. Noting the attempt so it isn't retried the same way.

**Fifth immediate follow-up, same pass:** the resolved-row result text ("New CV saved. Already applied to this vacancy, so no new application was added.") was rendering inline inside the card, replacing the buttons -- user pointed at that screenshot and asked for it as "a popup msg in the bottom" instead, matching the bottom-center `Toast` component already used elsewhere on this page (`uploadToast`, `decisionToast`). Rebuilt around that: `DuplicateReviewRow` dropped `resolved`/`resultNote` from its type entirely, `handleResolveDuplicate` now calls a new `setDuplicateResolveToast(\`${row.existingName}: ${note}\`)` and removes that row from `duplicateReviews` outright (rather than flagging it resolved and keeping it rendered) so HR can move straight to the next match instead of reading a result sentence where the buttons used to be. If resolving the last row in a batch empties the array, the modal now closes and refreshes immediately rather than sitting open and empty -- done inline in `handleResolveDuplicate` with the already-computed `remaining` array rather than by calling `handleFinishDuplicateReview()`, since that function reads `duplicateReviews` from its own render's closure and wouldn't yet reflect a `setDuplicateReviews` call made moments earlier in the same synchronous block (a real stale-closure bug caught before it shipped, not just a style choice). `handleFinishDuplicateReview`'s own unresolved-count logic simplified to `duplicateReviews.length`, since every row still in the array is unresolved by construction now. Removed the now-dead `.cnd-duplicate-result-note` CSS rule.

## Sixty-sixth pass: Reconsider notice relocated + reworded, duplicate-resolve toast simplified without losing the applied/already-applied distinction, CV History "not showing" explained

**Reconsider notice moved to the top of the page and reworded.** On `CandidateDetailPage.tsx`, the "Rejected before being shortlisted. There's no way to reapply for this vacancy, but you can reconsider them instead." notice + Reconsider button used to sit at the very bottom of the page, past CV, Review Notes, Applicant History, and Interviews -- user pointed out this is exactly the kind of thing HR might come back to reconsider a while later (e.g. hiring for the same role again after some time), so it shouldn't require scrolling past everything else to notice. Moved the whole block up to right after the ON_HOLD notice, near the top, and gave it its own alert styling (`.cnd-reconsider-banner`, copying `.cnd-pending-cv-banner`'s look one section above it -- both are "this candidate needs a decision" notices on the same page, so they now read as the same kind of thing) instead of the plain `cnd-detail-decision-row-space` text-plus-button row it used at the bottom. Text reworded from the blunt "There's no way to reapply for this vacancy" to a plain question: "This candidate was rejected before being shortlisted for this vacancy. Would you like to reconsider them?"

**CV History visibility explained, not a bug.** User asked why CV History wasn't showing after uploading "the same one twice." Confirmed in code: `candidate.controller.ts` only writes a new `CandidateCvVersion` row on new-candidate creation and inside `replaceCandidateCv` (the "Use New CV" path) -- choosing "Keep Current CV" (`dismissPendingCv`) never adds one, correctly, since the CV on file didn't change. `CandidateDetailPage.tsx` only renders the History section when `cvVersions.length > 1`. So uploading the same CV twice and choosing Keep Current CV both times leaves exactly one version on record and the section stays hidden by design -- there's nothing to show a history *of* yet. This is working as built, not a defect; no code changed here.

**Duplicate-resolve toast text simplified, but not all the way to "always say applied."** User quoted the toast ("Freddie Sandhu: New CV saved. Already applied to this vacancy, so no new application was added.") and asked for something as simple as "New CV saved, applied to this vacancy." First pass did exactly that -- dropped the name and collapsed both outcomes to one blanket "applied to this vacancy" line -- but caught a real problem before shipping it: `result.alreadyApplied` covers more than "HR uploaded the same person twice in one session." Per `applicationController`'s unique-per-vacancy constraint (surfaced in this same conversation via the "would HR reconsider someone they rejected years ago" question), a candidate who was previously REJECTED for this exact vacancy already has an application row on file for it, so resolving a duplicate CV match against them also hits the alreadyApplied branch -- nothing about their REJECTED status changes. Calling that "applied to this vacancy" in the toast would actively misreport what happened. Kept the distinction but shortened both branches: `"New CV saved."`/`"Kept the current CV."` followed by `" Applied to this vacancy."` when a new application was actually created, or `" Already on this vacancy."` when one already existed (dropped the candidate-name prefix per the user's own example, which didn't include one).

**Design question resolved: auto-reconsider.** Presented the user three options (leave decoupled, add an explicit "reconsider them?" confirmation prompt, or auto-reconsider with no extra prompt) -- user picked auto-reconsider: routing a new CV to this exact vacancy through the upload flow is itself a deliberate enough signal, so asking again would just be redundant friction.

**Implemented in `candidate.controller.ts`'s `applyToVacancyIfRequested`.** On a P2002 collision (an application already exists for this candidateId+vacancyId), the helper now looks up that existing application; if it's `REJECTED` with `hiringDecision === null` (the exact same eligibility rule `updateApplicationStatus`'s explicit Reconsider path already uses) and the vacancy isn't `ON_HOLD`, it calls `transitionApplicationStage(existing.id, { stage: "SHORTLISTED" }, userId)` -- the same stage-transition helper the explicit Reconsider button uses -- and returns a new `reconsidered: true` flag alongside `applied`/`alreadyApplied`. `ResolvePendingCvResult` (frontend `api/candidates.ts`) picked up the new field, since both `replaceCandidateCv` and `dismissPendingCv` spread `applyResult` straight into their JSON response.

Both places that read this result were updated to report it accurately rather than folding it into "already applied": `CandidatesPage.tsx`'s duplicate-resolve toast now says "...They were previously rejected here and have been reconsidered." instead of "Already on this vacancy" when `reconsidered` is true, and `CandidateDetailPage.tsx`'s pending-CV banner notice says "Previously rejected for `<vacancy>`. Reconsidered and moved back to Shortlisted." Also patches `row.stage` to `"SHORTLISTED"` locally (and refetches `candidateDetail` for Applicant History) when the just-reconsidered application happens to be the one currently open on that page -- otherwise the top Reconsider banner and stage badge would keep showing stale REJECTED state until a manual reload.

Caught mid-edit and fixed: the first draft of the `CandidateDetailPage.tsx` notice used a "--" (double-hyphen) in user-facing text, which this project explicitly swept out sitewide a few passes ago (Sixty-fourth pass). Replaced with a period before it shipped.

## Sixty-seventh pass: reconsideration lands on Unreviewed not Shortlisted (real bug, caught by live testing), Review Notes History, clickable CV History, and a file-deletion bug found while building it

**Real bug: auto-reconsider skipped the review-note gate.** User live-tested the Sixty-sixth pass's auto-reconsider feature and reported it directly: "when i clicked add new cv there were automatically shortlisted no they should unreviewed because HR has to write a Review Notes to click either shortlist or reject." Correct -- `updateApplicationStatus`'s review-note gate (Forty-eighth-pass-adjacent rule: `Candidate.lastCvReviewNote` must be non-empty before SHORTLISTED/REJECTED) only fires when `existing.stage === "APPLIED"`. By moving a reconsidered application straight to SHORTLISTED, both the automatic path (`applyToVacancyIfRequested`) and the pre-existing manual Reconsider button (`updateApplicationStatus`'s `isReconsider` branch, built back in the Thirty-ninth pass) let HR shortlist or reject a reconsidered candidate on a stale review note from years earlier, or with no note at all, without ever hitting the gate.

Fixed both to target `APPLIED` instead of `SHORTLISTED`, and to clear `Candidate.lastCvReviewNote`/`lastCvReviewedByUserId`/`lastCvReviewedAt` at the same time so the old note can't silently satisfy the gate on the next Shortlist/Reject attempt -- a fresh review note is now genuinely required. Asked the user whether the manual Reconsider button (identical bug, same root cause, just older code) should get the same fix for consistency; user confirmed yes. Frontend followed: `ApplicationStatusTarget`/`VALID_STATUS_TARGETS` widened to include `"APPLIED"`, `handleReconsider()` now calls `updateApplicationStatus(row.id, "APPLIED")`, and both reconsideration notices ("This candidate... reconsidered and moved back to...") now say Unreviewed instead of Shortlisted, matching the Twenty-sixth-pass status-label rename (APPLIED displays as "Unreviewed" on the list, "Applied" on the detail page).

**New: Review Notes History.** User asked to "show the review notes history by whom and when... from the previous years ago when they applied" -- `Candidate.lastCvReviewNote` is a single field HR overwrites every time, so once a candidate is reconsidered years later and gets a new note, the old one (and who wrote it, and when) is gone for good. Added an append-only `CandidateReviewNote` model (`id`, `candidateId`, `note`, `authorUserId`, `createdAt`), mirroring the existing `CandidateCvVersion` append-only pattern. `updateCandidate` now writes a new row here whenever a non-empty `reviewNote` is saved, alongside its existing (unchanged) write to `lastCvReviewNote` -- the history is purely additive and doesn't touch the gate-check field. `getCandidate` includes `reviewNotes` (with author name, newest first). Frontend shows a "Review Notes History" section on `CandidateDetailPage.tsx` underneath CV History, reusing the same `.cnd-review-list`/`.cnd-review-row` card styling already used for CV History, each entry showing the note text plus "`<author>` &middot; `<date>`". `handleSaveReviewNote()` now refetches the full candidate record after saving (instead of a local state patch) so a just-written history entry appears immediately without a reload.

**New: clickable CV History.** User asked for CV History entries to be openable, not just a list of upload dates. Added a `getCvVersion` controller + `GET /candidates/:id/cv-versions/:versionId` route, scoped by candidate id (unlike the pre-existing `getPendingCv`, which serves any filename with no ownership check) so one candidate's history can't be used to fetch another's file by guessing an id. Frontend: `fetchCvVersionBlobUrl` (same blob-URL pattern as `fetchCvBlobUrl`) and each CV History row is now a real `<button>` calling `handleViewCvVersion(version.id)`, opening the file in a new tab with its own busy/error state (`cvVersionViewingId`/`cvVersionViewError`) separate from the current-CV viewer's state. Deliberately does not call `markCandidateReviewed` -- only viewing the *current* CV counts toward the review-note gate; opening an old superseded version shouldn't. Added `.cnd-cv-version-row` (button-chrome reset, laid out like `.cnd-summary-row`) and `.cnd-review-note-history-text` to `CandidatesPage.css`.

**Real bug found while building the above, not by the user: `replaceCandidateCv` was deleting old CV files from disk.** While wiring up clickable history, found that every "Use New CV" resolution called `deleteFile(oldCvUrl)` on the file being superseded -- but `CandidateCvVersion` has been documented since it was introduced (Sixty-second-pass-adjacent) as append-only, "never deleted or edited." In practice this meant every "Earlier CV" row, once built, would 404 the instant HR clicked it, because the actual file was already gone from disk. The version *row* survived; the file it pointed to didn't. Removed the file-deletion entirely -- old CVs are now kept on disk indefinitely so History stays genuinely viewable, with a comment at the call site flagging the disk-usage tradeoff for anyone extending this later (a real system would want a retention policy or archival storage; out of scope here).

**Not yet migrated.** The new `CandidateReviewNote` model requires `npx prisma migrate dev` (and, per the Windows stale-Prisma-Client issue diagnosed earlier this project, the dev server must be fully stopped first, then `npx prisma generate` run standalone and confirmed with "Generated Prisma Client" before restarting).

**Not verified this pass.** Bash/shell access was unavailable for this entire pass (a Windows-update-related environment issue, unrelated to the app itself) -- no `tsc --noEmit` could be run on either side. All of the above was reviewed by hand, but the user should run `tsc --noEmit` on both `backend` and `frontend` before relying on it, and should manually exercise: auto-reconsider landing on Unreviewed with an empty review note required before Shortlist/Reject, the manual Reconsider button doing the same, Review Notes History appearing after saving a note, and clicking an "Earlier CV" row actually opening that file.

## Sixty-eighth pass: reset-on-new-CV rule extended to Shortlisted (interview-count gated, not time-based), history sections collapsed behind a toggle

**Live-tested and reported: uploading a new CV for an already-Shortlisted candidate left them Shortlisted.** Correct, and not actually a bug in the Sixty-seventh pass's fix -- `applyToVacancyIfRequested`'s reset only ever fired for an existing application in `REJECTED`, so a candidate who was already `SHORTLISTED` (not rejected) when the new CV came in was, by design, left untouched. Confirmed this against the actual code and explained it back to the user rather than guessing at a different cause.

**Design question: should Shortlisted get the same reset REJECTED already gets?** Presented three options (always reset, reset only if no interview scheduled yet, or leave Shortlisted alone). The user talked through the real tension out loud: a candidate rejected or shortlisted years ago should clearly reset on a fresh CV, but the exact same rule could just as easily clobber a decision HR made five minutes ago by accident, and there's no clean time cutoff between "years later" and "5 minutes ago" -- correctly pointing out this same risk already exists, unaddressed, in the REJECTED case that shipped two passes ago. User explicitly declined to pick a threshold ("i dont want to decide here") and left the call to be made here.

**Resolved with a structural signal instead of a time-based one.** Rather than guess at a cutoff, `isEligibleForCvReset()` (`candidate.controller.ts`) now resets a `SHORTLISTED` application only when it has **zero `Interview` rows** on record (`prisma.interview.count({ where: { applicationId } })`). No interview scheduled yet means nothing is actually in flight to disrupt -- this is exactly as "early-stage" as the REJECTED-with-no-`hiringDecision` case already handled (both, by construction, only ever happen before interviewing starts: a CV-stage Reject happens at APPLIED, before any round exists, and a HIRED/final-REJECT decision always sets `hiringDecision`, which is excluded up front). Once even one `Interview` row exists for that application, this is left alone -- the new CV still saves as the candidate's current one (`replaceCandidateCv` always does that, independent of this check), but the application's stage and review note are untouched, so a candidate genuinely mid-interview-pipeline can't get silently bumped back to Unreviewed by a new CV upload. `HIRED` was never at risk either way, since `hiringDecision` being non-null is checked first and excludes it outright.

**Toast/banner copy generalized.** `CandidatesPage.tsx`'s duplicate-resolve toast and `CandidateDetailPage.tsx`'s pending-CV banner notice both used to say "previously rejected" for `result.reconsidered`, which is no longer accurate now that a reset can also happen from Shortlisted. Reworded both to "already in review here" / "already in review for `<vacancy>`", reset to Unreviewed" -- stays true for either originating stage.

**CV History and Review Notes History collapsed behind a toggle.** User picked this over leaving both always-expanded, given this same test candidate already has 5 CV versions from repeated live testing and an always-open list would only get longer. Both sections' `<label>` became a clickable `.cnd-history-toggle` reading "View CV History (5)" / "View Review Notes History (3)" (count included so HR knows there's something there without opening it); clicking toggles a `cvHistoryOpen`/`reviewNotesHistoryOpen` boolean that shows or hides the existing list in place. No change to what's inside each list.

**Not verified this pass.** Bash was still unavailable (same standing Windows-update issue) -- no `tsc --noEmit` run. User should verify compilation, then manually test: a Shortlisted candidate with no interview scheduled resets to Unreviewed on a new CV; a Shortlisted candidate who already has an interview scheduled does NOT reset (CV still updates, stage/note stay put); and both history sections start collapsed and expand/collapse correctly.

## Sixty-ninth pass: Create Vacancy error wording, duplicate-CV gate scoped to same vacancy (real design gap, live-tested), history-toggle reorder/relabel, toast trimmed further

**Create Vacancy validation message named a field that isn't on the form.** `VacanciesPage.tsx`'s `handleSave` still said "Title, department, and description are required" even though Department was removed from this form entirely back in an earlier pass (task #129) -- it's pre-filled from whichever department page the modal was opened from, never something HR types here. Reworded to "Title and description are required." -- the two fields actually visible. `form.department` itself still gets validated (still auto-filled, so this can't realistically trip), just no longer named in the message.

**Real gap found via live testing: uploading the same person's CV to a genuinely different vacancy triggered the duplicate-review gate.** User uploaded Freddie Sandhu's CV to a second vacancy (Finance Intern) and got the "Review Matched Candidates" step -- and rightly pushed back: that gate exists to resolve a real file conflict (should this replace or be discarded in favor of what's on file), and there's no conflict here at all -- this is a different role, so of course the new CV should just be used. `confirmCvUpload` (`candidate.controller.ts`) was gating on email match alone, regardless of vacancy, ever since the SCRUM2-30 "gate ALL duplicate-email uploads" rebuild. User confirmed the intended check is "same email **and** same vacancy," not email alone.

Added a `matchesSameVacancy` check: an existing-email match only becomes a gated duplicate (pendingCvFileId set, "Review Matched Candidates" step shown, CV History implicated) when that candidate already has an application for the specific vacancy this upload targets. When the email matches but the vacancy is new to them, this now reuses the existing Candidate row (still one person, one record -- email stays unique) but proceeds exactly like a brand-new upload: CV saved as current, a new `CandidateCvVersion` logged, no HR decision, no duplicate-review UI at all. The resulting `CandidateApplication` is created by the frontend's existing per-`created`-entry `applyCandidateToVacancy` call, not inside `confirmCvUpload` itself, since `matchesSameVacancy` being false already guarantees that call won't conflict. With no target vacancy on the upload at all, this falls back to the old always-gate behavior (no vacancy to disambiguate against).

**History sections reordered and re-labeled.** Per direct user feedback: "View Review Notes History" now sits after the editable Review Notes box (Last Reviewed By / textarea / Save Note) instead of before it -- reads as "here's the current one, then what came before it," matching reading order. Both this and "View CV History" also dropped their "(N)" counts -- plain text labels only, per explicit request.

**Duplicate-resolve toast trimmed further.** The reconsidered-case message built two passes ago ("New CV saved. They were already in review here and have been reset to Unreviewed. Write a new review note...") was reported back as too much for a toast -- that context is already visible on the candidate's own page (stage badge, unlocked Review Notes box) the moment HR gets there. Reconsidered now just shows the same short base line as any other resolution ("New CV saved."/"Kept the current CV."), nothing appended.

**Not verified this pass.** Bash was still unavailable (same standing Windows-update issue) -- no `tsc --noEmit` run. User should verify compilation, then manually test: uploading an existing candidate's CV to a vacancy they've never applied to goes straight through with no duplicate-review step, while re-uploading against a vacancy they already have an application for still gates as before.

**Follow-up, same pass: CV History and Review Notes History moved inside their parent cards.** Screenshot showed "View CV History" rendering as its own separate bordered box below the CV card, rather than reading as part of it. Both toggles (and their expandable lists) now live inside the CV card and the Review Notes card respectively -- at the bottom of each, under a thin `.cnd-history-block` top rule instead of a second full box -- so each card reads as "the current thing, and the history of it" in one place, not two separate cards for what's conceptually one topic. No change to the toggle behavior itself, purely a JSX/CSS move.

## Seventieth pass: visual redesign of the Hiring Manager, Management, and Leadership dashboards

**Ask: "recreate the dashboards ... keep the basic idea and all the instructions i gave u previously but make it neat, eye-catching and easy to identify data according to our theme and colour palatte ... nice and legite and corporate like and proffesional."** All three dashboards (Hiring Manager's `DashboardPage`, Management's `DashboardPage`, Leadership's `RecruitmentOverviewPage`) share the exact same underlying structure -- title, filter bar, 4-tile KPI grid, a Recruitment Progress section of anchor stages + interview rounds -- just scoped differently (HM: their own assigned vacancies; Management: their department; Leadership: org-wide). None of that structure, the filters, or the underlying data changed. Only the visual layer did, applied identically across all three so every role's dashboard now reads as the same product rather than three separately-styled pages.

**No new colors invented.** Every accent color used is one already established elsewhere in the app: the exact status-pill palette from `CandidatesPage.css` (`.cnd-status-green` #1e7a34, `.cnd-status-red` #b91c1c, `.cnd-status-yellow`'s #a9660b family represented here by `--altrium-gold`, and `.cnd-status-blue` #1d4ed8), plus the existing `--altrium-gold` / `--altrium-black` / `--altrium-ink` / `--altrium-line` theme variables from `index.css`. "According to our theme and colour palette" was taken literally -- reuse, not redesign the palette itself.

**KPI tiles.** Went from flat beige boxes with a plain border to white cards with a soft shadow, rounded corners, and a 4px colored accent bar along the top (`.hmd-kpi-tile--blue/gold/green/red` and the `mgd-`/`ro-` equivalents) -- Open Vacancies is blue (informational), the "needs attention" KPI (Awaiting My Decision / Active Candidates) is gold (matches gold's existing meaning as the app's primary/action color), Hired-type KPIs are green, Rejected is red. Gives each number a distinct identity at a glance instead of four identical tiles differing only in their label.

**Recruitment Progress rebuilt as a comparative bar list instead of a grid of same-size boxes.** The old layout gave a stage with 1 candidate and a stage with 40 the exact same visual weight -- reading the actual shape of the pipeline meant reading every number one at a time. Anchors (Shortlisted/Hired/Rejected) and interview rounds are now combined into one list of horizontal bars, each bar's length proportional to whichever stage/round currently has the most candidates (computed client-side per page, `Math.max` over the combined list). Shortlisted is green, Hired is gold, Rejected is red, and every interview round shares one neutral blue (rounds are per-vacancy-configurable free text, not a fixed small set like the three anchors, so they don't each get their own color). This whole section now sits inside one card (`.hmd-panel`/`.mgd-panel`/`.ro-panel`) rather than as loose tiles directly on the page background.

**Page header and filter bar.** Titles got a subtitle line explaining the page's scope, and the old full-width grey divider was replaced with a short 56px gold accent bar -- a small, deliberate brand touch at the top of every page rather than a generic rule any admin tool would have. The filter bar became a proper card (white surface, border, soft shadow) instead of an unstyled row of controls floating on the page, and its selects/Apply button got refined spacing, uppercase field labels, and a gold focus ring on the selects to match the rest of the app's interactive states.

Grepped the rest of the frontend for the old, now-removed class names (`hmd-progress-tile`, `hmd-divider`, `hmd-progress-col`, and their `mgd-`/`ro-` equivalents) and confirmed nothing else in the codebase still referenced them.
