## Seventy-first pass: seed-viva-demo.ts extended -- CV History and Review Notes History never had any real data to show

**Ask: "make test data but professional ones not dummy or test anything like we did for the viva ... so that i can check all dashabords and see all vales."** `seed-viva-demo.ts` (built and iterated on since the Forty-sixth pass) is already exactly this -- 30+ named candidates with real career histories, real generated PDF CVs, spread across all 8 canonical departments -- so the ask was to get a clean run of it, not build something new.

**Real gap found while checking it was actually ready to demonstrate everything: CV History and Review Notes History (both added in later passes, well after this script was first written) had nothing to read.** `ensureCandidate()` writes `Candidate.cvUrl` and `Candidate.lastCvReviewNote` directly via Prisma, bypassing the real upload/review-save endpoints entirely -- which also means it never wrote a `CandidateCvVersion` or `CandidateReviewNote` row, since those only get created by `candidate.controller.ts`'s actual upload/`updateCandidate` code paths, not by a raw `prisma.candidate.create()`. Every seeded candidate would show a real CV and a real note on their page, but "View CV History" and "View Review Notes History" would never appear for anyone (`cvVersions.length > 1` and `reviewNotes.length > 0` would both be false), even though the note/CV visibly exists elsewhere on the same page -- confusing to demo, and this dataset's whole purpose is to have every feature show something real.

**Fixed at the shared `ensureCandidate()` helper, not per-candidate:** it now also writes the first `CandidateCvVersion` and (when a review is given) the first `CandidateReviewNote` row for every candidate it creates -- one-line-per-model addition, applies automatically to all 30+ candidates already defined in this script with no per-candidate changes needed. Added an optional `daysAgo` to the `reviewed` spec so this first entry can be backdated (both History lists sort newest-first, so a candidate who later gets a second CV/review needs the first one genuinely earlier, not "now" for both just because they happen in the same script run).

**New `ensureUpdatedCvAndReview()` helper + 4 calls (Tomas Reyes/IT, Camille Dupont/HR, Priya Chandrasekaran/Marketing, Julian Ostrowski/Finance):** each gets a second, different CV (a small realistic update -- a new certification, a new project, a new skill) and a second review note dated 2 days after their first, so CV History and Review Notes History have genuine multi-entry, multi-timestamp data to show, spread across four different departments rather than piled onto one. Idempotent like the rest of this script (checks `candidateCvVersion.count` before doing anything, so a re-run is a no-op for candidates that already have their second version).

**Run order (same as every previous reset -- this project's standing rule is that `prisma migrate reset` is safe here since only seed/test data exists):**
```
cd backend
npx prisma migrate reset
npx prisma db seed
npx tsx scripts/seed-viva-demo.ts
```
A full reset is the right call here specifically -- the database currently holds this session's live-testing debris (Freddie Sandhu re-uploaded 7+ times while testing the reconsider/duplicate-CV features), none of which belongs in a clean demo dataset, and `migrate reset` also guarantees the DB schema is fully current (`CandidateReviewNote` and every other model added this session included) rather than relying on whatever state `migrate dev` was left in earlier.

**Not verified this pass.** Bash was still unavailable (same standing Windows-update issue) -- could not execute the script, so the `ensureUpdatedCvAndReview` logic and the four new CV profiles have been reviewed by hand against the existing, already-proven `ensureCandidate` pattern but not run. User should watch the console output on this run for anything unexpected, and specifically check Tomas/Camille/Priya/Julian's candidate pages for both History sections showing two entries each.

## Seventy-seventh pass: "missing 4th KPI" clarified as never-existed, Top Candidate Comments cap bug fixed, Comments panel sizing fixed, Compliance Officer seeded to a real Top 5

**User asked "wasnt there a 4th KPI here in the candidate comparison one," worried the previous pass's restyle had dropped data it shouldn't have touched** (they'd been explicit: "dont change data make the ui/ux like that"). Checked `hiringManager.ts`'s `Comparison` type: `summary` has exactly three fields -- `topCandidateCount`, `averageScore`, `highestScore` -- and always has, going back through this page's history in the log. There is no 4th metric anywhere in this endpoint's data and none was removed; the confusion was almost certainly the reference point itself, since the Hiring Manager Dashboard (the template this page's card style was just copied from) has a 4-tile grid (Open Vacancies / Awaiting My Decision / Hired / Rejected). Left at 3 tiles -- adding a 4th here would mean inventing a new metric, which the user's own "dont change data" instruction rules out. Flagging this explicitly rather than silently leaving it, since it's the kind of thing that should get a clear answer, not a guess.

**Investigated the Top Candidate Comments box looking "too long" with only one candidate**, per the user's screenshot showing a single short comment card stretching the full height of the two stacked panels beside it. Root cause: `.cc-comments-panel` had a hardcoded `height: 100%` forcing it to match its row's height regardless of content, even though the grid it sits in (`.cc-panels`) already has `align-items: start` (meant to let panels size to their own content). Removed the forced height -- the panel now sizes to whatever comments it actually holds.

**Investigated "add test so top 5 candidates can be seen and their comments" and found a real bug, not just a data gap.** `hiringManager.controller.ts`'s `getComparison` builds `topCandidates` from the full `TOP_N` (5) but built `comments` from `top.slice(0, 3)` -- hardcoded to 3 regardless of `TOP_N`, left over from before task #189 bumped the ranking list itself from 3 to 5. So even on a vacancy with 5 scored candidates, the Top Candidate Score Ranking panel would show all 5 but Top Candidate Comments would silently drop the 4th and 5th. Fixed to `top.slice(0, TOP_N)` so every ranked candidate gets a comments entry.

**Compliance Officer - Legal (the vacancy in the screenshot) only had one scored candidate (Anouk Delacroix, score 8)** -- Silas Radcliffe had only just applied, no interview yet -- which is why the KPIs read "1" and the Numeric Score Distribution had a single filled bucket. Added four more filler candidates via the existing `ensureFillerCandidate` helper, each with a realistic UK compliance/legal background and a distinct score chosen specifically to land in every remaining distribution bucket: Isla Bramwell (10, "the clear standout candidate"), Harriet Voss (9), Dominic Ashworth (7), Callum Whitfield (6, lands in "Below 7" -- junior profile, honest weaker comment to match). Combined with Anouk's 8, this vacancy now has one candidate in every one of the five score buckets and a genuine Top 5 with five distinct comments, once reseeded.

**Not verified this pass.** Bash still unavailable -- no `tsc --noEmit` run on the controller change, and the seed additions were hand-checked against `ensureFillerCandidate`'s existing usages (unique emails/phone numbers confirmed via search, matching call shape) but not actually run. User needs to run `npx prisma migrate reset && npx prisma db seed && npx tsx scripts/seed-viva-demo.ts` from `backend/` to see the new Compliance Officer data, then open Candidate Comparison for that vacancy and confirm all 5 candidates, all 5 comments, and a fully-populated distribution chart render correctly, and that the Comments panel no longer looks stretched on vacancies with only 1-2 candidates.

## Seventy-ninth pass: KPI tiles narrowed, evenly spaced per page instead of one fixed width

**User asked to narrow the KPI tiles (width only, not height), explicitly ruling out one fixed size sitewide** ("that cant be a fixed size across the whole system so depending on each page customize the width") **and requiring the tiles stay evenly spaced apart** once narrower. The tiles had been full-bleed `display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(N, 1fr)` -- each tile stretched to fill its column, so shrinking a tile's own width property would have done nothing without also changing how the row distributes space.

**Switched every KPI grid from `grid` to `flex; flex-wrap: wrap; justify-content: space-evenly`, and gave each tile a fixed `width` instead of stretching to `1fr`.** `space-evenly` puts equal gaps before the first tile, between every tile, and after the last one -- so narrowing the tiles doesn't just leave a lopsided gap on one side, the whole row re-balances. Widths are set per grid, not one system-wide value: 220px for every 4-tile grid (`hmd-`, `mgd-`, `ro-`, `ht-`, `dp-kpi-tile`) and 290px for every 3-tile grid (`cc-`, `sys-kpi-tile`) -- fewer tiles get a bit more width each so a 3-KPI page doesn't look sparser than a 4-KPI one at the same tile size. Added `box-sizing: border-box` alongside the new `width` so padding/border stay inside that width rather than inflating it.

**Removed the now-obsolete `@media (max-width: 900px) { .xxx-kpi-grid { grid-template-columns: repeat(2, 1fr); } }` overrides on the three dashboards.** That rule doesn't apply to a flex container and would have been dead CSS; `flex-wrap: wrap` already reflows the fixed-width tiles onto a second row on narrow screens without needing a breakpoint rule.

**Not verified this pass.** Bash still unavailable -- no `tsc --noEmit` run, and none of the seven pages' new tile widths/spacing or narrow-screen wrap behavior (previously handled by the removed 900px rule) were visually confirmed. User should run `npx tsc --noEmit` in `frontend`, then check both that the tiles look evenly spaced at normal width and that they still wrap sensibly on a narrow window on all three dashboards.

## Eightieth pass: Overdue Roles KPI sub-label filled in, Department Performance given real hired/fill-rate data

**User asked why Department Performance's "Overdue Roles" tile has a blank area under the number where the other three tiles ("Best Fill Rate," "Fastest Hiring," "Most Open Roles") each show a department name, and suggested filling it with something like "all departments or vacancies."** Checked `leadership.controller.ts`'s `buildDepartmentPerformanceData`: the other three summary KPIs each pick one department as a "winner" (`{ department, value }`), but `overdueRoles` is `rows.reduce((sum, r) => sum + r.overdueRoles, 0)` -- a plain sum across every department, with no single department to attribute it to. So the blank tile wasn't a rendering bug, it genuinely had nothing to put there under the old per-department-label assumption. Filled it with the literal string "All departments" in `DepartmentPerformancePage.tsx`, since the user's own suggested fix was correct.

**User also flagged that every department showed a 0% Fill Rate, most showed "--" for Avg Time to Hire, and the Hired by Department / Rejected by Department bars and the By Department table were almost entirely empty -- correctly calling this unrealistic** for a system that's supposed to be mid-way through active recruiting, with the explicit instruction that any new data added must read as genuine/professional and must never contain the literal words "test" or "dummy." Root-caused it in `seed-viva-demo.ts`: `ensureVacancy()` always hardcodes `status: "OPEN"` with no way to close a vacancy, and no code path anywhere in the script ever transitioned a vacancy to `CLOSED` -- not even Marketing's Content Marketing Specialist role, which already has a real `HIRED` candidate (Baptiste Laurent) from an earlier pass. Since `fillRate` is computed purely from `vacancy.status === "CLOSED"` counts, this was the single cause of the universal 0%.

**Fixed Marketing first, since it was the pre-existing case:** added `prisma.vacancy.update(..., { status: "CLOSED" })` for Baptiste's vacancy right after his existing HIRE block, reflecting an outcome that was already true in the data but never applied to the vacancy record.

**Then gave five more departments a real, earned fill instead of inventing new candidates from scratch.** Five of the eight departments' secondary (single-round) vacancies already had exactly one `SHORTLISTED`-with-scored-feedback filler candidate sitting undecided forever: Jonas Kessler (QA Engineer, IT), Sana Iqbal (Support Specialist, Customer Service), Rosalind Pike (Talent Acquisition Coordinator, HR), Elliot Nakashima (Accounts Payable Specialist, Finance and Accounting), and Reuben Frost (Logistics Coordinator, Operations). Each of these candidates' `currentVacancyStageId` was already the vacancy's only (and therefore last) configured round, satisfying the same hire-decision guard `application.controller.ts` enforces for a real hire -- the same reasoning already established for Baptiste earlier in this file. Converted each to a real `HIRED` outcome (`hiringDecision`, `decidedByUserId`, staggered `decidedAt` dates 1-3 days ago so Avg Time to Hire varies realistically per department instead of landing on one repeated number), sent the matching hire email via `sendOnceAndLog`, wrote an `HM_DECISION_COMMENT` audit-log entry with a short, real-sounding reason each, then closed each of the five vacancies. Sales and Legal were deliberately left at 0% -- both already have an established "still actively recruiting, nothing decided yet" story from earlier passes (Legal's Compliance Officer just got a full 5-candidate shortlist seeded two passes ago; Legal's Corporate Counsel and Sales' Account Executive both have a genuinely undecided shortlisted candidate as their current arc), so leaving them unfilled is more realistic than forcing every department to 100% at once.

**Also gave "Rejected by Department" real variety instead of just the pre-existing Marketing/Sales pair.** Each of the five now-filled vacancies already had a second, still-`APPLIED` (never interviewed) candidate sitting in the data: Bethany Coleman (IT), Dexter Holt (Customer Service), Gideon Marsh (HR), Paloma Serrano (Finance and Accounting), Vera Lindholm (Operations). Since their vacancies are now filled, a courtesy rejection at the CV stage is the realistic outcome for an applicant who was never even interviewed once the role closed -- converted each to `REJECTED` using the same minimal pattern as the pre-existing Freya Lindqvist CV-stage rejection (no interview, no `hiringDecision` fields, just a real HR review note explaining the role was filled), rather than the post-interview `hiringDecision: REJECT` pattern used for Dimitri. Every review note describes genuine, professional reasoning and does not contain the words "test" or "dummy" anywhere, per the user's explicit instruction.

**Net effect once reseeded:** Department Performance shows six departments (Marketing, IT, Customer Service, HR, Finance and Accounting, Operations) at a real 50% fill rate (1 of 2 vacancies filled) with varied Avg Time to Hire figures, Sales and Legal still at a deliberate, explained 0%, non-zero bars on both "Hired by Department" and "Rejected by Department" for all six filled departments, and a populated By Department table across the board.

User should run `npx tsc --noEmit` in both `backend` and `frontend`, then from `backend/`: `npx prisma migrate reset && npx prisma db seed && npx tsx scripts/seed-viva-demo.ts`, and confirm Department Performance's KPI tiles, bar charts, and table all render the new figures as expected.

## Eighty-first pass: IT Admin's System Monitoring KPIs reverted; Hiring Trends chart rebuilt to fix a real distortion bug

**User clarified that "dashboards/KPIs" in their earlier sitewide restyle requests never meant IT Admin's System Monitoring page** -- only the actual Hiring Manager/Management/Leadership dashboards (and, by extension, Candidate Comparison/Hiring Trends/Department Performance, which the user had separately confirmed by screenshot). The Seventy-sixth pass had swept `admin/SystemPage.css`'s `.sys-kpi-tile` into the same white-card/accent-bar conversion on a literal reading of "wherever KPIs are used," which this pass reverses. `.sys-kpi-tile` is back to the documented pre-redesign look (Seventieth pass: "flat beige boxes with a plain border") -- flat `#faf9f6` background, `1px solid var(--altrium-line)` border, `10px` radius, no shadow, no `::before` accent bar -- and the `sys-kpi-tile--blue/gold/green/red` modifier classes were removed from both the CSS and `SystemPage.tsx`'s JSX since there's no longer an accent bar for them to color. Grid layout, tile width (290px), and spacing were left untouched since the user's complaint was specifically about the KPI look, not the page's layout. Exact original byte-for-byte CSS isn't recoverable (project has no git history to diff against), so this is a faithful reconstruction from the decision log's own description of the prior state, not a verbatim revert.

**User also flagged Hiring Trends' "Hires per Month" chart as looking "very 3d and bad and basic."** Root cause: the SVG used a fixed `720x220` viewBox with `preserveAspectRatio="none"`, while the CSS rendered it at `width: 100%; height: 240px` -- any time the panel's actual rendered width doesn't match a 720:220 (3.27:1) ratio, which in practice is always, `none` stretches the x and y axes by different factors to fill the box. That non-uniform stretch flattens the "circular" dots into ellipses and skews the line -- the exact warped, pseudo-3D look the user was describing. This was a real rendering bug, not just a style preference.

**Rebuilt the chart as a proper responsive area/line chart rather than patching the aspect-ratio flag.** Added a `ResizeObserver` on a wrapping div (`chartWrapRef`) so the SVG's `viewBox` width always equals the panel's real rendered width in pixels -- the coordinate system is always 1:1 with the screen, so nothing needs non-uniform scaling regardless of window size. On top of that fix, made the chart itself more polished to match the rest of the app: the point-to-point line is now smoothed with Catmull-Rom-to-Bezier interpolation (`buildSmoothPath`) instead of sharp straight segments; a soft gold gradient area fill sits under the line (`ht-chart-area-stop-start/end`, faithful to the app's existing `--altrium-gold-dark` accent); three light horizontal gridlines were added for readability; and each data point is now a small white-centered ring with a solid gold center dot instead of one flat filled circle, which stayed perfectly round now that the distortion bug is fixed.

**Not verified this pass.** Bash still unavailable -- no `tsc --noEmit` run, and the reverted System Monitoring tiles and rebuilt Hiring Trends chart weren't visually confirmed in a browser (in particular, whether `ResizeObserver` behaves correctly across the panel's real container width, and whether ~5+ months of trend data still reads cleanly at the new dot/label spacing). User should run `npx tsc --noEmit` in `frontend`, then open System Monitoring to confirm the KPI tiles read as plain/flat again, and Hiring Trends to confirm the chart's dots are circular (not ellipses) at the actual window width and the curve/gradient/gridlines look right.

CSS-only change -- the modifier class names stay in the JSX on every page (harmless, since they still exist as selectors, just no longer produce different colors), avoiding a 7-file TSX sweep for a purely cosmetic outcome.

**Recruitment Progress bars, all 3 dashboards** (`hmd-`, `mgd-`, `ro-progress-row-fill--*`): repointed the same way -- `--blue` (interview rounds) to `--altrium-gold-pale`, `--green` (Shortlisted) to `--altrium-gold-dark`, `--gold` (Hired) stays `--altrium-gold` (already on-theme), `--red` (Rejected) to `--altrium-gold-deep`. The `ProgressRow` type's `accent: "green" | "gold" | "red" | "blue"` and the components that build these rows were left untouched -- only the CSS class definitions changed color, so no risk of touching the underlying data logic.

**Department Performance's `.dp-bar-fill-rejected`**, the one other bar chart on the "still red" list, changed from `#b91c1c` at `0.75` opacity to `var(--altrium-gold-deep)` at full opacity -- matching the same deepest ramp step used for Rejected elsewhere, no longer softened since it's not meant to read as an alert color anymore.

## Eighty-seventh pass: Hiring Trends chart width cap removed; real ResizeObserver bug found and fixed

**User asked to widen the "Hires per Month" box back out** (drew arrows pointing outward from the 700px cap set two passes ago), explicitly noting it should be "the whole graph['s] width... not just the outer box." Removed `.ht-chart-panel`'s `max-width`/`margin: 0 auto` entirely, back to filling the container.

**User's screenshot after that change showed exactly the problem they'd pre-empted: the white box widened, but the plotted line/dots stayed narrow and left-aligned inside it**, with a large empty gap on both sides. Investigated rather than assuming a CSS fix would cover it, since the chart's width is supposed to be measured live off its container via `ResizeObserver` (added in the Eighty-first pass specifically so the chart always matches its box) -- if that were working, widening the box should have widened the line automatically with no separate fix needed.

**Found a real bug in that ResizeObserver setup, not a CSS issue.** It was attached inside a plain `useEffect(() => { const el = chartWrapRef.current; if (!el) return; ... }, [])`. That effect runs exactly once, on mount -- but `.ht-chart-wrap` only renders once `data` has loaded (it's behind a `trend.length === 0` check), and `data` loads asynchronously. So on first mount, the ref was still `null`, the effect's early return fired immediately, and no observer was ever attached, silently. `chartWidth` stayed stuck at its `720`px initial default for the component's entire lifetime -- explaining precisely what the screenshot showed: a chart rendered at a fixed ~720px width inside a box that was otherwise free to grow.

**Fixed by converting `chartWrapRef` from a plain `useRef` to a callback ref.** A callback ref fires every time React actually attaches or detaches the DOM node, regardless of when that happens relative to the initial render -- so it correctly attaches a fresh `ResizeObserver` the moment `.ht-chart-wrap` first mounts (after the data load resolves), and would also handle it correctly if the element unmounts/remounts later (e.g. toggling between the empty state and a populated chart via the filters). The previous `ResizeObserver` instance is disconnected on every callback invocation before attaching a new one, avoiding a leak if the div is ever swapped.

**Not verified this pass.** Bash still unavailable -- no `tsc --noEmit` run, and the fix's actual effect (the line now spanning the full widened box) wasn't visually confirmed, only reasoned through from the callback-ref mechanics. User should refresh Hiring Trends and confirm the chart now fills the box edge-to-edge (matching the box's padding, not stopping partway across it).

## Eighty-eighth pass: SCRUM2-30's duplicate-CV/auto-reconsider machinery reverted back to a plain email-match notice; CV History and Review Notes History removed

**User asked to remove "the whole when a vacancy is closed and reopened if someone applies again a rejected or later rejected person, the pop up that will ask if they wanna review the new cv"**, plus CV History and Review Notes History on the candidate profile -- keeping only "the candidate duplicate detection through email and the previous pop up." This is a genuine, large rollback: the duplicate-CV/reconsider feature line had gone through roughly ten passes (Sixty-first through Seventy-first) since it was last this simple, ending up with a persisted `pendingCvFileId` decision flow (Use New CV / Keep Current CV, both able to silently auto-reconsider a REJECTED or early-SHORTLISTED application back to Unreviewed), a full `CandidateCvVersion` history table, and a `CandidateReviewNote` history table. Given the ambiguity in exactly which past version "the previous pop up" meant, asked the user to confirm three specific choices before touching anything: (1) which pop-up survives -- picked the original Fortieth-pass plain "Existing candidate(s) found" informational modal (message + "View their profile" link, no CV-swap decision); (2) whether re-uploading a CV for an already-REJECTED/SHORTLISTED candidate on the same vacancy should still silently auto-reconsider them -- picked no, it should just block as a normal "already applied" duplicate, like before any of this existed; (3) whether to also drop the `CandidateCvVersion`/`CandidateReviewNote` tables from the schema -- picked no, leave the tables/migrations in place, just stop reading/writing them from the live app's UI and API.

**Backend (`candidate.controller.ts`):** removed `isEligibleForCvReset`/`applyToVacancyIfRequested` (the auto-reconsider machinery), `getPendingCv`, `replaceCandidateCv`, `dismissPendingCv`, and `getCvVersion` entirely -- all four existed only to serve the decision flow and CV History viewer that no longer exist. `confirmCvUpload` rewritten: an email match on the exact vacancy this upload targets is now a plain blocked duplicate (nothing about the existing application changes, the new file is simply left unused in storage) reported back as a `matched` entry with the existing application's id to link to; an email match on a genuinely different vacancy (or no target vacancy) still silently reuses the Candidate row and updates the CV, exactly as before, and is also reported as a `matched` entry (no application id yet -- the frontend fills that in once its own apply-loop creates it). `getCandidate` no longer includes `pendingCvVacancy`/`cvVersions`/`reviewNotes`. `updateCandidate` no longer writes a `CandidateReviewNote` row on save (still writes `lastCvReviewNote` itself, unchanged). The `CandidateCvVersion`/`CandidateReviewNote` Prisma models and their columns on `Candidate` are untouched in `schema.prisma` -- no migration needed, they're just no longer read from or written to by the live app (the demo-data script `seed-viva-demo.ts` still populates them for its own purposes; left alone since it's a one-off tool, not the live app).

**Routes (`candidate.routes.ts`):** removed the now-dead `GET /candidates/pending-cv/:fileId`, `POST /candidates/:id/replace-cv`, `POST /candidates/:id/dismiss-pending-cv`, and `GET /candidates/:id/cv-versions/:versionId` routes.

**Frontend (`api/candidates.ts`):** dropped `pendingCvFileId`/`pendingCvIsIdentical`/`pendingCvVacancy` from `CandidateSummary`, `cvVersions`/`reviewNotes` from `CandidateDetail`, and the now-dead `CandidateCvVersionEntry`/`CandidateReviewNoteEntry` types, `fetchCvVersionBlobUrl`, `fetchPendingCvBlobUrl`, `ResolvePendingCvResult`, `replaceCandidateCv`, `dismissPendingCv`. `ConfirmCvResult` reshaped from `{duplicates: [...identical...]}` to `{matched: [...alreadyOnThisVacancy, applicationId...]}` matching the simplified backend response.

**Frontend (`CandidatesPage.tsx`):** the upload modal's whole "duplicates" step (`DuplicateReviewRow`, `handleViewDuplicateCv`, `handleResolveDuplicate`, `handleFinishDuplicateReview`, the View Current/New CV links and Use New/Keep Current CV buttons) is gone. In its place: `handleConfirmAndApply` now applies every `created` entry (brand-new candidates and cross-vacancy reuses alike) to the vacancy via the existing per-entry `applyCandidateToVacancy` loop, tracking each new application's id by candidateId; any `matched` entries become a separate `DuplicateMatch[]` shown in their own centered modal (`duplicateMatches` state) once the upload modal itself closes -- heading "Existing candidate(s) found", one card per match with its message and a "View their profile" link (using the existing application id for a same-vacancy match, or the just-created application id for a cross-vacancy match), and a Close button. This is a straight rebuild of the Fortieth/Forty-first-pass design, just wired to the current codebase.

**Frontend (`CandidateDetailPage.tsx`):** removed the persistent pending-CV banner section entirely (all of `pendingCvViewingWhich`/`pendingCvActionBusy`/`pendingCvError`/`pendingCvNotice`/`pendingCvApplyChecked` state, `handleViewCurrentOrPendingCv`, `handleResolvePendingCv`), the CV History block inside the CV card (`cvHistoryOpen`, `handleViewCvVersion`, `cvVersionViewingId`/`cvVersionViewError`), and the Review Notes History block inside the Review Notes card (`reviewNotesHistoryOpen`). The manual "Reconsider" banner/button (`handleReconsider`, `.cnd-reconsider-banner`) was left untouched -- it's a distinct, older feature (explicit button, no CV upload involved) that the user's ask didn't cover, and it already correctly targets Unreviewed (Sixty-seventh-pass fix), not Shortlisted.

**CSS (`CandidatesPage.css`):** removed `.cnd-cv-version-row`, `.cnd-review-note-history-text`, `.cnd-history-toggle`, `.cnd-history-block`, `.cnd-pending-cv-*`, and every `.cnd-duplicate-card`/`.cnd-duplicate-view-*`/`.cnd-duplicate-btn-*` rule from the old decision-flow step. Added back `.cnd-duplicate-modal-icon`/`.cnd-duplicate-list`/`.cnd-duplicate-row`/`.cnd-duplicate-link` for the plain notice modal (reusing the already-existing `.cnd-modal-backdrop`/`.cnd-modal`/`.cnd-modal-actions`/`.cnd-cancel-btn` shared modal chrome). `.cnd-reconsider-banner` kept as-is.

**Not verified this pass.** Bash was unavailable for the entire pass (same standing Windows-update sandbox issue) -- no `tsc --noEmit` run on either side. Reviewed every touched file in full, before and after editing, and grepped the rest of the codebase (frontend, backend, seed scripts, Vitest specs, Selenium suites) for every removed symbol/class name to confirm nothing else still references them -- only the untouched `schema.prisma`/migrations and `seed-viva-demo.ts` (a demo-data tool, deliberately left alone) still mention `pendingCv*`/`CandidateCvVersion`/`CandidateReviewNote`, and neither breaks compilation since the Prisma models themselves weren't removed. User should run `npx tsc --noEmit` on both `backend` and `frontend`, then manually verify: (1) uploading a CV for someone already applied to the selected vacancy shows the plain "already applied" notice with a working profile link, and does NOT change their stage or CV; (2) uploading a CV that matches an existing candidate on a genuinely different vacancy still goes through silently and shows the informational match afterward; (3) the candidate profile page no longer shows a pending-CV banner, CV History, or Review Notes History anywhere; (4) the manual Reconsider banner/button still works exactly as before.
